### A-265 — a cycle count on an oversold batch did nothing the first time, and the ledger double-counted the correction
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-25, client and server, TDD. Stages 2 and 3 of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-oversell-compensating-movement-design.md` (Stage 1 shipped 2026-10-14), on the owner's go-ahead.
- **Was:** an oversell (6 sold from a batch whose log held 4) left the log at −2, and the single floor over the sum carried that deficit forward. The next inbound paid it off before adding anything, so a count of 2 left the batch at 0, a second count of 2 reached 2, and the ledger recorded +4 of found stock for 2 real units. The Stage-0 server forgave the deficit per push, so the two replicas disagreed by the deficit after every count (A-217). Live case: batch `868a8d17`, `gluformin`, store `571582a9`.
- **Now (Stage 2, client):** `writeSaleItemStock()` writes an inbound `adjustment` of `+N` against an oversold batch, after the sale row, for the units it did not hold (`reference_type = "oversell"`, `reference_id` the sale). The log never sums below zero, the first count sticks, and a Stage-0 server lands on the same figure when both rows share a push. The Stock Adjustments ledger lists it as "Oversell"; it is never a stock loss, and counts as value added. Rules: `client/AGENTS.md`, "An oversell writes two rows".
- **Now (Stage 3, server):** `php artisan stock:compensate-oversells` finds every batch whose log sums below zero (excluding A-148 legacy batches) and writes the same `+N` row, dry-run by default, labelled per run, revertible with counter-rows, refused while Health Sync is on. It asserts no stored number and leaves `stock_batches.quantity` alone. **It has not been run**; production needs the owner's word (root `AGENTS.md` §12). Rules: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Oversell compensation backfill".
- **Review changed the first version.** The client compensated even on a device behind on its movement log, where a count another till had already written made the "oversell" phantom stock on every replica (proven with a throwaway test); it now compensates only when `movementLogIsComplete()` holds and leaves other deficits to the backfill. The backfill took a batch's store from nowhere for a store-less product, writing a row no device pulls; it now skips and reports those, and scopes by the product's store. It could compensate a sale whose own compensation had not been pushed yet, booking the units twice; a batch with a movement that reached the server (`_synced_at`, not the till's own `created_at`, which a push keeps) more recently than `--settle-days` (default 7) is now left alone; the second review caught the first version reading `created_at`, which an old backlog would have slipped past. It included inactive batches, unlike the client rule it claims to share. The dashboard's recent-activity feed showed each compensation as a manual adjustment by the cashier; it is now left out like the sale. The docs claimed a legacy carve-out batch could not be oversold; the fallback path can, and the docs now say so.
- **Deviations from the spec:** the revert writes counter-rows, because `StockMovement` has no `SoftDeletes` and a deleted row would never reach a device; the compensation stays in the ledger grouping as an "Oversell" entry, since that grouping is the ledger; the stock-value card has no tooltip to annotate.
- **Not done:** a sale from a bundle older than this, or from a device behind on its log, still leaves a deficit, which the command's next run picks up. The completeness gate cannot see an inbound another till wrote since this device's last completed pull, so a phantom of N is still possible, for as long as a device stays offline; the next count corrects it. Product history labels the row a plain adjustment (its reason names the oversell). A decayed A-148 batch is backfilled under the oversell label (A-264). A push that splits a sale from its compensation leaves the Stage-0 server N above the client until the derived-quantity Stage 2 (A-217). A mis-keyed oversell books N units that are not there, which the next count removes with a visible negative.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** no schema or contract change: an existing `movement_type`, a new `reference_type` string in a free-text column. An old bundle pulls the row like any adjustment; one that floors per movement reads N until its daily fold corrects it from the log (spec, "Backward compatibility").
- **Coverage:** `client/__tests__/oversell-compensation.test.ts`, 11 cases (the live case through a count; none on a device whose log is incomplete; no compensation within stock; only the remainder on the oversold batch across two batches; a product with no batch; a legacy batch untouched; no deficit left; not a stock loss and no loss-label prefix; an "Oversell" ledger entry; agreement with a per-push-floored server through a count; kept out of the dashboard's activity feed), seven RED against `dev`. `record-sale-item-stock-depleted-batch.test.ts`'s sale-row queries are narrowed to `movement_type = 'sale'`. `laravel-server/tests/Feature/CompensateOversellsTest.php`, 12 cases (dry run; write to zero with the stored quantity untouched; a second run finds nothing; a legacy batch excluded; a recently moved batch left until `--settle-days` allows it; a backlog row dated long ago but arrived recently still left alone; a store-less product skipped; an inactive batch excluded; `--store`; revert once; a used label refused; refused under Health Sync).
- **Full suite:** backend 1,625 passed, 5 skipped; client 3,181 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### A-333 — a purchase receipt's stock movement showed one hour early
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-24, client only, TDD. Found by the local browser smoke test (not a regression: the writer predates the base commit).
- **Was:** `receivePurchaseOrder()` and `createAndReceivePurchaseOrder()` stamped the movement, batch and order with `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 19).replace('T', ' ')`, a UTC wall clock with the zone stripped. The movements ledger does `new Date(...)` on it, which reads a zone-less string as local time, so a receipt at 21:17 in Lagos showed 20:17 while sales and adjustments (full ISO) showed the real time. Day grouping and any date-range comparison could put a late or early receipt on the wrong day.
- **Now:** the receipt writers (`procurement-receiving.ts` x2, `received_at` in `procurement.ts`) store `toISOString()`. Existing zone-less rows are read as UTC through `toUtcInstant()`, applied in `mapMovement`, `adjustment-derivations.ts` and the movement details dialog. The server and older clients are unaffected: the push still rewrites ISO strings to the zone-less form MySQL needs (`normalizeDatetimeFields`), so the wire format is unchanged.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** no contract change and no backfill; the reader accepts both forms. The shim can go once no device holds a zone-less `created_at` or `movement_date`; see `client/AGENTS.md`, "Stored instants are real ISO instants".
- **Not done:** `formatDateSafe` and other readers still parse a zone-less string as local; nothing else is known to write one. The adjustments ledger groups by the UTC day of the stored instant, which is a separate display question.
- **Coverage:** `client/__tests__/procurement-receipt-timestamps.test.ts` (both receipt paths write UTC instants; fails on the old writer) and `stock-movement-zoneless-date.test.ts` (`toUtcInstant`, and `mapMovement` reads a legacy receipt as its UTC instant).

### A-334 — the Customers directory "Last Visit" rendered a bare ISO date
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-24, client only, TDD. Found by the local browser smoke test.
- **Was:** `transformCustomer()` turned `last_visit` into `YYYY-MM-DD` (or `"-"`) in the view-model, and the directory row and detail panel printed it as is, against the `DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm` rule for an instant.
- **Now:** `Customer.lastVisit` carries the raw instant (empty when the customer has never visited), and the two display sites format it with `formatDateSafe(value, "dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm", "-")`. `updateCustomer` writes `last_visit: existing.lastVisit || null` back through `transformCustomer`, so the view-model must stay parseable: formatting inside `transformCustomer` would have made that second pass throw on `new Date("24/10/2026 21:17")`.
- **Sorting:** the directory sorts on the raw value, which is now an ISO instant, so the order is unchanged and correct.
- **Coverage:** `client/__tests__/customer-last-visit-format.test.tsx` (row, never-visited dash, detail panel); all three fail on the old code.

### A-196 (oversized files) and PG-13 (web files) — the client and web files past the 350-line limit were split
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-24, client and web, no behaviour change. Closes the file-size parts of both entries; what each leaves is A-196's missing CI lint step and PG-13's two PHP services (both still open in `KNOWN_BUGS.md`).
- **Was:** `pos-cart.tsx` (429), `base-helpers.ts` (687), `push.ts` (663) and `pull.ts` (625), and `web/` `broadcasts-tab.tsx`, `users/page.tsx`, `subscription-config-tab.tsx` and `users/new/page.tsx`, plus about twenty more client files, were over `.agents/AGENTS.md` §4's limit. ESLint's `max-lines` skips blank and comment lines, so the lint pass alone left many at 350 to 716 raw lines.
- **Now:** every changed client and web source file is below 350 raw lines. The sync-engine splits (`pull-*.ts`, `push-*.ts`, `sync-queue.ts`) and the query splits (`inventory-*.ts`, `report-*.ts`, `stock-transfer-*.ts`) were verified by diffing the original's non-blank lines against the new files: only imports, exports and the call sites of extracted helpers differ.
- **Not done:** `client/lib/db/core.ts`, `schema.ts`, `schema-migrations.ts`, `local-database.ts`, `constants/suggestions.ts` (a data table) and `components/ui/chart.tsx` (shadcn) stay over the limit by raw count. `SyncController.php` is about 810 lines after the 2026-10-24 service split; its `push()` loop (about 650 lines) stays in the controller (see `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Where the sync server code lives").
- **Review fixes in the same pass (Fable review of the lint cleanup):** the active store is persisted only when the stale-profile fallback runs, so a pinned staff user's logout no longer overwrites the saved `store_id`. The regression came from 9ea3bedd's effect on `[activeStoreId, hasFixedStore]`; 2f626dac predates it and never had the bug. `store-context-logout-keeps-persisted-store.test.tsx` logs a pinned user out with the owner's store profile already cached. It asserts the persist helper is never called and the saved id is unchanged, and it fails when 9ea3bedd's effect is put back. Its sibling test fails if the fallback correction stops persisting. The `!hasFixedStore` guard on the fallback is not covered: removing it leaves both tests green, because a pinned user's profile query only returns that user's own store. The impersonation banner builds its URL in the click handler by concatenation, not `new URL()` at import; the delete dialogs' `target` objects are memoized.

### A-328 — `funnel:remind` crashed on the first user at reminder level 1 or 2, so second and third setup reminders never went out
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-24, server only, TDD. Found by Larastan during the level-5 baseline cleanup.
- **Was:** `SendFunnelReminders` called `addHours()`/`addDays()` directly on `$user->setup_reminder_last_sent_at`, which `User` does not cast, so Eloquent returns the stored string and the call throws an `Error`. The `try` only wrapped the mail send and caught `\Exception`, so the daily scheduled run (`routes/console.php`) died on the first level-1 or level-2 user it reached: that user and every user after them in the result set got nothing, including first reminders. Even with a cast, `clone $x->addHours(72)` clones the result of the call, so it would have mutated the model's attribute first.
- **Now:** `isReminderDue()` reads the timestamp through `Carbon::parse()`, which takes a string or a Carbon and returns a fresh instance, so it is correct whether or not `User` ever gains the `datetime` cast. The 24h / 72h / 7d gaps are unchanged.
- **Burst cap:** users stuck at level 1 or 2 receive their overdue reminder on the next daily run, so the first run after deploy would have sent them all at once, synchronously. A run now sends at most 100 (`MAX_EMAILS_PER_RUN`); the rest keep their level and go out on the following daily runs. Nothing backdates or skips a reminder.
- **Coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SendFunnelRemindersCommandTest.php`, five cases (the cap, and level 1 due and not due, level 2 due and not due, a not-yet-due level-1 user no longer blocks a level-0 user). All four were RED against `dev` with `Call to a member function addHours() on string`.

### A-248 (part c) — a never-pushed legacy product was stamped with whichever store was active when it was first pushed
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-23, server and client, TDD. GitHub #177. Closes A-248 with parts (a) and (b); what it leaves is A-326 and A-327.
- **Was:** `_sync_queue` does not record the store a change was written under, so a legacy product with no local `store_id`, queued under store 1 and first pushed while store 2 was active, arrived with no store and `normalizePushPayload()` backfilled store 2. The proposed fix, a queue column carrying each change's store, was rejected on inspection: the rows at risk were queued before anything recorded their store, or are re-queued at push time, when the only store to record is the same guess.
- **Now: evidence instead of guessing, at ingest.** **Server:** a store-less product INSERT from a multi-store or super-admin session is not backfilled; `App\Services\Sync\StorelessProductAttributor` gives each store-less product a push touched the single store named by its stored batches plus the movement stores the client sent, once the whole push is written, and restamps its batches so incremental pulls deliver them. Contradictory evidence, evidence outside the session's scope, or none at all leaves it unattributed and logged; it never takes the active store. **Client:** `attributeStorelessProductsFromEvidence()` does the same from local stock before pushing, for products with a pending INSERT, so the INSERT usually carries its store. **Repair:** `sync:repair-product-store-stamps` also attributes store-less products from stored evidence and now restamps a moved product's batches, which closes an incremental-pull gap the original command had too. Rules: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` and `client/AGENTS.md`.
- **Review changed the first version.** It left a store-less product unattributed until someone ran the repair command, invisible on every other device while its movements still arrived; it is now attributed in the same push. The repair moved products without restamping their batches, so incremental pulls never delivered them. The client pass could fail a whole push, ran an unbounded query on every push, and ran after the fingerprints were built. An author resolving to nobody was reported as another tenant. A first ingest attempt attributed on the batch before the push's movements had been written, and fell back to the active store for products touched by an UPDATE; both were caught by tests and fixed before commit.
- **Second review (2026-10-24) changed:** the no-evidence fallback to the active store is gone, because it was the same guess this fix exists to stop; such a product now stays unattributed (A-327). Evidence is recorded per change and dropped when the change rolls back, so a refused batch no longer attributes its product. Evidence naming a store outside the session's scope is refused rather than written; a super admin's scope is every store. The stamp runs in a savepoint and passes the product's `category_id` through `CrossStoreReferenceGuard`, so a product moved onto store B no longer points at store A's category. The store-less products are loaded in one query rather than one per candidate. The repair command no longer counts a store-less product's movements as evidence, since the server backfills a movement's store from the session. The client pass writes in one transaction and only touches products with a pending INSERT, so a synced row is never changed locally without being pushed.
- **Third review (2026-10-24) found a cross-tenant claim:** ingest attribution checked only that the evidence store was in the session's scope, never the product's own tenant, so a stranger who knew another tenant's store-less product id could push one batch naming it with their own store and take the product. `attribute()` now leaves a product unattributed and logged when its `user_id` resolves (author if they own stores, else their store's owner, as the repair command's `tenantOf()`) to a tenant other than the evidence store's owner. An author resolving to nobody is not treated as a stranger, and a super admin is unchanged.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** no contract change. A shipped bundle that pushes a store-less product with its stock gets it attributed from that stock. From a multi-store session, one pushed without stock now stays `NULL` where it used to take the active store; the pushing device keeps it and nothing it reads changes, but other devices do not see it until it is attributed (A-327). From a single-store session it is backfilled as before. Six `SyncPushCrossStoreCategoryTest` fixtures that omitted `store_id` on a product INSERT were updated to send it, as a real client does.
- **Coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushStoreIdBackfillTest.php` (attributed at ingest from stock pushed with it; the stock overrules the active store; contradictory stock stays unattributed; a product with no stock is not guessed; stock arriving in a later push attributes it; an attributed product naming the other store's category is re-owned; a single-store session is still backfilled), `SyncPushStorelessProductTenantTest.php` (another tenant's store-less product is not claimed by stock naming it, RED before the third-review fix; the tenant's own is still attributed), `RepairProductStoreStampTest.php` (store-less products attributed, refused for another tenant, left alone when contradictory; one whose only evidence is movements is not a candidate; a moved product's batch reaches an incremental pull), `client/__tests__/legacy-product-attribution.test.ts` (attributed locally and in the payload; contradictory evidence and a single-store device left alone; a product with no pending INSERT left alone). The behaviour cases were RED against `dev`.
- **Full suite:** backend 1,607 passed, 1 skipped; client 3,159 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean. `client/__tests__/sync-engine.test.ts` scripts `query()` answers in order, so it mocks the attribution pass, which has its own tests.

### A-321 — a legacy `NULL`-store supplier merged on push left the other store's purchase orders, payments and batches on the wrong store's supplier
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-23, server only, TDD. GitHub #263.
- **Was:** the client's push-side `id_map` remap of a `NULL`-store row is deliberately unscoped (A-303), so on a two-store device a legacy supplier merged into store A's same-named one repointed store B's `purchase_orders`, `supplier_payments` and `stock_batches` at store A's supplier and requeued them. `CrossStoreReferenceGuard::GUARDED` covered only `products.category_id`, so the server stored them that way, and store B's devices, which never pull store A's suppliers, showed those rows with no supplier permanently.
- **Now:** the guard also covers `supplier_id` on those three tables, re-owning a sibling store's supplier onto the row's own store's live same-named one, or creating one with the **name, `is_active` and the store owner's `user_id` only**. New `App\Services\Sync\OwnedSupplierResolver`. Full rules: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Cross-store supplier references".
- **Review changed the guard.** A supplier is re-owned only between stores of the **same owner** (a cross-tenant supplier is left as pushed rather than copying another tenant's supplier name into this store; categories keep A-302's cross-tenant healing, which a second review restored after the first version had dropped it); it re-owns a **soft-deleted** sibling referent instead of skipping it; on UPDATE it checks the store the row is being written to; and it follows the per-change lifecycle `PushedRowRestamper` uses, so a rolled-back change reports no remap and leaves no memo entry pointing at a row the rollback removed, a rejected UPDATE creates nothing, and a created row is stamped at commit (A-286). Each of these was RED against the first version of this fix.
- **Second review (2026-10-23) changed:** a `stock_batches` row is judged by its **product's** store, as everywhere else on the server, not its own `store_id` (which is `NULL` on the legacy batches this fix is for, so they were skipped); supplier creation locks the store row, so two concurrent pushes cannot both create the same supplier; a created supplier is owned by the store's owner (`user_id`); the supplier lookup matches the name exactly, as the push collision handler does; and only a referent this request wrote is restamped, not every matched one.
- **Not done:** rows already stored with a cross-store `supplier_id` are healed only when something re-pushes them; no repair command was built because nothing has measured that any exist. The measuring query is in the same `AGENTS.md` section. `OwnedSupplierResolver` and `resolveOwnedCategoryId()` are deliberately not merged: they differ by design (suppliers never revive).
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** no contract changed. A pushed row whose supplier or category belongs to its own store, or to no store, is stored exactly as before; a sibling-store reference is rewritten and reported through the existing `cross_store_reference` record.
- **Coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushCrossStoreSupplierTest.php`, 20 cases. The first fix landed 14: six RED against the previous guard (purchase order INSERT and UPDATE, supplier payment, stock batch INSERT, creation with the name only, the soft-deleted same-named supplier), six RED against the first version of this fix (the review cases above), and two pinning that the store's own supplier and a `NULL`-store supplier stay untouched. The second review added four (a legacy NULL-store batch and a batch whose store disagrees with its product's are both re-owned by the product's store, a created supplier carries its owner, a matched supplier is not restamped), all RED against the previous commit. A third pass added two: a stock batch UPDATE naming a sibling store's supplier is re-owned (RED with the guard disabled), and a batch update that names no supplier, such as the quantity-only one every sale makes, does not look up its product for the guard (RED against the previous commit, which queried on every batch write). `SyncPushCrossStoreCategoryTest` has seven: the six A-302 cases plus the second review's cross-tenant category case.
- **Full suite:** 1,557 passed, 1 skipped.

### A-254 — a pull carrying an `X-Store-Id` the caller does not own returned nothing and the client stamped every window as synced
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-23, server and client. GitHub #181.
- **Was:** `resolvePullTenantScope()` turned an unowned header into an empty scope, every store-scoped table matched `whereIn('store_id', [])`, the pull succeeded with empty arrays, and the client completed every table's window for that store key. If access to that store was later regained, its rows older than the stamp were never pulled.
- **Now:** for a header outside the caller's scope, `pull()` serves only the tables whose scope never depends on the header (`stores`, `payment_accounts`), returns `refused_store_id`, and logs at most once per user per day. If the refused id is the owner's own soft-deleted store, its tombstone is added to `stores`. The client clears every other window it holds under that store key, which also heals a window a pre-fix pull already poisoned. Full rules: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "An `X-Store-Id` outside the caller's scope withholds every store-scoped table".
- **Deliberately not the entry's 403.** A stale header is what a deleted store, or a staff member moved between stores, leaves on a device, and the `stores` rows it still receives are how that device can notice. A 403 would have failed every round permanently on every shipped bundle.
- **Found by review and fixed in the same change:** the deleted store's tombstone was not being served at all (trashed stores are outside the owner's scope); a non-string `store_id` input made the first version 500; `payment_accounts` was withheld although its scope never used the header; the log line was unthrottled and interpolated a request value.
- **Second review (2026-10-23) changed:** the deleted-store tombstone is limited to the caller's own store (the first version served a staff member a deleted *sibling* store's row, reopening A-129 for that case); a non-string `store_id` is refused as `(invalid)` instead of being treated as absent, which had widened the pull to every owned store; the header-independent table list is now checked by a test that derives it from `applyPullTenantScope()`; the `users` lookup is skipped on a refusal; the log throttle is per user and can no longer fail a pull; and the touched A-129 comment block moved to `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`.
- **Not done:** the client neither surfaces a refusal nor re-resolves its active store (logged as A-323). A device poisoned before this fix that has already regained access cannot be detected: logged as A-322. The SyncController is far over the 350-line limit and this adds to it; extracting pull scoping into a service was left out of a bug fix.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** additive field; `pull.ts` back to its first version (2026-09-26) stamps only tables in `changes`, so older bundles also leave omitted tables alone.
- **Coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPullStaffCrossStoreIsolationTest.php`, 7 new cases (unknown store and sibling-store refusals, RED before; deleted-store tombstone and non-string input, RED against the first version of this fix; a staff account naming a deleted sibling store gets no row for it; the header-independent table list is derived from the scope itself; an owned header still pulls everything with the field present and null). `client/__tests__/pull-refused-store-stamps-only-served-tables.test.ts`, 3 cases, one RED without the window reset.
- **Full suite:** backend 1,539 passed, 5 skipped; client 3,153 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean (no client change in the second review).

### A-292 — the store purge's user-level cascade did nothing on production, because it replayed foreign keys production has never had
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-23, server only, TDD. GitHub #180.
- **Was:** after the `store_id` sweep and `OWNER_SCOPED_TABLES`, everything else a purge promised to remove for the force-deleted owner and staff (`notifications`, `permission_user`, `coupon_usages`, actor `activity_logs`, `NULL`-store business rows, the `account_manager_id`/`referred_by_id`/`registered_by_id` pointers on other users, and a legacy cashier sale's `sale_items`) was delegated to `ForeignKeyCascadeEmulator`, which builds its map from `Schema::getForeignKeys()`. Production returns zero, so none of it ran. The suite could not see it: under `RefreshDatabase` the purge's FK suspension is ignored and SQLite itself cascades.
- **Now:** new `App\Services\Admin\PurgedUserRowSweeper` introspects every table with a `user_id`. Tables without `store_id`: the users' rows go. Tables with one: only `NULL`-store rows, and only when the purge takes the whole tenant; a swept parent's children go with it. It nulls every `SET NULL` pointer on surviving rows and stamps them (`_version`, `updated_at`, `_synced_at`) so devices pull the change. Rules: `docs/ADMIN_STORE_LIFECYCLE.md`, "Rows keyed to a purged user".
- **A second defect, found while fixing it:** on any schema that *does* declare foreign keys (the test suite, a local MySQL built from migrations), the emulator replayed `products.user_id → users ON DELETE CASCADE` and its siblings, so purging store A deleted **sibling store B's** products, sales and customers authored by a staff member who had once worked at B. The emulator is now told to skip every store-scoped table. Production was never affected (no FKs), which is the only reason this did not destroy data.
- **Review changed the first version:** it bumped `_version` without `updated_at`, so the cleared pointer never reached devices and their next edit would be refused as a conflict; it deleted the `NULL`-store rows of a staff member even when the owner kept another store those rows may belong to; and it left the children of swept `NULL`-store prescriptions and returns orphaned. A test now checks `POINTER_COLUMNS` against every `SET NULL` reference the schema declares. The service's remaining explanatory comments moved to `docs/ADMIN_STORE_LIFECYCLE.md`, and one shared `App\Support\SchemaTables::names()` replaces three copies of the table-listing loop.
- **Second review (2026-10-23) changed:** a deleted legacy sale's `returns` and `return_items` were orphaned on production (confirmed by the reviewer with a probe), because the children map stopped at `sale_items`; `PurgedUserRowSweeper::CHILDREN` is now one recursive map covering them, a test checks it against every `CASCADE` the schema declares for those parents, and the emulator's legacy-sale cascade now skips store-scoped tables too, so both schema shapes agree. `vendors` is kept on a partial purge. `SchemaTables::unqualified()` replaces the remaining inline copy, and each table's columns are read once per sweep. The deliberate exception is documented: a legacy *sale* of the owner's is still deleted when the owner keeps another store, because the revenue query attributes it to the purged store.
- **Not done:** a whole-tenant purge's `NULL`-store rows in tables without a `user_id` (A-324). Rows earlier production purges already left behind are not swept retroactively; the 2026-10-19 measurement found 50, all belonging to a deleted trial account. Columns declared `NO ACTION` (`sales.cashier_id`, `purchase_orders.ordered_by`, `stock_movements.performed_by`, `backups.created_by`) are still left dangling, and a purged staff member's rows carrying a surviving store's id are kept on purpose. A staff member's `NULL`-store rows kept on a partial purge are permanent inert residue, not something `sync:backfill-store-ids` can place later: it attributes through `users.user_id`, and that `users` row is gone (same class as A-324). `sale_items.prescription_id` (`nullOnDelete` to `prescriptions`) is left dangling when `NULL`-store prescriptions are swept; harmless, since the client treats a missing prescription as absent.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** no contract changed. A purge removes the rows its documentation always said it did; cleared pointers arrive on devices as ordinary updates.
- **Coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/Admin/AdminStorePurgeUserRowsTest.php`, 10 cases (12 runs) including both FK shapes for the sibling-store and legacy-return cases. The class turns SQLite FK enforcement off before the test transaction opens, so it models production rather than the migrations, and restores it on RefreshDatabase's stored PDO afterwards; restoring it through `DB::statement()` (the first version) left enforcement OFF for every later test class, shown by a scratch class run after it failing `PRAGMA foreign_keys = 1`.
- **Full suite:** 1,548 passed, 1 skipped.

### A-287 — a mis-stamped product's batch never moved in the cloud again, because the movement was accepted and only its delta refused
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-23, server only, TDD. GitHub #183.
- **Was:** A-280 refused a stock delta against a batch whose product belongs to a store outside the session's scope, but accepted the movement row itself, because `authorizeInsertTarget('stock_movements')` resolved ownership from the movement's own (session-backfilled) `store_id`. The refusal carried `'id' => null`, which `push.ts` skips, so the device was told nothing and the batch's cloud quantity froze while the device kept deriving its own from the local log.
- **Now:** a movement whose `stock_batch_id` resolves, through its product, to a store outside the session's scope is refused whole, `forbidden`, with its own queue id, on INSERT and on an UPDATE that repoints it (`namesForeignBatch()`). The device parks it visibly (A-165); for a mis-stamp it succeeds once `sync:repair-product-store-stamps` has run. The batch's store is one memoized join per request. The delta check stays as defence in depth. Rationale: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Stock batch deltas are store-scoped".
- **Not done:** deltas A-280 already refused while a product was mis-stamped are not reconstructed (recorded in A-248's residue); a resent movement is accepted as an UPDATE, which applies no delta. A sale from A-291's phantom product (legitimately moved to another store) on a staff till no longer lands in the wrong store's ledger; the movement stays parked (retried with backoff, visible under Settings → Device diagnostics "Stuck changes" and in Sentry) until an owner session pushes from that device or A-291 ships, per A-165's documented transient `forbidden`.
- **Review changed the first version:** it added the UPDATE-path check, replaced two uncached queries per movement with a memoized join, restored a direct test of the delta backstop (the rewrite had removed its only coverage), and corrected three docs this change made false (`laravel-server/AGENTS.md`'s A-280 opening, the test's docblock, `docs/FEATURE_ROADMAP_SPEC.md`'s open list, which also still named A-286 and A-249).
- **Second review (2026-10-23) changed:** the UPDATE check refused any movement UPDATE whose batch was out of scope, including a resent INSERT or a void that leaves `stock_batch_id` unchanged, which would have parked them for ever; it now refuses only a repoint. The batch-store memo lived on the controller, which Laravel reuses across requests, so a movement parked before a repair stayed refused after it; it is reset at the start of each push. `resolveParentStoreId('stock_batches')` now includes soft-deleted products (`withTrashed()`), so a batch INSERT under another tenant's deleted product is refused instead of falling back to the client's own `store_id`. The A-291 entry and the backstop rationale were corrected (movements are processed last in a push, which is why the in-push staleness the review raised cannot occur). The review also surfaced A-325: a batch UPDATE can repoint `product_id` at another tenant's product, logged as a Tier 1 entry rather than widened into this PR.
- **Still a judgment call (raised by both reviews):** a sale from A-291's phantom product now leaves the cloud ledger without that movement until it drains (see Not done above), where before the ledger row landed under the wrong store and only its delta was refused.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** server only; an existing refusal reason the client already parks, and no change to the response shape. Only **staff** tills park: an owner session's scope covers every store it owns, so its movements are accepted. The sale itself (`sales`, `sale_items`, `sale_item_batches`) still syncs; only the `stock_movements` ledger row is withheld.
- **Coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushStockMovementBatchScopeTest.php`, 10 cases: the cross-tenant movement is refused whole with its queue id and not stored, and the A-287 shape (a staff sale against a batch whose product is stamped to a sibling store) both RED before; an UPDATE repointing a movement at a foreign batch, RED against the first version; the delta backstop tested directly; a resent movement whose batch has since left the scope still accepted; a parked movement succeeding once the stamp is repaired; a batch under another tenant's soft-deleted product refused; and the own-batch, missing-batch and super-admin cases unchanged.
- **Full suite:** 1,542 passed, 1 skipped (2026-10-23, worktree with copied vendor).

### A-248 (part a) — an owner's device could move an existing row between the owner's own stores, which is how two production products changed tenant
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-23, server and client, TDD. GitHub #177. The part of A-248 that actually caused the 2026-10-08 incident; the entry stays open in `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` for the never-pushed legacy INSERT.
- **Was:** two halves. The client's `update()`/`softDelete()` stamped the **active** store onto any local row whose `store_id` was `NULL`, and into its push payload, even on a device holding two stores, where the active store is only a guess. The server then applied that `store_id` through `forceFill()` because `normalizePushPayload()` only rejects stores outside the caller's scope, which for an owner is every store they own. So an edit made under store 2 moved a store 1 product to store 2 while its batches stayed behind (the ±18 units).
- **Now, server:** `withoutStoreReassignment()` drops a payload `store_id` on UPDATE whenever the stored row already has one (every table except `users`), and honours a claim on a stored `NULL` only from a single-store session; the A-298 DELETE claim follows the same rule. **Client:** `assertStoreOwnership()` no longer claims a `NULL`-store row when the device holds more than one store, via the shared `deviceHoldsSeveralStores()` that A-303's pull guard now uses too. Rationale: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` (the `STORE_ID_BACKFILL_TABLES` notes) and `docs/CLIENT_LIB_COMMENTS.md#db-base-helpers-L62`.
- **A second instance, found by review and fixed here:** `normalizePushPayload()` filled in the active store on **every** `users` change whose payload carried no `role`, UPDATE included, so editing a staff member's phone while another store was active moved them to it. That backfill is now INSERT-only, and a resent `users` INSERT flipped to UPDATE drops a merely-backfilled store.
- **Second review (2026-10-23) changed:** a `NULL`-row claim must now name the session's one and only store (`claimIsEvidence()`), so a single-store session can no longer write another tenant's store into a child table's `NULL` row, and a super admin's claim is treated as the guess it is; the stripping moved right after authorization, so a dropped claim cannot cause a false version conflict on a quantity-only batch update; and a resent `users` INSERT always drops `store_id`, which closes a path where the sanitizer stripped an owner's own value and the INSERT backfill then put the active store back.
- **What it cannot fix:** an old bundle that already stamped the wrong store into its local row keeps that local copy until it next pulls the row's real store; the push response has no way to rewrite a local `store_id`. The server row stays correct, which is the change from before. On a multi-store device a legacy row now stays unattributed rather than guessed, by design. A server row whose `store_id` is `NULL` can only be attributed by a staff session or a single-store owner's push; for an owner with two or more stores, or a super admin, the remedy is `php artisan sync:backfill-store-ids` (A-288), and until it runs the row is invisible to every other till, because `NULL` never matches `whereIn` (§11).
- **Review changed the first version:** it added the single-store rule for `NULL` claims on the server (the first version still accepted a shipped bundle's guess for a server-`NULL` row), fixed the `users` backfill above, shared the multi-store check, corrected a stale `AGENTS.md` claim ("a client UPDATE never resends `store_id`") and the first version's claim that the next pull corrects the device, restored a docblock the new method had displaced, and added the cases below.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** shipped bundles keep sending the claim; the server ignores it for a row that already has a store or when it is a guess, and still honours a single-store session's claim, so legacy rows keep syncing. No field changed meaning.
- **Coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushStoreIdBackfillTest.php`, 7 new cases: an UPDATE naming the owner's other store (on `loyalty_tiers` and on `products`, the incident table), the INSERT-turned-UPDATE variant, and a soft delete sent as an UPDATE all keep the row's store (RED before); a single-store session can still claim a `NULL` row; a two-store owner's claim is ignored (RED against the first version); editing a staff member under another store no longer moves them while an explicit reassignment still does (RED before). `client/__tests__/store-ownership.test.ts`, 2 new cases: on a two-store device `update()` and `softDelete()` leave a `NULL`-store row unattributed and send no `store_id` (RED before).
- **Full suite:** backend 1,543 passed, 5 skipped; client 3,152 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean (no client change in the second review).

### A-303 — a legacy `NULL`-store category on a multi-store device was folded with an unscoped remap on pull, repointing either store's products
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-23, client only. `pull.ts`'s duplicate-name reconciliation now skips a `NULL`-store losing row when the device holds more than one store (`SELECT COUNT(*) FROM stores > 1`, the same unfiltered count `backfillStoreIdOnLegacyRows()` reads, so soft-deleted stores count too; an empty `stores` table, as on a first-ever pull before `stores` has applied, still folds, which is harmless because such a device holds no legacy rows). Suppliers share the guard. Single-store devices, where the legacy fold is safe and is the reason it exists, still fold.
- **How it was reachable, which the entry did not say:** for an ordinary session it was not. Since A-301 the name lookup is keyed `${store_id ?? ""}|name`, a `NULL`-store local row looks up `|name`, and a tenant-scoped pull never returns a `NULL`-store row (`whereIn` cannot match `NULL`). But `SyncController::pull()` gives a **super_admin** no tenant scope at all, so their page carries `NULL`-store categories, the `|name` key matches, and `remapForeignKey(…, null)` repointed every store's products onto a category no other device can pull. A first pass at this fix concluded the entry was unreachable and changed no code; the independent review found the super_admin path, and the test below reproduces it.
- **Push is deliberately left unscoped for categories.** A `NULL`-store category pushed from a two-store device is stamped with the push's store by the server's INSERT backfill and, on a name collision, comes back in `id_map`; the client must honour that mapping, so `remapForeignKey(…, null)` repoints both stores' products and requeues them. Store B's then point at store A's category, and `CrossStoreReferenceGuard` (A-302) re-owns each onto store B's same-named category on ingest, creating it if missing. Scoping the remap to the push's store would instead leave store B's products on an id that exists nowhere, permanently "Uncategorized". **Suppliers had no such guard,** so the same push remap left store B's purchase orders, supplier payments and batches on store A's supplier for good; closed by A-321, which extends the guard to them.
- **Residue on the pushing device:** until it next pulls store B, store B's remapped products point at store A's category id, and store B's category views filter `store_id = B OR store_id IS NULL`, so those products drop out of that category's count and filter there in the meantime.
- **Coverage:** `client/__tests__/duplicate-name-reconciliation.test.ts`, a pair over the same super_admin-shaped page: on a two-store device the `NULL`-store category is left alone (RED before the fix: both products were repointed), and on a single-store device it still folds. `client/__tests__/push-id-map-reconciliation.test.ts` pins the push remap as unscoped with an active store set, and fails if it is scoped to the push's store.
- **Client suite:** 3,150 passed across 492 files; `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### A-199 — two endpoints resolved a Personal Access Token from the request body without checking its abilities or expiry
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-23, server only, TDD.
- **Was:** `AdminStoreController::restoreSession()` and `AuthHandoffController::create()` (and `consume()`, which re-reads the token at redemption) called `PersonalAccessToken::findToken()` on a token from the **request body** and acted on its owner. `findToken()` checks neither abilities nor `expires_at`, and because the token is not the bearer, Sanctum's guard never checked its expiry either (the guard never checks abilities at all, A-315). `restoreSession` then minted a fresh `admin-refresh` cookie, which `/admin/session/refresh` turns into a 30-day `web` token.
- **Less latent than the entry said:** it called the problem latent because "no such narrow token is minted today". The admin `['refresh']` token is one. More concretely, an **expired** super_admin token, which survives until the daily `sanctum:prune-expired --hours=24` removes it a day after expiry, was enough for `restoreSession` to mint a brand-new session.
- **Now:** all three reads go through `App\Support\FullAccessToken::find()`, which returns the token only if it exists, records abilities (a NULL `abilities` row used to reach Sanctum's `in_array()` and 500), has the `*` ability and has not lapsed. Lapsed means `TokenLifetime::hasLapsed()`: `expires_at`, plus the global `sanctum.expiration` if it is ever set again, matching the bearer guard. `refreshAdminSession()` now uses the same helper instead of its own copy of the `expires_at` check. Rationale and the reason the body token cannot just be the bearer: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Body-token endpoints".
- **Not done, from the entry's fix:** "prefer deriving the identity from the authenticated bearer." Neither endpoint can: impersonation mints a handoff code for a token that is not the caller's, and restore-session restores the admin's token while the bearer is the impersonated user. Written down in the same `AGENTS.md` section.
- **Found while fixing it, logged as A-315:** a `refresh`-only token is accepted as a bearer on every `auth:sanctum` route, so the ability check this fix adds is only enforced on the body-token endpoints.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** no contract changed. Every token a shipped client passes to these endpoints is a live `['*']` login, web, impersonation or till token, so legitimate calls behave as before; only refresh-only and expired tokens are now refused with the existing 403.
- **Coverage:** seven new cases; the first five were RED before the fix, and the last two came from review. `tests/Feature/AuthHandoffTest.php`: `create` refuses a refresh-only token and an expired token; `consume` refuses a code whose token expired between `create` and redemption. `tests/Feature/Admin/RestoreSessionTest.php`: refuses a refresh-only and an expired super_admin token, and sets no cookie. From review: a NULL-`abilities` row gets 403 rather than 500, and `create` honours a global `sanctum.expiration`.
- **Full suite:** 1,513 passed, 5 skipped.

### A-318 — Cycle Count's Print, PDF, CSV and Excel exported the whole catalogue whatever the ledger was filtered to
- **Closed:** 2026-10-23. All four outputs now read one export set: the on-screen, already-sorted ledger list filtered strictly by Count status (`exportItems` in `client/lib/hooks/use-audit-ledger-lens.ts`), so it is always a subset of what the screen shows. The ledger's sort key and direction moved from `AuditLedgerStep` into the parent (`client/lib/hooks/use-audit-ledger-lens.ts`) so the screen and the outputs share it. CSV cells that start with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` are now prefixed with `'` on export (text only, numbers untouched) and the prefix is stripped on CSV import, and accented category names transliterate in file names ("Médicaments" becomes "Medicaments"). Counted Qty is filled when a count was entered. The print/PDF subtitle names the filters (`describeAuditLens()`), the CSV/XLSX/PDF file name carries the sanitised category (`auditExportFileName()`), and Print plus the three export items are disabled when the set is empty. The ledger's Totals row now says "(filtered)" for any active filter, not only a category. With no filter set the output is what it was (all rows, `#` 1..N, "N product(s)"). `stock-audits.tsx` went from 658 to about 300 lines on the way, with the export handlers in `use-audit-sheet-export.ts` and the markup in `audit-printable-sheet.tsx` / `audit-export-menu.tsx`. Pinned by `client/__tests__/audit-export-derivations.test.ts`, `stock-audit-rows.test.ts` and `stock-audits-export-filters.test.tsx`, `audit-ledger-lens-export.test.ts` and `spreadsheet-csv-formula-injection.test.ts`; rules in `client/AGENTS.md`, "Counted Qty opens EMPTY".
- **Not done:** the "Not Counted" export is strict while the screen is sticky, so the printout can hold fewer rows than the screen; that is intended and documented. `audit-ledger-step.tsx` is still over the 350-line limit (581). No real-browser smoke test of the print dialog, PDF or downloaded files was run.
- **Original finding:** reported by a store owner (Ada) on 2026-10-23: with the Category filter set, every output contained the whole catalogue. The row model was built from the unfiltered draft (`items`), not the filtered list, and the sort lived in the child, so even the filtered list would have come out in database order.

### A-320 — client-written `permission_groups.permissions` was double-encoded on the server, which skipped the escalation check and would have let the catalog backfill wipe a group
- **Closed:** 2026-10-23, alongside `view_stock_quantities`. `App\Models\PermissionGroup` casts `permissions` with `App\Casts\JsonList` (the `A-29` cast) instead of `'array'`, so a pushed JSON string is stored once and rows already double-encoded heal on read without a migration. `App\Services\Sync\LegacyStockQuantitiesGrant::apply()` decodes the push payload's `permissions` to a list before `sanitizePermissionGroupSyncPayload()` runs, so its "grant only what you hold" check now sees real client pushes. Pinned by `test_a_json_string_payload_still_goes_through_the_escalation_check` and `test_a_double_encoded_row_reads_back_as_a_list` (`tests/Feature/LegacyStockQuantitiesGrantTest.php`) and `test_a_double_encoded_custom_group_keeps_its_keys_when_backfilled` (`PermissionCatalogBackfillTest.php`), which fails against the old cast.
- **Not done:** no data repair is needed (rows heal on read), but production's raw bytes stay double-encoded until each row is next written. Whether the v2 backfill (2026-09-29) already overwrote any default group this way in production was not checked; it could only have happened to a default group a client had written before the server's first backfill of that store. A read-only query for `permission_groups` rows with only v2 keys would answer it.
- **Original finding:** found 2026-10-23 while building the `view_stock_quantities` old-bundle shim. The client writes `permissions` as `JSON.stringify([...])` and pushes the string. The `'array'` cast encoded it again, so the server read it back as a string: the escalation check (`is_array($payload['permissions'])`) was skipped for every real client push, which let a non-owner holding `manage_roles_permissions` grant keys they did not hold, and `ensureCatalogBackfilled()` read such a row as `[]`, so the v3 all-groups backfill would have replaced every client-created custom group's permissions with just `view_stock_quantities`. Confirmed with a throwaway feature test before the fix.

### A-311 — an unlinked till had no self-service recovery, and 30 days offline unlinked it
- **Closed:** 2026-10-23 in two parts. (1) Till tokens now carry a 180-day `expires_at` and admin tokens a 30-day one; the global Sanctum `expiration` is gone, existing rows were backfilled to `created_at + 30 days`, and `sanctum:prune-expired` runs daily (`laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Token lifetimes"). (2) The store owner can generate a single-use, store-bound, 15-minute re-link code (Settings > Account > Sessions & Devices, "Generate till re-link code"), and anyone at the unlinked till who can open Settings > Data enters it under > "Have a re-link code?" with no owner password (`laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Till re-link codes"; `client/AGENTS.md`, "Unlinked tills").
- **Not done:** the grace window on `/refresh` for a recently expired till token was not built; 180 days made it marginal. `_sync_queue` still has no cap while a till is unlinked, and a lost device still loses its unsynced sales. The code is entered in Settings > Data, which still needs `backup_restore_data`, so a cashier without it cannot re-link; widening that tab was left as the owner's call. The email/password path keeps its "email must match the logged-in user" guard; the code path does not need it, because the server binds the code to a store, not to a login.
- **Original finding:** found 2026-10-23, answering "what if a till unlinks and the owner isn't there". Tokens expired after 30 days and `/refresh` only runs online, so a till offline longer came back to a 401 and unlinked, and only someone with `backup_restore_data` and the owner's cloud email and password could re-link it.

### A-298 — a pushed DELETE's `store_id` claim was applied before the ownership check, so the claim authorized itself
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-23, server only, TDD. Found 2026-10-19 in the review of A-297's fix; not exploited.
- **Was:** `SyncController::push()`'s DELETE branch wrote `$payload['store_id']` onto a NULL-`store_id` `$target` and *then* called `authorizeChangeTarget()`, which reads `$target->store_id` through `resolveChangeStoreId()`. A caller naming its own store therefore made the resolver return that store and passed. The concrete hole: a victim's legacy `products` (or any direct-store table) row with `store_id` NULL and the victim's `user_id` — which the stored-row check denies through the `user_id` fallback — was soft-deletable by any tenant that knew its uuid.
- **Now:** authorization runs on the row as stored; the claim is written and saved only after it passes, so a legitimate legacy row's claim still reaches the server (§11), and only when it names one of the caller's own stores. The second condition came from review: `normalizePushPayload()` validates `store_id` only for `STORE_ID_BACKFILL_TABLES`, so moving the claim after authorization first let a fall-open child line (`purchase_order_items` under a NULL-store parent) take another tenant's store id. The old order had refused that case only by accident, through the resolver. The rule is in `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Authorize on the row as stored; apply a payload's claim only after".
- **UPDATE audited, not changed:** it already finds → authorizes → `forceFill()`s, so the payload cannot influence its own authorization. Pinned anyway, because the entry asked for it to be checked rather than assumed.
- **What this does not change:** a row whose ownership resolves to nothing on either side (no store, unresolvable parent, no `user_id`) still falls open and still takes the claim — A-297's deliberate fall-open, now the only remaining way such a row is writable, and unrelated to the claim. A NULL-store child line under a NULL-store parent is the example; `test_delete_against_a_line_with_no_store_of_its_own_still_succeeds` keeps pinning it.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** no contract changed. A legacy row the caller owns is deleted and its claim stored as before, and a cross-tenant claim now gets `forbidden`, which `push.ts` already parks and retries (A-165). **Legitimate cases changed with it, logged as A-313:** a NULL-store row whose `user_id` is outside the caller's allowed users (a removed staff member, one moved to a sibling store, or one whose `users.store_id` was cleared) used to pass only on the claim and is now `forbidden`, the same answer UPDATE already gave it. Accepted rather than widened, because widening the `user_id` fallback loosens UPDATE too, and the 2026-10-19 backfill left no live tenant's rows in that shape.
- **Coverage:** five cases in `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushOwnershipTest.php`. The `products` DELETE case was RED before (`Failed asserting that actual size 0 matches expected size 1` on `failed`, i.e. the delete was accepted); the UPDATE case was green before and pins the audit; one pins that the caller's own legacy row still deletes and records the claimed store; the child-line case the entry asked for (a NULL-store line under another store's parent, claiming the caller's store) is refused; and a foreign claim on a fall-open line is never written, which failed against the first version of this fix.
- **Full suite:** 1,485 passed, 5 skipped.

### A-255 — the daily close called gross profit "Net Profit" in its export, while the P&L tab uses the same words to mean profit after expenses
- **Closed:** 2026-10-23, part (a) only (GitHub #187). The figure is now labelled **"Profit before expenses (Est.)"** everywhere it appears: the metric card, the breakdown modal's title and total line, the CSV row (was "Total Net Profit (Est.)") and the PDF (was "Net Profit (Est.)"). One constant, `DAILY_CLOSE_PROFIT_LABEL` in `client/lib/utils/daily-close-breakdown.ts`, feeds all four so they cannot drift. "Profit before expenses" was chosen over "Gross Profit" because it tells a shop owner what is missing without accounting vocabulary, and because the P&L's "Gross Profit" (`net sales − COGS`) does not also net off tax and reseller payouts the way this figure does. The modal footnote originally pointed at Profit & Loss under Analytics for profit after expenses; since part (b) was built (below) it points at the "Profit after expenses (Est.)" figure instead. The arithmetic is unchanged. "Net Profit" now means profit after expenses only, and `use-bi-data.ts` and `use-monthly-sales-data.ts` still agree on `grossProfit - expenses`. Pinned by the CSV case in `client/__tests__/daily-close-reseller-profit.test.ts`.
- **Part (b), deducting expenses, was built later (2026-10-23) as a separate figure**, after the owner chose it, leaving "Profit before expenses (Est.)" as it was. "Profit after expenses (Est.)" (`DAILY_CLOSE_PROFIT_AFTER_EXPENSES_LABEL`) is the before-expenses lines plus an "Expenses for this day" line: every non-deleted expense dated that day in full, any payment method, and for an expense with `covers_months` > 1 the monthly installment for the report day's month divided by that month's days, rounded to 2 decimals, on every day of an installment month (not the full amount on its entry day). That answers the rent-day worry above. See "A Daily Close headline is the sum of its breakdown" in `client/AGENTS.md`; pinned by `client/__tests__/daily-close-profit-after-expenses.test.ts`.
- **Original finding:** found 2026-10-12, when a store owner asked whether the daily close accounts for expenses. It does not: `getDailyCloseData()` reads only sales, sale items, returns and return items, and the figure is `net sales − tax − cost of goods − reseller payouts`. The CSV called that "Total Net Profit (Est.)", while the P&L tab's "Net Profit" is `grossProfit − totalExpenses`, so two reports used one term for two quantities and the daily one overstated by the store's running costs.

### A-307 — a return never adjusted a reseller sale's markup, its unpaid commission, or commission already paid out
- **Closed:** 2026-10-21 in two steps. `useProcessReturnMutation` calls `resellerPatchAfterReturn()` (`client/lib/utils/reseller-return-adjustment.ts`), which re-derives the sale's markup from the lines that survive the return (quantity minus earlier returns minus this one) with `computeResellerCommission()`'s net-of-cuts rule, reading the sale's reseller state fresh inside the transaction. Unredeemed: `reseller_markup_amount` and `reseller_commission_amount` are rewritten. Kept by the store: only the markup. **Redeemed as cash:** the paid figures stay as history and the new nullable `sales.reseller_commission_owed_back` records `max(0, redeemed amount - recomputed commission)`; the return dialog warns before confirming, the details dialog shows "owed back by the reseller", and daily close subtracts `redeemed - owed_back`. Pinned by `reseller-return-adjustment.test.ts`, `process-return-credit-and-loyalty.test.ts` and `daily-close-reseller-profit.test.ts`.
- **Not done:** nothing books the settlement (no cash-drawer or account movement exists for a commission payout today, so there is nothing to reverse), and the owed-back amount is not marked settled; revisit once a reseller identity is stored. A sale with any line lacking `list_price` (anything recorded before 2026-10-21) is left as stored, so a return on one still leaves its commission at the full amount, the pending badge overstates it and Redeem pays out the refunded markup. Returning a price-cut line raises the net markup (floored at zero overall), so the reseller can end up underpaid with nothing recorded. Two tills returning different lines of one sale offline is open as A-309. With no `sale_item_id` on `return_items`, a second return on a sale carrying one product on two lines at different prices is attributed to the earlier line (the same per-product attribution `isFullyReturned` uses), so the recompute can drift by the price gap. Daily close dates both the payout and the owed-back to the sale's day, not the return's, matching how the payout was already counted.
- **Compatibility:** additive nullable column on both sides (`SYNC_COLUMN_MIGRATIONS`, Laravel migration `2026_10_21_000002`, `Sale` fillable); old bundles ignore it. Deploy the migration before client builds that write it.
- **Original finding:** found 2026-10-21. The customer was refunded at the marked-up price while the commission written at checkout stayed, so the pending badge overstated, Redeem paid out refunded markup, and cash already paid out was never recorded as refundable. No `return_items.sale_item_id` was needed: at return time each returned item is its sale line, so `list_price` is known.

### A-286 — `_synced_at` was stamped per change inside the push loop, so a slow push committed rows stamped long before they were visible
- **Closed:** 2026-10-20. `push()` now records every row it writes in `App\Services\Sync\PushedRowRestamper` (INSERT, UPDATE, soft DELETE, a revived duplicate-name conflict target and an accepted re-sent INSERT) and calls `restamp(now())` immediately before the outer `DB::commit()`. The restamp is a query-builder `update(['_synced_at' => …])` per table, so `updated_at` and `_version` do not move; the per-change stamp stays as the initial value for any path that is not tracked. A change that is rejected, or whose savepoint rolls back, is discarded and never restamped. `PULL_WATERMARK_LAG_SECONDS` dropped from 60 to 10, now only covering the second-precision hole and the commit itself. Pinned by `tests/Feature/SyncPushRestampAtCommitTest.php`; rationale in `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "The pull watermark lags".
- **Not done:** `stock_batches.quantity` deltas (`applyStockBatchDeltas()`) are raw SQL and never stamped `_synced_at` before or after this change; the batch row is covered only when the push also wrote it. The window between the restamp and the commit (the touch of `stores.last_sync_at` runs before it) is a few statements, not zero.
- **Original finding:** found 2026-10-18 in the review of A-282. A row's `_synced_at` recorded when its change was processed, not when it became visible, so a pull could stamp a watermark newer than rows still uncommitted. A-282's 60s lag was a mitigation, not a bound: `max_execution_time` is CPU time on Linux, the host is LiteSpeed, and the UPDATE branch's per-change `lockForUpdate()` lets a contended push accumulate several 50s `innodb_lock_wait_timeout` waits and still commit rows stamped minutes earlier — silently lost on any device that pulled in between.

### PG-15 — the admin logo linked every role to `/admin`, which only super_admin can load
- **Closed:** 2026-10-20. `web/components/admin/admin-sidebar.tsx` now links the logo to `visibleSidebarItems(user)[0]?.href`, falling back to `/admin` only when the role has no visible item (or the session has not loaded). A delegated session therefore lands on a page it can load; super_admin still gets Overview, which is the first item. Pinned by two cases in `web/__tests__/admin-nav-role-visibility.test.tsx`.
- **Not done:** a bookmarked `/admin` opened by a delegated session still shows the sync-failure screen. The logo was the in-app path; a page guard on `/admin` (the `web/AGENTS.md` Subscriptions pattern) would close the bookmark path and remains open.
- **Original finding:** found in code review of admin Phase 1. The reviewer's "every non-super_admin's landing page is an error screen" overstated it — login already redirected to `visibleSidebarItems(user)[0].href`; only the logo click and an `/admin` bookmark reached the error.

### A-249 — `_sync_state` had no store dimension, so a two-store device declared the inactive store synced without ever receiving its rows
- **Closed:** 2026-10-20, by A-305. `client/lib/db/schema.ts:274-282` now declares `_sync_state (store_id, table_name, …)` with `PRIMARY KEY (store_id, table_name)`, and every reader is store-scoped — A-305 for the pull path, commit `32c8b851` for the four stragglers. The related halves were already closed under A-274 (the panel no longer scores a no-copy device as a disagreement) and A-273 (the requeue/rewind loop the switcher lit).
- **The one residue is a deliberate non-fix, already recorded under A-274 — do not re-open it here.** A device switched into a store still leaves a fingerprint row in that store's agreement panel, and there is still no "forget this device" action. It no longer inflates the headline; it is tracked in A-274's entry and nowhere else.
- **The original entry follows, kept for its field evidence** (the switcher aggregation, `DRX-HO4QM3TWU`'s starved store-2 copy, and the mass-requeue observation), which is still the best record of how this presented.
- **Found:** 2026-10-12, answering "does a force resync on one store resync the other store on the same PC?". It does not, and the reason is worse than the question.
- **Location:** `client/lib/db/schema.ts:274-280` — `_sync_state` is `PRIMARY KEY (table_name)`, with no `store_id`. Server side `resolvePullTenantScope()` (`SyncController.php:1193-1203`) narrows `storeIds` to the single `X-Store-Id` the client sends (`client/lib/api/client.ts:33`), and the window is stamped per table on completion (`pull.ts:546`, server `now()` from `SyncController.php:888`).
- **What's wrong:** one watermark per table, one store's rows per pull. A sync under store A pulls only store A, then advances `products.last_synced_at` to *now* — a claim that covers store B's rows too. The incremental filter is `_synced_at > X OR updated_at > X`, so every store-B row older than that stamp is permanently outside the window. The device cannot discover them again; nothing errors.
- **Consequence:** on a device used for two stores, whichever store is inactive is continuously declared synced while being starved. This is the straightforward explanation for `DRX-HO4QM3TWU` reporting **0 units across 0 batches** for store `68e4c0e5` while its windows read recent — store 2's rows fall outside a watermark that store-1 syncs keep pushing forward. `forceFullResync()` does not escape it: `DELETE FROM _sync_state` (`index.ts:60`) is table-keyed and therefore clears **both** stores' windows, then refills only the active store's and stamps every table to now — repairing the active store and leaving the other with a false watermark. Resyncing after each switch ping-pongs the damage rather than fixing it.
- **Fix:** key `_sync_state` on `(table_name, store_id)` — a schema change, so §11 applies: the old single-row-per-table form must keep working for devices that have not migrated, and a migration has to decide which store an existing row's watermark belonged to (safest: treat it as belonging to the device's active store and leave the others NULL so they pull from scratch).
- **Field evidence that the switcher is the trigger, and the whole chain (2026-10-12).** Aggregating `area: sync-pull-window` by device over 24h: `DRX-Y8UK10GC3` store 1 — 450 events; `DRX-HO4QM3TWU` store 2 — 136; `DRX-HO4QM3TWU` store 1 — 48. **Both offenders are the two devices that used the store switcher, each hitting it under both stores. The devices that only ever served one store (`DRX-OCGGTOPX2`, `DRX-8DY6G5XH8`) produced none.** The chain, with one link corrected on 2026-10-17: a switch remounts the app, but `requeueOrphanedRowsOnce()` runs **once per install**, not per boot — it returns early unless `localStorage` has no `ORPHAN_REQUEUE_MARKER`, or `DatabaseProvider.tsx:124-128` passes `force` because the `pendingCrashes` marker is set. **A store switch on its own therefore does not mass-requeue**; the forced requeue needs that crash marker, which is why the two switcher devices (both of which were also crash-reporting) saw it and the single-store devices did not. From there the old chain held: every `_synced = 0` row is requeued as an INSERT (observed as 2 queued → 2683) → each batch of 50 is refused `version_conflict` → every refusal called `rewindPullWindow()` → the budget of 3 was spent inside the first batch → the re-pull that would set `_synced = 1` was refused from then on → the rows stayed unsynced, were requeued, and conflicted again indefinitely. **Both ends of that chain are now closed** (`docs/FIXED_BUGS.md` A-273): the server accepts a re-sent INSERT instead of booking it as a conflict, so the requeue drains, and the rewind is spent once per table per batch instead of once per row. A-248, A-249 and the rewind-exhaustion loop are three faces of one defect, and the switcher is what lights it.
- **The panel half of this is fixed (`docs/FIXED_BUGS.md` A-274); the store dimension is not.** A device holding no copy of the store reported an honest zero and the stock-agreement panel rendered it as a `-45,727` divergence. That row now reads "No local copy of this store" and is excluded from the "N of M disagree" count, because absence of a measurement is not evidence of disagreement. **The starvation itself remains**: `_sync_state` still has no store dimension, so a switched-in store's rows still never arrive. The panel no longer misreports it, which also means it no longer advertises it — this entry is the only remaining signal.
- **It also pollutes the admin panel beyond the device itself.** A fingerprint row is written under whatever store was active at push time, so every device ever switched into a store leaves a row in *that* store's agreement panel reporting its partial copy. Observed 2026-10-12 on store `68e4c0e5`: **five of six rows were devices that serve store `571582a9`** (two Amawbia tills, the owner's laptop, a stranded Android till and a retired Mac), each disagreeing by a different amount, while the store's own device was merely 48 batches behind on newly received stock. The panel's headline becomes unreadable and the one row that matters is buried. The two halves that made this actively misleading — scoring a no-copy device as a disagreement, and never ageing a retired device's snapshot (A-252) — are fixed in `docs/FIXED_BUGS.md` A-274, so such rows no longer inflate the headline. What remains is that the rows exist at all: a device switched into a store still leaves a snapshot there, and there is still no "forget this device" action.
- **Symptom to recognise:** a device in this state reports a *stuck, wrong* stock fingerprint that no sync corrects — `DRX-Y8UK10GC3` sat at +2655 units across 2654 batches while in the loop. It reads as catch-up lag and is not; the device cannot converge until `_sync_state` is cleared. `forceFullResync()` is the only operator-side exit, because it is the one thing that resets `rewind_count`.
- **This is a broken shipped feature, not an operational footnote.** The store switcher (`store-context.tsx:316`, `header-store-switcher.tsx`) is sold on the enterprise plan and is the whole point of multi-store support; A-249 means it silently starves whichever store is not active, on every device that uses it. Telling an owner "don't switch stores" withdraws the feature. Prioritise accordingly: A-206 (now fixed) stranded one device, where this one breaks the feature for everyone who has two stores.
- **Related observation, same incident, not yet explained:** on 2026-10-12 `DRX-HO4QM3TWU` reported **2 queued** under store 1 at 12:02 and **2683 queued** under store 2 at 12:24 — the queue grew by ~2681 across the switch. That is the mass-requeue shape `base-helpers.ts:525-530` warns about (rows whose `_synced` was never flipped back are treated as orphans by `requeueOrphanedRows()` on the next boot), and a store switch that remounts the app is a boot. Worth confirming whether a switch reliably mass-requeues, because if so every switch also costs a full re-push of the catalogue.
- **Until then:** one device per store. A device that must serve both needs a full resync after every switch, and the operator has to know the other store's watermark is now lying.

### A-189 — 5,088 products across two stores referenced each other's `categories` rows, so their category could never reach any device
- **Closed:** 2026-10-20. The data repair ran on production in both directions on 2026-10-19 (**2,138** products of Nest Amawbia and **2,500** of Enugu Agidi) — see A-301, which is the same defect found again from the owner's "my categories keep reverting" complaint. The ingest side is closed by A-302's `App\Services\Sync\CrossStoreReferenceGuard`, which remaps a cross-store `category_id` onto the pushing store's own same-named category instead of storing it. This entry's last open bullet — "the command exists but has not been run against production" — is no longer true.
- **A-189 is the originating report;** A-301 and A-302 carry the production measurement, the burst timing and the §11 reasoning.
- **Found:** 2026-10-08, investigating a store reporting "duplicates" and a catalogue that showed `UNCATEGORIZED` on 98.6% of products even after a factory reset and a clean re-sync.
- **Location:** `categories.store_id` / `products.category_id` data, plus `SyncController::pull()`'s `'categories' => $query->whereIn('store_id', $storeIds)` scoping.
- **What's wrong:** products belonging to store `68e4c0e5` carry a `category_id` owned by store `571582a9`, and 2,628 of `571582a9`'s products point the other way — the two stores are cross-wired. (Both belong to the same owner, two branches of one business; the defect is not owner-specific — the unscoped match that caused it would equally have merged two *unrelated* customers' categories, which is why the repair never touches the other store's rows regardless of who owns them.) The pull correctly refuses to send another store's `categories` rows, and `whereIn` never matches `NULL` either, so the client receives a `category_id` it cannot resolve. The catalogue's `LEFT JOIN categories` yields NULL and renders "Uncategorized". The server is self-consistent throughout (`SELECT COUNT(*) ... category_id IS NULL` returns 0), which is why this is invisible from the server side.
- **Origin:** the push-time natural-key collision handler matched a pushed category by name *without* scoping to the store, so a bulk import pushing `drugs` was remapped onto an unrelated store's `drugs`. The code was scoped by `2026_09_12_000000_scope_category_uniqueness_to_store.php`; the rows it had already mis-pointed were never repaired. The affected store still owns its own 0-product `machine`, `toiletries` and `wine` rows, which is the fingerprint of the remap.
- **Consequence:** no client-side action fixes it — not a resync, not a factory reset, not Health Sync — because the referenced row is one the device is never allowed to receive. It is also live cross-tenant coupling: a rename or delete in the other store silently re-labels or orphans this store's products, which is the hazard the comment at `SyncController.php:1607` already warns about.
- **Root cause closed 2026-10-08.** `findDuplicateInsertConflict()` matched a pushed category/supplier by name against the payload's own store **or any NULL-store row**, and `push()` backfills the caller's store onto the payload before that check — so a store pushing a name that matched one of the nine retired NULL-store seed categories (`Analgesics`, `Antimalarials`, `Vitamins & Supplements`, …) was merged onto the unowned row, and `pull()`'s `whereIn` never sends it. The NULL fallback is gone; a payload that genuinely carries no `store_id` still matches NULL-store rows, which is the pre-multi-tenancy path the `id_map` regression tests cover. Regression coverage: `SyncEndpointTest::test_push_does_not_merge_a_store_scoped_category_into_a_null_store_row`. The two existing `id_map` duplicate-name tests had fixtures creating the pre-existing row with no `store_id` at all; they now create it in the caller's store, which is the only shape a real duplicate can take once push backfills the store — their assertions are unchanged.
- **Fix:** `php artisan sync:repair-cross-tenant-categories` (`App\Services\Sync\CrossTenantCategoryRepairService`). Reports by default; `--apply` writes, `--store=` narrows. It finds or creates a same-named category owned by the product's own store and repoints the product, never touching the other tenant's rows. Products with a NULL `store_id` (9 on production) are reported and skipped — they have no owning store to repoint to and need one assigned first. Uses Eloquent so `updated_at` is PHP-generated UTC: a raw `UPDATE ... NOW()` here would write a timestamp ~4h behind UTC and the corrected rows could never pass the pull's `updated_at` filter (root `AGENTS.md` §7). Covered by `tests/Feature/CrossTenantCategoryRepairTest.php` (9 cases).

### A-300 — a device report request could not be re-issued while the last one sat unanswered, which hid every other diagnosis
- **Closed:** 2026-10-20. The entry described itself as fixed and was never moved out of `KNOWN_BUGS.md`; this is that move, with no change to the record below.
- **Its one open residue was folded into A-204**, which is the entry for a `sent` command that is never aged out or re-offered, and now also carries `SyncCommandService::forStore()`'s 50-command-per-store cap.
- **Found:** 2026-10-19, by the owner trying to request a report mid-support-visit and finding no button. `DRX-ZKI5K81UG` had read "Sent to till" since 12/10 — seven days.
- **Was:** `web/components/admin/stores/details/device-report-action.tsx` returned a badge **and nothing else** when the latest `send_device_report` command was `pending` or `sent`. The server flips a command to `sent` the moment a device claims it, and it stays there until that device reports a result — so a till that claims a request and then goes quiet (offline, stale bundle, read-only tab) left the operator with no control at all, for as long as the silence lasted.
- **Consequence, which is the reason this is filed rather than quietly fixed:** the device report is the *only* instrument that shows a till's queue breakdown, its "unsynced with no queue entry" counts, its stock integrity and its build hash. Losing the ability to ask for one blocks every other investigation — including, on the day this was found, working out why a laptop was showing empty categories. An operator standing in the shop could not get the one piece of data that would have answered it.
