- **One existing test asserted the old behaviour and was corrected, not deleted.** `PermissionGroupPrivilegeEscalationTest`'s delete case used `assertDatabaseMissing` with a comment stating the hard delete was correct for a model shaped this way. It is now `assertSoftDeleted` with the comment rewritten.
- **What this cannot fix, stated plainly.** Rows already hard-deleted are gone, so **no backfill is possible**. New deletes propagate from here; **old ones never will**, and a device still holding its copy keeps it indefinitely. **A per-device Force Full Resync does not help either** — it re-pulls what the server holds, and the server has no record the row ever existed, so the stale local copy survives the resync untouched. The only remedy is deleting those rows again by hand on each device, and they cannot be enumerated, because the evidence was destroyed.
- **Out of scope, filed:** `StockMovement`/`StockBatch` as `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` A-283 (same shape, but quantity is derived from the movement log, so the trait alone would leave quantities silently wrong — it belongs with the derived-quantity design). Found alongside and filed as A-284: `sale_items`, `return_items` and `sale_item_batches` have the same hidden-by-a-trashed-parent subquery bug, pre-existing because those parents always had the trait.
- **A-285, patched before merge after an independent review found two defects in this fix.** Both were real; both are now pinned.
  - **The `restore()` discarded what the owner had just typed.** The remap tells the client to tombstone the row it just created (`push.ts`'s `id_map` loop), so any column not carried onto the survivor is lost content. A category deleted while `is_active = 0` and re-created as active came back **inactive**, and the client's `getCategoryList()` filters on `(is_active IS NULL OR is_active = 1)` — so the category the owner had just made was simply **missing from the list**. `reviveConflictTarget()` now restores the row, applies the payload's user-authored columns (`name`, `description`, `parent_id`, `is_active`, by a strict `match` per §8) and **bumps `_version`**, because the row's content changed and a device still holding the old version must not win against it. `is_active` is a real client column — `SYNC_COLUMN_MIGRATIONS` in `client/lib/db/schema-migrations.ts` adds it to `categories` at runtime, which the base `SCHEMA_SQL` does not show — so the stale value really did reach the device.
  - **The `suppliers` half of the probe widening was an unrequested behaviour change, and this entry's own justification for it was false.** It claimed suppliers "had this hole already". **`suppliers.name` has no unique index at all** — verified against every migration; the only `unique('name')` in the schema is the store-scoped categories one. So a supplier named after a deleted one never collided and must stay a genuinely new row; remapping it revived the old supplier's phone, address and contact over the one the owner just made. The `withTrashed()` is now gated to `categories` on the table name, and the sentence above is corrected in `AGENTS.md`.
  - Coverage: `test_reviving_a_deleted_category_applies_what_the_owner_just_typed` (RED: `The owner re-created the category as active. Failed asserting that 0 is identical to 1`) and `test_a_supplier_named_after_a_deleted_one_is_not_remapped_onto_it` (RED: the `id_map` entry was present — `Failed asserting that true is false`).
- **Deploy ordering matters for this one.** `.github/workflows/deploy-backend.yml` ships files **before** migrations run — it only *detects* a pending migration afterwards and fails the run (A-210) — so between the file deploy and the migration, `PermissionGroup` carries a soft-delete global scope on a `deleted_at` column that does not exist yet. Pull is safe (`withTrashed()` strips the scope) and push is recoverable, but any store behind `CATALOG_VERSION = 2` reaches `ensureCatalogBackfilled()`, whose query carries the scope, and **500s on every sync** until the migration runs. **Run the pending migration from Admin > Maintenance immediately after the backend deploy completes** — do not leave it to the next working day. **On `dev` there is no such warning at all:** `.github/workflows/deploy-dev.yml` carries no schema check — only `deploy-backend.yml` (pushes to `main`) has the `?schema=1` step — so a pending migration on `api.dev` is silent and must be run from the dev panel's Admin > Maintenance unprompted, then confirmed with `GET /api/v1/health?schema=1` returning `"schema_current":true`.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** server-only. Old bundles already handle `_deleted` on every pulled table, and the extra `deleted_at` key the response now carries for these nine is dropped by `pull.ts`, which filters every pulled column through its own `validColumns` — verified in the code, not assumed. No shim, nothing to retire.
- **Cost on the next sync for a typical till:** one re-pull of each row whose `updated_at` the delete moved, i.e. only rows deleted from now on. Nothing is re-sent retroactively, because nothing changed about rows that were never deleted.
- **Coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncDeletePropagatesForAllTablesTest.php` — 5 cases, all nine tables driven through each. RED before on the three core cases (`categories: the DELETE destroyed the row instead of soft-deleting it`). The two reader cases were re-run against the traits-added-but-readers-unfixed state to confirm they were RED for the right reason: `A deleted purchase order must not hide its items from the pull` and, for the category collision, a push failure count of 1 where 0 was expected.

### A-280 — a pushed `stock_movements` row could change the quantity of any batch it named, in any store
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-18, server only, TDD. Full reasoning in `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Stock batch deltas are store-scoped".
- **Was:** `applyStockBatchDeltas()` updated `stock_batches` by id with no join to the owning store and no check against `$allowedStoreIds`. The movement cannot supply one either: `authorizeInsertTarget('stock_movements')` resolves ownership from the movement's own `store_id`, and `normalizePushPayload()` backfills that from the session, so the movement always authorizes. **Reproduced before the fix, not reasoned about**: a push from store A naming store B's batch took it from 40 to 15 and filed the movement under A, so B's pull never showed it. A `sales_staff` token is enough. The non-malicious shape is A-248's: a mis-stamped product moving quantity on one store while the movement files under another.
- **Now:** `isBatchOutsideScope()` resolves the batch's store through `products.store_id` (`Product::withTrashed()`, per A-176a — a batch of a soft-deleted product is still real) and refuses the delta `permission_denied` when that store is outside the caller's scope. `super_admin` is exempt, as elsewhere.
- **The fail-open for a non-existent batch is preserved deliberately** — no store resolved is not the same as another store's, and a batch may arrive later in the same backlog. Pinned by its own case, asserted as "no ownership refusal" rather than "no failure at all", because a dangling `stock_batch_id` is rejected by the movement's own foreign key first; that is pre-existing and unchanged.
- **The refusal is reported but today's client ignores it, and that is worth stating plainly.** Every `$failed` entry this method adds carries `'id' => null` (an accumulated delta has no queue row behind it), and `push.ts`'s failure loop begins `if (f.id == null) continue;`. So it lands in the response, the `Log::warning` and `SyncFailureRecorder` and nowhere on the device: it is **not** parked the way A-165 parks a `forbidden` row, and nothing is settled. Strictly better than a silent cross-tenant write, strictly worse than a visible refusal. Surfacing it needs the null-id case handled in `push.ts`; left out of this change on purpose and recorded here.
- **The edge this leaves open, filed as `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` A-287.** The movement row is still *accepted* while only its delta is refused, so for a mis-stamped product (the A-248 shape) the till keeps pushing accepted movements whose deltas are all refused and **that batch's cloud quantity never moves again**, while the device keeps deriving its own from its local log — a permanent single-batch divergence visible only in `sync_failures`. Before this fix the quantity did move, on the wrong store's batch, so it is a knowing trade rather than a regression. The fix is to move the check up into `authorizeInsertTarget('stock_movements')` so the whole movement is refused with its own change id and parks visibly.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** server-only, no shim, no response-shape change. A legitimate client never names another tenant's batch, so no field device loses a write it should have had.
- **Cost:** two indexed primary-key lookups per distinct batch per push, only for a non-super-admin caller.
- **Coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushStockMovementBatchScopeTest.php` — 4 cases. RED before: the cross-tenant case failed on `Failed asserting that 15.0 is identical to 40.0`, i.e. the exploit worked. The caller's-own-batch and super-admin cases pass either way and pin that the scope did not over-reach.

### A-279 — a re-sent INSERT that is behind was accepted but its content left stale for ever
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-18, server only, TDD. A regression introduced by A-273's own fix, found by a read-only audit of the sync engine and confirmed in code before implementing.
- **Was:** on `$isResentInsert && $versionConflict` (`SyncController::push()`), the server committed the savepoint, handed back its `_version`, counted the change processed and wrote **nothing**. The client's `markSynced()` then deletes the queue row and sets `_synced = 1`. A behind re-send is by construction a row whose content lost, so the device was left holding its losing snapshot with a correct version and no queue entry — and because the model was never written, `updated_at`/`_synced_at` did not move and the incremental pull (`_synced_at > W OR updated_at > W`) never re-offered it. Nothing left could correct that device. Before A-273 the `version_conflict` refusal drove `rewindPullWindow()` → a full re-pull, which fixed the content; that healing was removed without replacement, and A-273's own entry records the reasoning that the pull "cannot substitute for it" — correct about `_version`, wrong about the content.
- **Concrete harm:** a till that lost a `products.selling_price` conflict kept selling at the old price until someone ran a Force Full Resync.
- **Now:** that branch calls `reopenPullWindow()` — a **query-builder** `update(['_synced_at' => now()])` keyed on the model's own primary key, inside the change's savepoint, so `updated_at` and `_version` cannot move and no model event fires. The row re-enters every device's pull window; the re-sender's next pull overwrites its stale fields, which `pendingLocalEdit` does not block because the acceptance already deleted the queue row. Verified in `client/lib/db/sync-engine/pull.ts` rather than assumed: that guard keys off a live `_sync_queue` row, nothing else.
- **Every table reachable on this branch has `_synced_at`** — all 30 in `pull()`'s list were probed against the migrated schema. The probe is still made, sharing `stampSyncedAt()`'s per-push cache, so a future table without the column is a no-op instead of a SQL error.
- **Backward compatibility (§11):** server-only, no client change, no shim. An old bundle receives the row on its next ordinary pull, a path it has always had; the response shape is unchanged.
- **Cost:** every device holding that row re-pulls it once. One row per behind re-send, and a behind re-send is already the rare case within traffic that is itself a repair pass.
- **Coverage:** `SyncPushResentInsertTest::test_a_behind_resent_insert_reopens_the_pull_window_without_moving_the_row` — RED before (`_synced_at` unchanged), GREEN after, with `updated_at`, `_version` and the row's content all asserted unmoved. `client/__tests__/pull-overwrites-a-stale-accepted-resend.test.ts` (2 cases) pins the client contract the fix depends on — it passes either way, because no client code changed; it documents the contract rather than proving a change, the same way A-273's `versions` case did.

### A-277 — a stale device could reactivate an account a lapsed plan or a closed store had just deactivated
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-18, TDD. Pinned by `laravel-server/tests/Feature/UserDeactivationVersionBumpTest.php` (5 cases).
- **Was:** four writes changed `users.is_active` or `users.permission_group_id` without moving `_version`. `resolveUpdateConflict()` accepts a push whose `payloadVersion === modelVersion`, and the client's `update()` deliberately never bumps `_version` locally, so equal version means "the server has not moved", not "equal content". `is_active` is **not** in `USER_SYNC_FORBIDDEN_FIELDS`, and the self-edit allow-list only governs a push against the caller's *own* id — so a `manage_staff` holder's device still holding the pre-deactivation copy of a staff row pushed it at an equal version, took the apply path, and **reactivated the account**. The failing test asserted the account stayed inactive and found `is_active = 1`: the exposure was real, not theoretical.
- **Now:** all four carry `'_version' => DB::raw('COALESCE(_version, 0) + 1')`.
  - `SubscriptionService::enforceStaffLimits()` (the staff-limit sweep a plan downgrade triggers — called on **every sync request**, via `SyncController:2942`, so it is the opposite of a rare path).
  - `StoreController::destroy()` (store closure).
  - `PermissionGroupSeeder::ensureSeeded()`'s `permission_group_id` re-group — **also called on every sync request** (`SyncController:2952`). A-277's entry called this file a seeder; it lives in `app/Services/`, is not a database seeder, and absolutely can collide with a concurrent device push. The brief inherited that error.
  - **`StaffController::destroy()` — a fourth site A-277 did not list,** and the one a store owner actually uses to deactivate someone. Fixing the two rare paths and leaving the primary one open would have been theatre. It was `$staff->update([...])` on a model instance; that is **equally** exposed, because nothing but the sync push path ever bumps `_version` — no model event does — and `_version` is not in `User::$fillable`, so an instance `update()` would have dropped the key silently. Rewritten to `User::whereKey($staff->id)->update([...])`; `User` has no `updating`/`saved` hook, and nothing reads the in-memory model afterwards.
- **`updated_at` comes free on all four** — every one is an *Eloquent* builder, which adds it itself, so devices re-pull the corrected row. No redundant write was added.
- **On the `COALESCE`:** `users._version` is `integer default 1` (`2026_07_21_194736_align_schema_with_client_db.php:151`), so a NULL cannot occur. Kept for consistency with the documented shape and because it costs nothing.
- **`is_active` and `permission_group_id` deliberately stay OUT of `USER_SYNC_FORBIDDEN_FIELDS`** — the reasoning, and the evidence, is in `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` under "Why `users.is_active` stays pushable". Short version: the till pushes both, offline, as a shipped feature.
- **No backfill command.** Reasoning in the same `AGENTS.md` section: unlike A-276's 5,088 products, the exposed population is exactly `users WHERE is_active = 0`, which is a `SELECT` rather than a search problem, and it is a handful of rows.

### A-276 — three independent fixes: a repair a stale device could revert, an oversized device report lost with a 500, and a panel that would not let you ask twice
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-18, three independent fixes, TDD.

**1. A-245 — the cross-tenant category repair wrote `products` without moving `_version`.**
- **Was:** `CrossTenantCategoryRepairService` repointed `category_id` with a bare query-builder `update()`. The client's `update()` deliberately never bumps `_version` locally (the version only ever comes from the server), so **equal version does not mean equal content** — and this was the one place the server changed content without moving the version. `resolveUpdateConflict()` accepts a push when `payloadVersion === modelVersion`, so a device still holding its pre-repair copy was accepted, `forceFill`ed its stale `category_id` back, and `save()` then bumped `updated_at` and re-offered the reverted row to every other device in the store.
- **Not theoretical:** the owner confirms `sync:repair-cross-tenant-categories --apply` has been run against production stores, and A-189 names 5,088 affected products.
- **Now:** the same chunked query-builder update carries `'_version' => DB::raw('COALESCE(_version, 0) + 1')`. Kept as a query-builder update for performance; `updated_at` was already being set, because `Product::whereIn(...)->update()` is an *Eloquent* builder and adds it itself (which is what `test_bumps_updated_at_so_clients_repull_the_corrected_rows` already pinned).
- **On the `COALESCE`:** `products._version` is `integer NOT NULL default 1` (`2026_07_21_194736_align_schema_with_client_db.php:87`), so a NULL cannot occur and `NULL + 1 = NULL` is unreachable today. The `COALESCE` costs nothing and makes the statement independent of that; the test for it skips itself when the column is NOT NULL rather than asserting an impossible state.
- **Nothing relied on the repair *not* bumping versions** — the service has no other caller, and `_version` is read only by the sync conflict path. **The intended, and real, cost is pull traffic:** every device re-pulls every repaired product. For 5,088 products that is a genuine burst, and it is correct — those rows were wrong on the device.

**2. A-245's backfill half — `sync:backfill-product-versions`, opt-in.**
- **Why a command and not a migration:** rows repaired *before* fix 1 still carry un-bumped versions, so their exposure survives the code change. Per §11 that needs a backfill, and per §12 a write to production data is the owner's decision to run, not something a deploy performs silently.
- **How the already-repaired rows are identified:** they cannot be found by `category_id` any more (the repair made them correct, so `misalignedRows()` no longer matches them) and the repair logs only per-store counts, never ids. What *is* durable is that the repair bumped `updated_at` on exactly the rows it touched. So the command takes a **time window** on `products.updated_at`, with `--since` **required** — it refuses to run without one rather than defaulting to "everything".
- **It is a superset, deliberately.** An unrelated product edited inside the window is bumped too. That is safe for server data (the row is unchanged; it is only re-pulled), and its one real cost is that a device holding an in-flight equal-version edit to such a row has it refused as a conflict instead of accepted. Narrow the window to the repair's own run to keep that near zero.
- **Behaviour:** prints the database and connection it will write to **before** anything else; dry-run by default (`--apply` to write); `--store` to scope; chunked at 500. Re-running bumps by one again — the *effect* is identical (stale pushes refused, rows re-pulled), so it is safe to repeat, though not a no-op in the strict sense. No marker column was added for that; it would be a schema change to buy nothing.
- **Never run against a real database as part of this change.** Covered only by `php artisan test` against `:memory:`.
- **🛑 PRODUCTION DECISION, 2026-10-19: dry-run on production returned 5,182 products in the window (against A-189's 5,088 — the 94-row superset behaves as designed), and the owner decided NOT to apply it. Do not run `--apply` without re-reading this.** The reason is a cost the entry above understates. The command moves `_version` only — deliberately not `updated_at`, not `_synced_at` — which does avoid a re-pull burst, but the pull filter is `_synced_at > W OR updated_at > W`, so **no device ever learns the new version**. Every device would sit permanently one version behind the cloud on all 5,182 products with no path to correction, which means: (a) the next genuine edit to any of those products, on any device, is refused as a `version_conflict` and **dropped terminally** — recovered only via the rewind and a full `products` re-pull, by which point that edit is gone; and (b) the agreement panel reads `products` as `behind`, then `diverged`, for every store, because the device version sum is 5,182 lower. Against that, the protection only binds for a device still holding a **pre-repair** copy — and the repair *did* bump `updated_at`, so every device that has pulled since 2026-10-08 already holds the corrected categories. **That last claim was wrong and is corrected here:** it came from the admin panel's frozen agreement snapshots, not from `user_devices`. A read-only query on 2026-10-19 found **six devices whose `last_synced_at` predates the repair** — `DRX-E15KT930U` (05/10), `DRX-BI5JTZBDT` (06/10), `DRX-JIMA7TL8S`, `DRX-FIEJWTXDU`, `DRX-R1F46W4I7` (all 07/10) on Nest Amawbia, and `DRX-45EDLE2C2` (07/10) on EpiK Footwears. So the dormant population is real, not hypothetical — though **none of the six is a till in service**: five are nickname-less abandoned sessions on Amawbia, and EpiK Footwears is a store created for a family member who has never started using it. (For anyone reading `user_devices` later: **EpiK Footwears and Pikachuu Stores are not live tenants** — the second is the owner's own store for testing against production. The live stores are Nest Amawbia, Nest Agidi and Divamax.)
- **The decision still stands, for narrower reasons.** Merely *holding* a stale product cannot revert anything: a revert needs that product in the device's `_sync_queue`, i.e. a pending local edit, or a mass orphan requeue — and A-274 removed the `force: hadPendingCrashes` trigger that used to fire one, so the remaining path is a genuine queued edit on a device that has been dark for a fortnight. All six carry no nickname and generic UA labels (`Edge on Windows`, `Chrome on macOS`, `Chrome on Android`) while every named, in-service till synced on or after 12/10, so they read as abandoned sessions rather than tills in rotation.
- **And there is a better lever than the global bump if one returns.** Bumping 5,182 versions imposes a certain cost on all 25 active devices to defend against 6 inactive ones. If a dormant device ever reconnects, clearing its local database is targeted, costs nobody else anything, and removes the stale copy outright rather than refusing its push.
- **The one condition that would justify running it:** a till that has been switched off since before 2026-10-08 comes back. Run it **before** letting that device sync. Otherwise leave it; the command is idempotent and stays available.
- **When it can go:** delete `BackfillProductVersions` and `BackfillProductVersionsCommandTest` once the owner confirms they have either run it or decided not to. It exists solely for rows repaired before 2026-10-18 and has no standing purpose; nothing else in the codebase references it.

**3. A device report larger than the support column was lost whole with a 500.**
- **Was:** `/support`'s validator was `'message' => 'required|string'` with no `max`, and `feedback.content` is a MySQL `TEXT` column — **65,535 bytes**. An oversized submission reached the insert, MySQL strict mode raised, and the entire report was lost with a 500, on exactly the till that had the most to say.
- **Now:** `FeedbackController::MAX_MESSAGE_CHARS = 60_000` on the validator, so an oversized body is a clean 422 naming the field. The ordinary public support form is unaffected and pinned by its own test. The OpenAPI `maxLength` on the request body matches.
- **The client's cap already existed and was already 60,000** — the brief for this change said nothing truncated client-side, which was wrong. What was actually missing: the marker did not say how much was dropped, and the suffix was appended *after* slicing to the cap, so the result came out slightly **longer** than the cap it was enforcing. `fitToTicket()` now reserves room for the marker and emits `[truncated: N characters omitted]`, and `MAX_REPORT_CHARS` is exported so the two ends can be read together.
- **Truncation sacrifices the tail, and the header always survives** — identity, clock, missing tables and the queue summary are the first sections `buildReport()` emits.
- **Worth knowing before anyone re-tunes this: the report is already structurally bounded.** Every list `collectDeviceDiagnostics()` feeds it is `LIMIT`ed in SQL (20/50/100/50/20) and `buildReport()` slices several again. Today's reports run 15–25 KB and the cap is **insurance**, not a daily event — so the section ordering was left alone rather than rearranged for a truncation that practically does not fire.
- **Residual, stated rather than engineered around:** the cap counts **characters** and the column counts **bytes**. 60,000 characters plus the ~530-character `Name:`/`Subject:` prefix fits 65,535 bytes only while the content is predominantly ASCII, which it is — the report is ids, counts, table names and timestamps, and the only free-form text in it is up to 20 crash messages (themselves withheld when they look like SQL). A heavily multi-byte report could still overflow. The complete fix is `MEDIUMTEXT` on `feedback.content`; not taken here because it is a production schema change on shared hosting and was not asked for.
- **The 5/min `public-write` throttle is untouched.** The client's existing 429 message ("Too many reports sent just now. Download it instead, or wait a minute.") still fires and is still covered.

**4. `web/` — once a report arrived, the panel would not let you request another.**
- **Was:** shipped the previous day in `0470a731` (part 1 of A-274). `device-report-action.tsx` returned **only** a badge for `status === "applied"`, with no button, so after one successful report the control was permanently a label and a super admin could never re-check that device after a change — which is the entire point of it. The owner hit it immediately: a row reading "Report received 16/10/2026 20:53" and offering nothing else.
- **Now:** `applied` renders the confirmation badge **and** an enabled control, in the same stacked layout `refused`/`failed` already used. `pending`/`sent` are unchanged — a request is genuinely in flight then and a second would be redundant.
- **The button says "Request again" whenever a prior command exists** (`applied`, `refused` or `failed`) and "Request report" only on a device never asked, so it does not read as a no-op next to a stale "Report received" badge. The badge keeps its `DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm` via `formatDateSafe`; **it was deliberately not made to age** — unlike A-274's stock snapshots, a device report is a one-off artefact whose timestamp is the whole point, and an operator comparing it against "now" is the intended reading. No card surface was added. The super-admin guard is unchanged.
- **One §6 violation fixed while in the file:** the success toast rendered a bare `DRX-…` id. It now takes an optional `deviceLabel` and reads `Report requested from <label> (<id>)`, falling back to "Unnamed device". The label was already in scope at the only call site, which passes it to the sibling `DeviceRowActions` already; the nickname is preferred over the sniffed label, matching `ResolvesDeviceLabels`.

**Also found, deliberately not fixed — three more raw updates on a synced table that skip `_version`.** All are the same class as A-245 and all are reachable, so they are filed as A-277 rather than folded into this change:
- `SubscriptionService.php:219` and `Api/Web/StoreController.php:247` — `User::...->update(['is_active' => false])`. `users.is_active` is **not** stripped on the push apply path: a self-edit drops it (it is absent from `USER_SYNC_SELF_ALLOWED_FIELDS`), but a caller holding `manage_staff` pushing **another** user's row keeps it. So a manager's device holding an equal-version copy can reactivate a user the lapse sweep or a store closure just deactivated.
- `PermissionGroupSeeder.php:231` — `users.permission_group_id`, same path and same reasoning.
- **Confirmed safe, as expected:** `HealthSyncReconciliationRepairService.php:258` and `SyncController.php:2626` write `quantity` only, which the apply path never trusts from a client and always derives from `stock_movements` deltas. `Product.php:90` writes `stores.storefront_dirty_at`, which is in `STORE_SYNC_FORBIDDEN_FIELDS` and stripped before `forceFill`, and `forceFill` only ever sets keys the payload carries, so a field no client sends cannot be reverted either way.

- **Suites green:** server **1,388 passed / 4,191 assertions / 5 skipped**; client **458 files / 2,969 tests**; web **68 files / 372 tests**. `tsc --noEmit` clean in both `client/` and `web/`.

### A-274 — four sync-and-panel gaps: a vanishing report badge, throttles counted as refusals, a no-copy device shown as catastrophic loss, and a crash marker re-pushing a device's whole history
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-17, four independent fixes, TDD. Scoped out of the A-273 batch and taken next.

**1. `web/` — the admin panel never showed whether a requested device report arrived.**
- **Was:** `device-report-action.tsx` rendered "Report queued" only while `status === "pending"`. `SyncCommandService::pendingFor()` flips the row to `sent` the moment the device claims it, so the badge vanished on the next sync and the button returned — visually identical to never having asked. The real outcome (`applied` with a `result` like "report sent via sentry (unconfirmed) + support", or `refused`/`failed` with the reason) was already on `sync_commands` and already returned by `forStore()` through the very same `useStoreSyncCommands` hook the component called and discarded.
- **Now:** it takes the most recent `send_device_report` command for that device and renders its actual state — `pending` → "Report queued", `sent` → "Sent to till", `applied` → "Report received DD/MM/YYYY HH:mm" with `result` in the `title`, `refused`/`failed` → the reason on screen with the button still offered so the operator can retry. No API change, no server change, no new hook. The super-admin visibility guard is untouched.
- **One consequence worth naming:** the till's own rate limit ("Too many reports sent just now. Download it instead, or wait a minute.", `client/lib/utils/diagnostics-delivery.ts`) had never been visible to anyone, because the badge it would have appeared on had already disappeared. It is now.

**2. A-178 — a throttled push was counted as refusals.**
- **Was:** `recordRejectedPush()` tallied `changes_refused += max(1, changeCount)` for every plan rejection including `SYNC_THROTTLED`. A device paging a 1,400-row backlog through a plan throttle booked every row of every refused batch as a refusal, which is how Divamax read `12/10: 271 accepted · 1450 refused` with nothing actually rejected.
- **Now:** `DEFERRED_REASONS` (`sync_throttled`) tallies `refused = 0`. **No migration and no new column** — the alternative the brief offered, and the one chosen, because the signal is not lost: the `sync_failures` row is still written with reason `sync_throttled` and its timestamp, so the admin panel's refusal list still shows the throttle; it simply stops poisoning the success rate. `pushes` still increments, so PG-18 holds and the store does not look silent. Old readers are unaffected: no column changed meaning, and the two counters they read are now merely more accurate.
- **A-178's own constraint was satisfied for free:** "if throttling is excluded entirely and nothing else happened that day, the rate must render as unavailable, not as 100%." `AdminSyncHealthService::successRate()` already returns `null` when `accepted + refused === 0`, so a day of nothing but throttling renders unavailable without extra work.
- **Deliberately scoped to throttling.** `sync_disabled` and `store_limit_exceeded` keep counting as refusals: unlike a throttle they do not mean "come back shortly", they mean this store is not syncing and somebody should look — which is exactly what PG-18 added them for.

**3. A-249 (panel half) + A-252 — a device with no local copy rendered as a huge divergence.**
- **Was:** `StockDivergenceService::forStore()` judged `diverged` on the delta alone. A device holding no copy of the store reports an honest zero — per A-249 a switched-in store's rows never arrive, because `_sync_state` has no store dimension — so the panel showed `Device 0 across 0 batches` vs `Cloud 45,727` as `-45,727`, which reads as the store having lost its stock. A-252 is the same shape in time rather than space: a retired device id (clearing site data mints a new one) never reports again, so its last snapshot stayed on screen and red for ever.
- **Now:** two additive response fields. `has_local_copy` is false when the device reported zero batches against a cloud that has some; `stale` is true once the snapshot is older than 48h. `diverged` is `disagrees && has_local_copy && !stale`, so both are excluded from `diverged_devices`. The numbers are still shown in full — it is the *verdict* that changes, not the measurement.
- **The client needed care, because the server fix alone made it worse.** With `diverged: false` these rows fell through to the existing `else` and rendered **"Agrees"** — claiming agreement for a device that holds nothing, which is a worse lie than the one being fixed. They now render "No local copy of this store" / "Stopped reporting", and the headline counts only comparable devices, so a store whose only reporting device has no copy says "No device currently holds a comparable copy of this store" rather than "Every reporting device agrees."
- **§11:** both fields are optional in the TS type and the component branches on `has_local_copy === false`, so a bundle served before this ships (field absent) keeps its old behaviour exactly.
- **Not done, deliberately:** A-252's "forget this device" action for a retired till, which is a new endpoint plus a confirm dialog rather than an ageing rule. A-249's underlying `_sync_state` store dimension is untouched and still filed — this makes the panel honest about the starvation, it does not fix it.

**4. The forced orphan requeue on a crash marker — the suspected origin of the 20,000-row storms.**
- **Investigated before changing anything, and the `force` turned out to have no real purpose.** `git log -S` traces it to `931f7acb` (2026-09-28), a **performance** commit whose own message says the scan now "runs once per install, plus after a launch that found crashes queued by a previous session, instead of scanning every store-scoped table at every boot". So `force: hadPendingCrashes` was a conservative hedge added while *tightening* an optimisation — never a fix for an observed orphan. It is cited in `docs/FIXED_BUGS.md` A-160 and A-161 only as the amplifier that turned a handful of bad rows into constant re-pushes on a crashy device.
- **Its premise is also wrong, in both directions.** An orphan needs a *partial* write — row committed, queue entry missing — and `insert()` has wrapped the row write, its queue entry and its audit entry in a single `transaction()` with one save since A-8, precisely so that cannot happen (its own comment cites iOS backgrounding). Meanwhile `queueToLocalStorage` fires when the SQLite write **threw**, so no row was committed at all: "SQLite was unavailable" implies the opposite of "a half-written row exists". A crash marker is uncorrelated with the condition it was gating.
- **The real orphan producer was somewhere else entirely, and is now closed at the source.** `resetDatabase()` clears `_sync_queue` but only `LOCAL_WIPE_TABLES`, while `clearDatabaseForNewStore()` unions in `STORE_SCOPED_TABLES`. Three store-scoped tables were in that gap — `loyalty_tiers`, `loyalty_redemption_options`, `permission_groups`, the exact three A-161 named — so a reset left their rows at `_synced = 0` with no queue entry. They are now in `LOCAL_WIPE_TABLES` (a no-op for `clearDatabaseForNewStore`, which already covered them), and all three are in the server's pull list, so they re-pull after a reset like everything else.
- **So:** `force` is gone, `requeueOrphanedRowsOnce(tables)` takes no options, and `DatabaseProvider` no longer reads `pendingCrashes` at all. The once-per-install scan still runs for a genuinely new install, which is what the orphan repair exists for. Nothing is stranded: the one path that could still mint an orphan no longer can.
- **Coverage:** `local-wipe-leaves-no-orphans.test.ts` asserts `LOCAL_WIPE_TABLES` covers every `STORE_SCOPED_TABLES` entry — it failed naming exactly those three. `orphan-requeue-runs-once-per-install.test.ts`'s third case **asserted the old intent** ("scans again when a crash was recorded") and was rewritten rather than deleted, with the reasoning above recorded on it; a new case pins that the boot path no longer re-arms the scan, which is where the decision actually lived and what no unit test of the function could see.

- **Suites green:** server **1,378 passed / 4,164 assertions / 4 skipped**; client **457 files / 2,965 tests**; web **68 files / 370 tests**. `tsc --noEmit` clean in both `client/` and `web/`.

### A-273 — a re-sent INSERT is no longer booked as a conflict, so it stops dropping another device's edit (includes A-242)
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-17, server + client, TDD. Reported by a store owner (Divamax, `edc5b0f0-6f59-4ce7-8015-5f96fa311895`): stock audits made on her laptop reached some of her devices and not others — "some did, some didn't". Her refusal log shows `INSERT · version_conflict · stock_audits` and `UPDATE · version_conflict · stock_batches` at 17/10/2026 10:31. Three live stores carried tens of thousands of `version_conflict`/`stale_timestamp` rows.
- **Was (the mechanism, server side).** A device re-sends rows it already delivered — `requeueOrphanedRows()` (`client/lib/db/reconcile-identity.ts`) and `forceSyncAllData()` both re-queue existing rows as INSERTs by design. `SyncController::push()`'s existence probe converts each such INSERT into an UPDATE and then judges it by `_version`. A re-sent INSERT is **by definition a snapshot the server already holds**, so it can neither win nor be a genuine conflict, but the old code had only two outcomes for it: behind → `version_conflict` (noise), equal → applied **and `_version` bumped to `$modelVersion + 1`**. That bump is the damage. A different device holding a genuine pending edit to the same row, staged at the version everyone had, then pushed one version behind and had its edit refused `version_conflict` — which the client treats as terminal, dropping the queue row outright. The edit was lost with no retry, which is exactly the symptom reported.
- **Now:** `$isResentInsert` is set where the probe fires. A conflict on a re-sent INSERT returns **accepted without writing anything** — the server's row stands, nothing is added to `$failed`, `$processed` counts it, **and the server's current `_version` comes back in the `versions` map**. Versions equal still applies the payload but `resolveUpdateConflict()` returns `$modelVersion` rather than `$modelVersion + 1`. The apply-on-equal path is deliberately kept: `remapForeignKey()` depends on an INSERT→UPDATE to deliver a corrected foreign key, and the soft-delete restore at the probe depends on it too, so this is not a blanket no-op. Both are pinned by their own test cases.
- **The `versions` entry on the skipped path was missed in the first cut of this fix, and omitting it reintroduced the same data loss on the other device.** Worth recording because the reasoning that left it out was backwards. Accepting without writing removes the refusal that used to drive `rewindPullWindow()` → full re-pull → the device receiving the server's current version, which was the only path healing a stale re-sender. The incremental pull cannot replace it: the model is deliberately not written, so its `updated_at` still predates the device's watermark and the row is never re-offered — the stale `_version` is permanent, and that device's next genuine edit to the row pushes stale and is terminally dropped. The omission was justified at the time as "would change the response for a path old clients already handle", which is the wrong way round: the client's `versions` loop (`push.ts`, a table-agnostic `UPDATE … SET _version`) predates all of this and has always tolerated an entry for any row, so handing back the true version **is** the existing contract. Pinned by `test_a_resent_insert_hands_back_the_servers_version_so_the_resender_is_not_stranded`.
- **Backward compatibility (§11): zero client changes required, and that was verified rather than assumed.** An old bundle reads "accepted" as `markSynced()` (`client/lib/db/base-helpers.ts:571-592`), which deletes the queue row **and** sets `_synced = 1` on the source row — which is precisely what drains the orphan pool that was producing the re-sends, since `requeueOrphanedRows()` selects on `_synced = 0`. No new response field, no shim to retire.
- **A-242 is folded in here.** `SyncFailureRecorder::recordFailures()` keys `operation` off the original `$request->changes` entry, while `push()` flipped `$change['operation']` on its own by-value copy — so a refusal reached through the probe was recorded as `INSERT` although the server judged it as an `UPDATE`, and `version_conflict` is only reachable for a row the server already has. The display stated the opposite of what happened and cost an owner-facing scare. The loop is now `foreach ($changes as $changeIndex => $change)` and writes the flip back to `$changes[$changeIndex]['operation']`, so the recorder's own lookup sees the applied operation. The `web/` gloss was left alone on purpose: with the operation recorded correctly, "Conflict: another device changed this record first" is now accurate for every row that can still reach it, and after this fix an `INSERT` + `version_conflict` pair is no longer producible at all.
- **Client, the second direction the owner saw** (her edit reaching the server but not her other devices). `rewindPullWindow()` was called once **per refused row**. A 50-row batch where all 50 refuse spent the budget of 3 inside that one batch, and from the 4th refusal `restoreBaseline()` put `last_synced_at` back — cancelling the full re-pull the first three had correctly asked for, so a genuinely conflicted row in that batch was never re-offered and the A-205 fix failed silently. A rewind re-opens the **whole table's** window, so the 2nd through 50th asked for nothing the 1st had not already asked for. Now a `Set<string>` inside the failure loop rewinds **once per table per batch**.
- **Client, the report.** `restoreBaseline()` called `logCrash()` unawaited, so concurrent calls raced past `logCrash`'s SELECT-then-INSERT dedup and each wrote its own byte-identical `feedback` row (~30 observed from one iPhone). It is awaited now. Its message also claimed "this device cannot converge on its own", which is false — the budget is per round and `completeWindow` refreshes it on a skip-free drain, while the incremental pull keeps running throughout. It now says the budget was spent by refused re-pushes and that the incremental pull continues.
- **Client, expected-noise filtering — one source list, two checks.** `isExpectedSyncRestriction` held only plan restrictions; the read-only-tab message (`core.ts`'s `assertWritable()`, C1) and genuine network failures (`Failed to fetch`, `Load failed`) are expected outcomes too and were reaching Sentry, the server report and the `feedback` table. The first cut widened the single predicate and put every consumer on it, which was wrong for one of them: `sync-indicator.tsx` suppresses a **user-visible** error state, so an unreachable server began reading "Cloud Active" on an automatic sync — in a POS, a cashier shown "online" while nothing reaches the server is exactly the silent divergence this batch exists to prevent. So: `isExpectedPlanRestriction` (plan only) is read by the indicator, and `isExpectedSyncNoise` (defined as plan ∪ transient, so the plan list cannot drift between them) is read by the two reporting gates and by `push.ts`. `push.ts` stays on the wide check deliberately — a network failure is a whole-run condition, not a per-item fault, so aborting and rethrowing beats inflating every item's `retry_count`/`next_retry_at`, and the rethrow still reaches `sync()` so nothing user-facing is suppressed there. The rule for later additions is in `client/AGENTS.md`: the transient list means "do not report this", the plan list means "do not tell the user anything is wrong". The `_synced = 0` dedup predicate at `error-logger.ts` was deliberately **not** gated on either — that would introduce an UPDATE path for a table the client never pulls.
- **Server, device labels.** `ResolvesDeviceLabels::sniffedLabels()` filtered `user_devices` by `whereIn('store_id', …)`, so a device whose row sits under its home store rendered as "Unnamed device" in every other store although a sniffed label existed. UA sniffing is a property of the device, not of a store, so the filter is gone. Nicknames stay store-scoped — `device_nicknames` is `UNIQUE (store_id, device_id)` by design — and are still merged over the sniffed label by the same resolver.
- **Logging is per request.** A requeue storm is tens of thousands of rows on shared hosting, and three separate per-row `Log::info` lines sat on that path — the probe's own "Overriding INSERT to UPDATE" (pre-existing), the accepted-re-send line added by this fix, and `resolveUpdateConflict()`'s per-row "version conflict", which a re-send now reaches. All three are folded into one summary line after the loop, counting `$insertOverrides` and `$resentInsertsAccepted`; the conflict line still fires for a genuine conflict, which is rare after this fix and worth a line each.
- **Coverage.** Server: `SyncPushResentInsertTest` — 7 cases: the data-loss path end to end, a behind re-send accepted without overwriting and without a `sync_failures` row, the two `versions` cases, an at-version re-send still applied, a soft-delete restore, and A-242's recorded operation. 5 of the 7 failed before the change; the at-version `versions` case passes either way, because the apply path already populated the map. `Admin/ResolvesDeviceLabelsTest` — 3 cases, 2 failing before.
- **Coverage, client.** `push-rewinds-once-per-table-per-batch.test.ts` — 3 cases: two failing before (50 refusals spent 3 rewinds and restored the baseline), plus one pinning that an accepted row ends up both `_synced = 1` and stamped with the server's version. That third one passes either way, because the `versions` loop already existed — it documents the contract the server fix depends on rather than proving a change. `sync-indicator-surfaces-unreachable-server.test.ts` — 2 cases; the first fails on the single-predicate version with `expected 'Cloud ActiveLast synced never' to contain 'Sync Error'`. `pull-window-rewind-budget.test.ts` — 2 added (the await, pinned with a deferred promise so microtask ordering cannot mask it, and the wording). `sync-throttle-not-reported-as-crash.test.ts` — 4 added (the widened list, and the two checks agreeing on plan restrictions while disagreeing on transients).
- **Coverage, what was kept and what moved.** `pull-window-rewind-budget.test.ts`'s A-229 assertions were **kept**: per-round reporting is still the intent, and that file never pinned per-row rewinds — the per-row behaviour lived in `push.ts` and had no test at all, which is why it shipped. `push-version-conflict-handling.test.ts`'s outer `beforeEach` now clears `_sync_state` and `feedback`, because awaiting the report means a budget left exhausted by an earlier case writes its own `feedback` queue row in time to be counted by a later case's "the queue is empty" assertion. Suites green: server 1,371 passed / 4,144 assertions / 4 skipped; client 456 files / 2,963 tests.
- **What this does not fix:** A-249 (`_sync_state` has no store dimension) is untouched and still the reason a two-store device starves its inactive store. This closes both ends of the rewind-exhaustion chain that A-249's entry described, so the loop no longer compounds it, but the false watermark remains.

### A-272 — the oversold alert now says which item, and states its deficit instead of a phantom stock level
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-15, client-only, copy and one threaded prop. Found on Favour's store: the dashboard read "1 Item Oversold — Stock floored at zero — reconcile now" with no way to tell which item, and the Stock Batch Insights row that does name it read "Oversold: 4 unit(s) in stock (min: undefined unit(s))".
- **Was:** A-212 stage 1 made `getOversoldAlerts()` report a *deficit*, and `use-stock-batch-alerts.ts` maps it onto the shared alert shape's `quantity` — which `StockBatchInsightsTab` renders through the low-stock sentence, "N in stock (min: T)". An oversold alert has no stock level and no reorder threshold, so the number was labelled as its opposite and the absent threshold printed as `undefined`. The dashboard card, meanwhile, had `oversoldAlerts` in hand in `use-dashboard-overview.ts` and kept only `.length`, so the one screen that raises the alarm was the one screen that could not say what it was about.
- **Now:** the row branches on the threshold being absent and reads "Oversold: short by 4 unit(s)" (low-stock rows are unaffected — `getLowStockAlerts()`'s `HAVING … m.reorder_level > 0` means a threshold is always present there). The action-center card names the product when exactly one is oversold: "ampoul vellmax 20w — stock floored at zero." Two or more keeps the generic line rather than naming one of them, and a missing name falls back to it too; `oversoldProduct` is optional the whole way down, so no caller is forced to supply it.
- **Coverage:** `client/__tests__/stock-batch-alert-oversold-wording.test.tsx` (oversold states a deficit and prints no "undefined" or "in stock"; low stock keeps its sentence) — the first case fails on the old component with the literal `min: undefined` string. Two cases added to `action-center-alert-derivation.test.tsx` for the named and generic descriptions. Suite green: 454 files / 2,952 tests.
- **Not fixed here:** the deficit itself. A-265 stage 2 is still what writes the compensating movement; this only makes the one live deficit legible.

### A-271 (part) — a sale no longer invalidates another device's cost correction
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-15, server-side, one branch in `SyncController::resolveUpdateConflict()`. Found while investigating two of Cynthia's tills that agree with the server on quantity and disagree with each other on total stock value.
- **Was:** a quantity-only `stock_batches` UPDATE is accepted unconditionally (two tills selling the same batch is everyday operation), but the accept also returned `$modelVersion + 1`, versioning the row on the way through. Quantity is stripped from the pull and rebuilt from `stock_movements`, so that bump carried no information about any field a sale touches — it simply made every other device's base version stale. The owner enters cost prices on her laptop while the counter till keeps selling, so her `cost_price` push arrived on a pre-sale version, was refused as `version_conflict`, and the client dropped the queued edit, rewound the pull window and pulled the **old** cost back over her correction. Her evidence was a toast and a price that reverted.
- **Now:** the commutative branch returns `$modelVersion` unchanged, so a cost edit conflicts only with another cost or metadata edit — the case the check was written for, still pinned by `test_push_sync_does_not_exempt_stock_batches_non_quantity_field_from_conflict_check`. Nothing depended on the bump: the row is still saved and `_synced_at` stamped so the pull cursor re-offers it, and the returned `versions` map still carries the true server version, which means a stale client is now version-*stamped* by its own sale push rather than only by a later pull. That corrects the number, not the row: a device that has not yet pulled another's cost edit is still working from a stale cost, exactly as it was before this change. Old clients send `_version` and apply what comes back, so they see the same number and behave identically — no shim to retire (§11).
- **A claim worth correcting**, because it was asserted twice during the investigation and both times from code reading alone: the dropped edit does **not** leave a lasting disagreement on its own. `sync()` is push-then-pull in one cycle, and `restoreExhaustedPullWindows()` restores the baseline watermark at the top of every round, so an exhausted rewind budget pins the *budget*, not the data — its cost is a full table re-pull per round, not a frozen cost price. The chain fixed here produces **silently lost edits**, with both devices converging on the old cost. A lasting device-vs-server cost gap needs the row shielded from the pull, which is A-271's open half.
- **Coverage:** `SyncEndpointTest::test_push_sync_keeps_a_cost_correction_valid_after_another_device_sells` — till pushes quantity-only on version 2 (version must stay 2), laptop then pushes `cost_price` on version 2 and must be accepted. Failed before the change on the version assertion. Suite green: 1,365 tests / 4,126 assertions.

### A-234 — a stored timestamp a device could not read, or that sat in the future, silently switched off the daily health check
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-14, the day after the derived-quantity work made `healStockIntegrity()` the carrier of the stock-drift repair — which is what turned this from a curiosity into the thing that would have hidden the cure.
- **Was:** two guards in the same function. `if (!getLastSyncTime()) return;` treated "cannot parse the stored value" identically to "never synced" and skipped the whole check (A-234 as originally written). And `if (Date.now() - lastChecked < CHECK_INTERVAL_MS) return;` read a stamp **in the future** as "just ran": a device whose clock is ahead writes that future time into `localStorage`, so the check is skipped until real time catches up — the ~8 hours A-247 measured on one till, and for ever on a clock that has been reset (A-266). The same shape sat in `retention.ts`'s prune guard.
- **Now:** `isIntervalElapsed()` in `client/lib/utils/elapsed.ts`, used by the health check's interval and by the prune's (the prune additionally bounds its cutoff — see below). An absent, unreadable, non-finite **or future** stamp all read as due, on the principle that not knowing when something last ran is a reason to run it, never a reason to skip it. `hasStoredLastSyncTime()` in `storage-keys.ts` separates "never synced" from "unreadable", so only the first suppresses the check.
- **The existing suite caught a real bug in the first cut**, which is worth recording: the health check stores epoch milliseconds *as a string* while `retention` stores ISO, and treating every string as ISO made `Date.parse("1760…")` return `NaN` — so every device would have read as "never checked" and run the check on every app open. `toMillis()` now tries a numeric parse before `Date.parse`, and both forms are pinned.
- **The prune needed a bounded clock, not a different guard, and it took two attempts to get there.** A guard that *reads* should run when the clock is in doubt; one that *deletes* must not, and the prune derives its 730-day cutoff from the same suspect clock, so a device reading two years ahead would have dropped its whole local trail instead of a tail. The first attempt made the prune refuse on a future stamp — which a vet then showed converts one clock excursion into a **permanent** stop, because the prune is the only writer of that key and the stamp stays ahead of real time for ever. The fix is to bound the cutoff instead: `boundedNow()` takes the lower of this device's clock and `MAX(_sync_state.last_synced_at)`, which is the server's own `server_timestamp` and so can never run ahead of real time. The prune is then safe to run on any clock and goes back through `isIntervalElapsed()`; the cost is a cutoff lagging by the time since the last sync, against a 730-day window. A device that has never synced has nothing to bound with and also nothing to prune, since only `_synced = 1` rows are touched. Two cases in `audit-log-local-retention.test.ts`. (Not data loss either way — the server keeps the trail — but it is the device's offline history.)
- **What replaced the silence is noise, and it is a real trade, not a free win.** On a till whose clock stays ahead, the stamp the health check writes is in the future again, so the check is due on **every app open and reconnect** until the clock is corrected: one `/app/sync/counts` call, one `verifyStockIntegrity()` scan, and — for a till with an unreconstructable batch, which never folds — one Sentry report each time. Bounded by mounts, by the server's own throttle (a refused sync skips the check) and by `MAX_NON_IMPROVING_RESYNCS`, so it cannot loop, and "runs too often" beats "silently never runs" when what it carries is the drift repair. The clean fix is a server-anchored clock rather than `Date.now()`, which is the same fix the trading-day work needs (A-247); `boundedWallClock()` is not it, because its anchor is in-memory and seeded from `Date.now()` unless a server clock was read in that session.
- **Deliberately not changed:** the in-memory throttles (`error-logger`'s report throttle, the save-failure notice, the toast dedup, the arrow-key scan TTL, the PIN-recovery cooldown). They are module-scope values reset on every run, so a stored future stamp cannot reach them; a clock jump mid-session costs at most one interval.
- **Coverage:** `client/__tests__/elapsed-interval-guard.test.ts` (7 cases: absent, unparseable, non-finite, future by a second / by 8 hours / by a year, on the interval, inside it, and both stored formats). `sync-health-check.test.ts` passes unmodified — it is what caught the string-format bug.

### A-212 / A-265 stage 1 — the oversold alert now reports what the ledger is actually short, and the counting screen says so
- **Shipped:** 2026-10-14, client-only, no behaviour change to any write. Stage 1 of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-oversell-compensating-movement-design.md`, which exists because A-265 (a count on a batch carrying an oversell deficit is absorbed before it adds anything) needed a way to see the deficits before anything could fix them.
- **Was:** `getOversoldAlerts()` selected movements `WHERE sm.reason LIKE '%oversold%'` inside a 30-day window, grouped per product, reporting `MAX(ABS(sm.quantity))`. It answered "which products had a movement somebody labelled oversold recently, and how big was the largest one" rather than "whose ledger is short, and by how much". Four weaknesses in one query: it depended on free text; a deficit older than 30 days silently dropped off while still being owed; `MAX(ABS(…))` is the size of one movement, not the shortfall; and a change of arithmetic that stopped writing that reason string would have made it go permanently silent while deficits accumulated.
- **Now:** `getBatchDeficits()` in `client/lib/db/batch-quantity.ts` — the same grouped sum the recompute already runs, without the clamp: per batch, `Σ quantity` over non-deleted movements where that sum is below zero, scoped to the active store and to active batches, largest first. `getOversoldAlerts()` is a projection of it, keeping the exact shape its two consumers read, so `use-dashboard-overview.ts` and `use-stock-batch-alerts.ts` are untouched. A product appears while its ledger is short and stops appearing when the shortfall is made up, rather than when a month passes.
- **A carve-out batch is excluded even when its log sums below zero** (A-148): the units it sold were on the shelf, they were simply never written down, so nothing is owed and an inbound movement is not absorbed. The set reported is exactly the set `resolveBatchQuantity()` would rebuild to 0 — the definition and the alert cannot disagree. Two tests in the existing `get-oversold-alerts.test.ts` turned out to depend on the old semantics and were rewritten rather than deleted: the 30-day one now asserts that a months-old shortfall is still reported (that was the defect), and a third was added for the legacy shape.
- **Both readers are gated on `movementLogIsComplete()`.** Mid-catch-up a batch's sales can arrive pages before the receipt that covers them, so a raw sum goes negative for reasons the next page explains. The counting screen had that gate from the start; the dashboard alert did not, which a vet caught before this was pushed — "N Items Oversold — Reconcile Now" on a till updating mid-sync is how you send staff to count a shelf that is fine. `getBatchDeficits()` itself stays ungated, because measuring is not asserting.
- **The counting screen tells the truth now.** `useCountFreshness()` carries the active store's deficits and `CountFreshnessWarning` names each short product and what a count will actually do, alongside the existing staleness copy, acknowledged rather than blocked (A-211's pattern). **Read only when `movementLogIsComplete()` holds** — A-212's own condition: on a till that is behind, a negative sum can mean a delivery has not arrived yet, and alerting on that sends staff to count shelves that are fine.
- **Coverage:** `client/__tests__/batch-deficits.test.ts` (11 cases over real sql.js, including the live `gluformin` shape of 4 in and 6 out, the A-212 months-old case, reason-text independence, soft-deleted movements, deactivated batches, store scoping and per-product aggregation) and `count-freshness-warning-deficit.test.tsx` (4). Suite 447 files / 2,897 tests.
- **Still open:** A-265 itself. This stage only makes the deficit visible; the compensating `+N` write that stops it accruing is stage 2 and is gated on the owner's decision about what the stock ledger should say. The one live deficit is two units on `gluformin` (store `571582a9`).

### Derived stock quantity, Stage 1 — `stock_batches.quantity` is now a cache of its own movement log (closes A-232)
- **Shipped:** 2026-10-13, client-only. Stage 1 of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-derived-stock-quantity-design.md`; Stage 0 (`feat/stock-drift-autoheal`) shipped 2026-10-10 in PR #153.
- **Was:** every replica maintained `quantity` incrementally, applying `MAX(0, quantity + delta)` once per movement — `pull.ts` per pulled row, five local call sites per write, `deferred-stock-deltas.ts` per drained delta, and `SyncController::applyStockBatchDeltas()` per push. A per-movement floor is not commutative, so two devices applying the same movements in a different order land on different numbers and stay there: A-197 (a till reading 80 where the server had 52, 255 of 1,952 batches diverged), A-176's stranded deltas, and the 2026-10-08 "exactly twice the opening stock" shape are all the same defect.
- **Now:** `onHand(batch) = MAX(0, Σ its non-deleted movements)`, rebuilt whenever that log changes. One definition in `client/lib/db/batch-quantity.ts` (`resolveBatchQuantity()`, `recomputeBatchQuantities()`, `onHandFromLog()`), reached from two hooks in `base-helpers.ts`'s `insert()`/`softDelete()` — the chokepoint all ten local movement writers already pass through — and from one rebuild per batch per page in `pull.ts`. Order-independent, idempotent, and loss-tolerant: a movement that arrives late contributes the moment the batch is next rebuilt, so nothing has to be remembered and nothing can be forgotten.
- **The pre-writes had to go, not just be reordered.** The A-148 carve-out patches a batch by its delta, so a caller that had already written the quantity got counted twice. `updateStockBatchQuantity()` is deleted (its three stock-audit/adjustment call sites now rely on the hook), `restoreBatchQuantity()`'s and the demo seeder's writes are gone, and `deductFromBatch()` keeps its FEFO fallback honest with an in-memory `liveQuantity` map rather than a database round-trip. Net −119/+156 lines across 13 files.
- **The deferred-delta table is demoted, not deleted.** `applyDeferredStockDeltas()` rebuilds the batch instead of adding the stored delta, so it is idempotent and A-197's mid-round ordering hack is no longer load-bearing. `_pending_stock_deltas` stays because its *absence* signal still matters — a batch that never arrives means an incomplete catalogue, which is what reopens the `stock_batches` pull window (A-176). Stage 3 deletes both.
- **A-232 is closed by the same change.** The three untrue comments in `stock-integrity.ts` are gone: the soft-delete hazard (a soft-deleted movement is now simply absent from the sum), the backwards statement of that hazard, and the false client/server equivalence. `replayMovements()` is an alias of `onHandFromLog()`, so the fold, the auto-heal and the write path cannot drift apart again.
- **One deliberate behaviour change, and it is user-visible.** A single floor over the sum carries an oversell's deficit forward where a per-movement floor forgave it: `+80, −100, +20` is now `0`, not `20`. Forgiving it properly means writing an explicit compensating `shrinkage` movement at oversell time, which is the spec's Stage 4; a silent clamp is what made the old number path-dependent in the first place. Oversells are read off the log by `getOversoldAlerts()`, never off `quantity`.
- **Coverage:** `client/__tests__/batch-quantity-recompute.test.ts` (12 cases over real sql.js: the Pentazocine 80/−28/52 case, idempotence, loss tolerance, order independence, the oversell carry-forward, an unpushed movement counting, a voided movement's units returning, the push queue row and `updated_at` bump still happening, and three carve-out cases including A-215's adjusted-legacy batch). `pull-deferred-delta-order-across-pages.test.ts`'s 60-case fuzz now passes with the batch on any page and no drain at all. `stock-integrity-fold.test.ts`'s four path-dependence tests are rewritten around the new definition, and the three in-memory fakes that stub `local-database` share `__tests__/helpers/fake-batch-quantity.ts` so a fake cannot model a rule the code no longer follows. Full suite green: 445 files, 2,871 tests.
- **The review caught the carve-out being judged one instant too late, and it would have destroyed stock.** As first written, `resolveBatchQuantity()` took the `stored > inbound` test *after* the new movement was already in the log. `inbound` only grows while `stored` decays with every sale, so a legacy batch crossed out of the carve-out the moment its inbound caught up — a batch holding 100 unlogged units, sold down to 5, then found to hold 10 more rebuilt to **0** instead of 15, and propagated that to every other device as the movement arrived. The fix is two rules, both now stated in `client/AGENTS.md`: the test subtracts the change's own inbound back off (`BatchLogChange`), and a `stored` already equal to `MAX(0, Σ log)` short-circuits to "nothing to write" *before* the classification. That second rule is also what makes a batch created together with its first inbound movement safe, replacing an equality that the reviewer rightly called accidental, and it keeps one push row per created batch instead of two (worth 2,495 `_sync_queue` and 2,495 `audit_logs` rows on a full catalogue import). Three cases pin it; `__tests__/helpers/fake-batch-quantity.ts` was wrong in the same direction as the bug, which is why a new property test now asserts the fake and `resolveBatchQuantity()` agree over 80 randomised histories.
- **The re-vet then broke the fix's own shortcut, which is why batches are created empty.** Judging the carve-out against the pre-change log doubled a receipt — a batch inserted holding its first movement's quantity reads as a carve-out and got the delta patched on top (40 → 80) — so the first cut added a value-based short-circuit: if `stored` already equals `MAX(0, Σ log)`, write nothing. That is indistinguishable from a legacy batch receiving an adjustment the same size as its unlogged residue, and it silently dropped it: a batch holding 100 unlogged units, counted and found to hold 100 more, stayed at **100**. Provenance cannot be read off the numbers, so it was removed at the source instead — procurement receiving, product import and a transfer's destination now `insert` the batch with `quantity: 0` and let its first movement fill it. Three cases pin it. The cost is one extra `_sync_queue`/`audit_logs` row per created batch (~2,500 each on a full import), which gives back the saving above; recovering it means merging that `update()` into the batch's still-pending `INSERT` queue row, which `collapsibleUpdate()` deliberately refuses to do, and that is push-engine work rather than part of this.
- **And it found the drain lying about its own change.** `applyDeferredStockDeltas()` passed the deltas it was settling as though it were adding them to the log, but their movements were written pages earlier — so the pre-change subtraction invented an inbound of zero, the batch read as a carve-out, and 7 became 14. It now passes `{delta: 0, inbound: 0}`: a `BatchLogChange` describes what *this* write did to the log, never what a stored row happens to say. `pull-deferred-stock-delta-survives-failed-round.test.ts` caught it. One fixture in `deferred-stock-delta-chronic-reporting.test.ts` had to change with it: it seeded a batch holding 95 unlogged units *and* a pending delta, which no code path can produce (a delta is deferred only when the batch row is absent, and an arriving batch has its quantity stripped), so it now seeds the reachable state.
- **What the vet left standing:** no surviving pre-write, no other writer of a movement or of `quantity` (bar the documented 410'd Health Sync path), transaction and ordering safety, no new store-ownership rejection, read-only mode intact, and per-sale queue volume unchanged. The one hole it found that cannot be closed from the log is `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` A-264 — measured against production on 2026-10-14 at **zero exposure**, with the query recorded in that entry. Two review rounds, five findings, three of them stock-destroying or stock-doubling and none of them caught by 2,871 passing tests; the suite is 2,880 now, and the gap is what a second reader was for.
- **Still open:** the server half. `applyStockBatchDeltas()` still accumulates per push and applies one floored UPDATE, so `device_stock_reports.quantity_delta` will **not** reach 0 until Stage 2 — it must stop growing, which is the field signal to read before shipping it. A-217 is that gap, now halved.

### A-263 — a cycle count measured itself against the wrong moment, dropping real discrepancies and reversing legitimate sales
- **Found:** 2026-10-13, adversarial vet of PR #160, as a resumed-draft problem. The design pass that followed found it is **not** limited to resumed drafts.
- **Was:** `AuditItem.systemQty` was frozen when the sheet was built, and submit posted only rows differing from that frozen figure. Two failures, in opposite directions:
  - **Dropped count.** Sheet opens with 10 on hand; the day's sales take live stock to 0; the counter reaches the shelf, finds 10, enters 10. The row matched the stale 10, was filtered out, and a real 10-unit discrepancy was never recorded — the summary said "0 adjusted".
  - **Reversed sale.** Count a shortage at 09:05 (shelf 7 against a system 10), 5 units sell, submit at 17:00. `submitStockAudit` re-read live (5) and posted 7 − 5 = **+2**, putting the day's sales back as found stock. This needed no resume at all, only a sale between entering a figure and pressing submit — the normal case for a single-till shop counting through a trading shift.
- **Fix:** a count is an observation at an instant, so each counted row now carries the system figure as it was **at the moment that figure was entered**. `countRow()` (`client/components/stock-batch/stock-audits.tsx`) re-reads live stock on every entry and on every tick and stores it as that row's `systemQty`; `toStockAuditSubmissions()` sends it as `baselineQty`; `submitStockAudit` computes `countedQty − baselineQty` and applies it on top of whatever live stock has since become. The typed value lands optimistically so typing never waits on the database.
- **A review caught the fix making one case worse, which is why `baselineQty` is its own field.** The first cut sent `systemQty` as the baseline for every row. On a draft resumed from an older build that figure is still the sheet-build one, so a row counted after stock had moved posted the reversal this entry exists to prevent — built at 10, 5 sold, counter finds and enters 5, and `5 − 10 = −5` zeroed the product. Only a row `countRow()` has actually re-read now carries a baseline; everything else sends none and falls back to the re-read, i.e. the arithmetic it replaced.
- **Counting is last-typed-wins.** `EditableNumberCell` commits per keystroke, so "12" is two counts whose live reads can resolve out of order (a read queues behind a *reserved* sync transaction but bypasses a *running* one), which would have left the row holding "1". `createCountSequencer()` tokens each count and drops a read that is no longer current; the same guard stops a read landing on a row that was cleared, or on a sheet that was started over.
- **Why `systemQty` was still reused for display:** every existing consumer — the diff column, `hasError`, the review step's "Qty: X → Y", the differences filter, `differsFromSystem()` — then becomes correct for free, and the persisted draft shape does not change, so no `version: 2` migration.
- **No schema change.** `baselineQty` is a submission field, not a column; the `stock_audits` row still writes `expected_quantity`/`actual_quantity`/`difference`. For a cycle-count row `expected_quantity` now means system-at-count rather than system-at-submit, which is the better "expected" for a history view. A `counted_at` column was considered and rejected: it is forensic only, and `pull.ts` builds its INSERT from the server row's keys, so an old bundle pulling an unknown column throws — shipping one needs a column filter a release ahead.
- **Deliberately unchanged:** a submission with **no** `baselineQty` keeps the old re-read semantics, because the catalog quick edit and the CSV import mean "the stock is X right now". `stock-audit-concurrent-sale-race.test.ts` passes unmodified.
- **Coverage:** `client/__tests__/stock-audit-baseline.test.ts` (nine cases over real sql.js: the dropped count, the sale-after-count, shortage with and without movement, the negative clamp, a price-only row with a stale baseline, and the no-baseline fallback), `audit-count-row-baseline.test.tsx` (three, including the optimistic patch), plus assertions in `audit-derivations.test.ts` and `audit-ledger-blank-counted-qty.test.tsx`. `stock-audit.test.ts`'s fake was returning nothing for the live-quantity read, which is how the old behaviour looked correct — it now models the real SUM.
- **Still open:** the cross-device half of A-211. A movement made on another till and pulled after submit cannot be seen by any client-only baseline; the count-freshness warning remains its mitigation.

### A-261 — receiving stored a sub-kobo `cost_price`, and two of the three facts first recorded about it were wrong
- **Found:** 2026-10-13 in a Fable review of the A-260 rounding pass; logged open as a product decision, then re-investigated on 2026-10-13 before being closed.
- **Was:** `resolveBaseUnitCost()` (`client/components/procurement/po-line-item-math.ts`) divides a per-bulk cost by units-per-bulk with no rounding, so a ₦1,000 carton of 3 stored `333.3333333333333` in SQLite's `REAL` while MySQL's `decimal(10,2)` holds `333.33`. `immediateBaseUnitCost()` (`client/lib/db/procurement-receiving.ts`) was a **second copy** of the same division — `client/AGENTS.md` claimed the helper was the only place it happened — and it also missed the helper's null handling.
- **Fix:** the helper rounds its result through `roundMoney()`, and `immediateBaseUnitCost()` delegates to it instead of repeating the formula. One semantic change comes with that delegation, stated rather than buried: the old copy read a `null` override as `Math.max(0, Number(null))` — a stored cost of **zero** — where the helper reads `null` as "no override" and divides the bulk cost instead. `ImmediateLineItemDraft.cost_price_override` is typed `number | string | undefined`, so typed code cannot reach that path, but a row rehydrated from SQL could, and the new behaviour is the correct one. `undefined`, `""`, `0` and negatives behave identically before and after. Pinned by three cases in `client/__tests__/po-line-item-math.test.ts`. The regression test from A-260 could never have caught this: the value is a helper's return, not inline arithmetic, so a helper that returns a stored money value has to round inside itself.
- **What the original entry got wrong, corrected here rather than quietly dropped.** (1) It said the device "never matches the cloud for as long as that batch lives". It self-heals: the pull overwrites every column the server returns except `quantity` (`client/lib/db/sync-engine/pull.ts`), and a sync round pushes then pulls, so the receiving device's own row came back rounded in the same round. Exactly one device stock report ever carried the unrounded `value_sum`. (2) It said `sale_items.cost_price` snapshots the batch cost, so COGS differed between device and cloud. COGS snapshots the **averaged** `cost_price` from `getProductsWithStock()`, which is an unrounded division on every device — unrelated to this bug. (3) "A number an admin can see" was true for one report cycle, in sums rounded to whole units.
- **Why it was still worth fixing** rather than left as self-healing: it was a one-line change in a helper that was already the designated single source, it removed a transient divergence figure in the admin panel, and it let two false statements in `client/AGENTS.md` be corrected. ₦5 on a 1,000-unit carton was the worst realistic case, single-digit naira per store.
- **Why not a sub-kobo column scale** (the third option the entry offered): it would touch nine `decimal(n,2)` columns across both repos, the model casts and the sync engine, to buy precision no reader uses — every consumer traced (`stock-fingerprint.ts`'s checksum, COGS/margin, `getStockMoM`, the valuation report, stock-loss, the admin fleet/stock-value sums) displays or compares at 2dp, and none reconciles batch valuation against what was paid. What was paid lives on the purchase order (`total_amount`, `subtotal`, `amount_paid`, `supplier_payments`), so a batch valuing a ₦1,000 carton at ₦999.99 breaks no reconciliation a human performs.

### A-260 — twenty-three computed money writes stored unrounded floats, and the convention that forbids it had no enforcement
- **Found:** 2026-10-13, auditing numeric precision across the SQLite/MySQL boundary rather than from a reported symptom. Scope and severity below were both corrected by a Fable review pass before this entry was finalised; the first draft of it was wrong on the mechanism and understated the count.
