- **Was:** money is a `REAL` in `client/lib/db/schema.ts` (SQLite has no `DECIMAL` storage class) and a `decimal(n,2)` in MySQL, and the scheme only holds because nothing unrounded is persisted. `roundMoney()` was applied consistently in `lib/utils/pos-calculations.ts` and `queries/stock-transfers.ts` and nowhere enforced, so 23 computed writes had drifted from it: 11 in the db layer (`queries/inventory.ts` ×4, `procurement.ts` ×2, `procurement-receiving.ts` ×2, `queries/product-import.ts`, `queries/returns.ts`, `local-database.ts`) and 12 in the hook layer (`hooks/use-pos-cart.ts` ×4, `hooks/use-process-return-mutation.ts` ×3, `hooks/use-pos-payment.ts` ×3, `hooks/use-pos-held-transactions.ts`, `hooks/use-pos-prescription.ts`). One of the 12 rounded correctly but by inline `Math.round(x * 100) / 100` instead of the helper.
- **Actual severity, which is lower than first stated.** Verified that MySQL **rounds** an over-precise value to the column scale rather than truncating, and that `SyncController` does not cast or validate money fields on push. Every one of the 23 is a 2dp value times an integer, or a sum of 2dp values, so the error was a few ulps (~1e-13): the cloud row was always exactly right, the local row imperceptibly off, and no `SUM()` over a realistic row count could move a displayed cent. This was latent hygiene, not a live miscalculation, and the first draft of this entry claiming MySQL "truncates" and that the dust "accumulates in reports" was wrong on both counts. The genuinely material precision gap — an unrounded *division* into `cost_price`/`unit_cost` at receiving, worth ₦0.0033 per unit rather than 1e-13 — was **A-261**, fixed the same day and recorded in the entry above this one.
- **Fix:** all 23 wrapped in the existing `roundMoney()`, the inline one switched to it, and no new helper. No change to the shared `insert()`/`update()` path.
- **Why not round inside `insert()`/`update()` instead.** Money columns are not separable from the other `REAL` columns by type: `markup_percentage`, `tax_percentage`, `discount_percentage`, `points_multiplier`, `rating` and `loyalty_points_per_currency` are `REAL` too, and quantising them to two decimals would silently change stored values (a 33.333% markup becoming 33.33). Rounding therefore stays at the call site, where the author knows which kind of number it is. A per-column allowlist in the helpers is a legitimate alternative if the call sites keep drifting.
- **Why quantities were left alone.** `quantity` is `INTEGER` on both sides and exact in JS below 2^53, so it has no drift to fix. A fractional unit of measure — metres or litres for the construction-materials client in the pipeline — would break that assumption on both sides of the sync and needs its own design.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/money-write-rounding-convention.test.ts` walks `lib/db` and `lib/hooks` recursively and flags a write to a known money column whose value expression still contains arithmetic after every `roundMoney(...)` call is stripped from it. Eight detector cases pin the parsing, plus a guard asserting the file list is non-empty so it cannot pass vacuously. Confirmed RED on all 23 sites. Its first version was itself defective in three ways a review caught — deleting comments shifted reported line numbers, a `[^,\n]+` value capture cut `Math.max(0, a - b)` at its first comma and ran a parameter's `amount: number)` on into the rest of the file, and a substring check for `roundMoney(` accepted `roundMoney(a) * b` — so the parsing is now bracket-balanced and each of those is a pinned case.
- **Known blind spot, stated rather than papered over:** a text scan cannot see a money value computed into a variable or returned by a helper and then assigned. That is precisely how A-261 survives a green run, and extending the regex is not the way to catch it. Conventions written up in `client/AGENTS.md`.
- **Landed across two commits**, because this pass and the cycle-count batch were in the same working tree at the same time: the four `queries/inventory.ts` sites went in with `db6376d6` and the `client/AGENTS.md` write-up with `b3263bc7`, both already pushed; the remaining 19 sites and the regression test are in the commit that follows them. Nothing is missing — only the split is unusual.

### A-256 — a conflicted crash log rewound its own pull window, and the rewind's own crash report fed the next one
- **Found:** 2026-10-12 in production, immediately after a deploy. `DUMOSRX-CLIENT-26` reached **1,228 events and was escalating**, from one device, firing every two to four seconds. The issue had started on `products` that morning and migrated to **`feedback`**, which is the crash-log table.
- **Was:** a terminal `version_conflict` called `rewindPullWindow(f.table_name, …)` for **any** table (`push.ts:520`). For `feedback` that is both useless and self-feeding: useless because the client never reads its own crash logs back, and self-feeding because `restoreBaseline()` reports an exhausted budget by calling `logCrash()` (`pull-window.ts:46`) — which writes **another `feedback` row**, which is pushed, refused, rewinds, logs. Crash reporting generating the crash it reported, at the rate the sync loop runs.
- **Fix, in two halves — the first alone does not cure a stuck device.** (1) `TABLES_NEVER_PULLED` in `pull-window.ts`, currently `{feedback}`, skips the rewind on the push side. (2) **`restoreExhaustedPullWindows()` deletes the row instead of restoring it** for such a table. Without (2) the loop continues on any device already in the state: `RESTORE_SQL` does not reset `rewind_count`, that function runs at the start of **every** pull round, and `feedback` is absent from `pull()`'s table list (`SyncController.php:895`), so its window can never drain clean and `completeWindow` can never refresh its budget. The report would therefore fire for ever — and each report is itself a crash log, the row that caused the exhaustion. Deleting the row heals the device on its next round, with no migration.
- **What was deliberately NOT changed:** `RESTORE_SQL` still leaves `rewind_count` alone, and an exhausted *business* table still reports every round. That is A-229's intent and `pull-window-rewind-budget.test.ts` pins it: a device that genuinely cannot converge should keep saying so. The pathology is specific to a table that is never pulled. Deliberately narrow: the refusal still drops the queue row and still records the terminal conflict, and every business table keeps rewinding exactly as before, because for those the rewind is how a conflicted row gets reconciled.
- **Root cause, established on review (the third half of the fix).** A first guess — that the server remaps crash-log ids by fingerprint — was **wrong**: `findDuplicateInsertConflict()` (`SyncController.php:1620-1655`) handles only `users`, `categories` and `suppliers`. The real mechanism: `logCrash()` **coalesces** a repeat crash into the newest `_synced = 0` row of the same fingerprint via `update()` (`error-logger.ts:257-270`), and `update()` queues that row's *current local* `_version`. So the moment a crash row's local version diverges from the server's even once — a push the server committed whose response the client never applied, which the A-257 reload loop guaranteed by killing the in-flight push every ~3 seconds — that row becomes a permanent **sink**: every later crash of the same shape coalesces into it, queues an UPDATE carrying the stale version, and is refused again. Nothing can correct it, because `feedback` is never pulled.
- **So `feedback` joins `TERMINAL_CONFLICT_SETTLES_SOURCE_ROW`.** This is not a new policy: `reportStuckCrashLog()` (`base-helpers.ts:563-577`) already drops and settles a crash row after five failed attempts, on the stated grounds that "crash rows are already delivered to Sentry and to `/logs/client-error` the moment they are captured, so a crash row that the server has rejected five times is retried forever for no benefit". A terminal conflict is strictly more final than five retries. Without this, fix (1) removes only the amplifier and leaves the sink, which `requeueOrphanedRows()` re-pushes on every crash-launch.
- **Test moved rather than flipped:** `push-settles-conflicted-audit-logs.test.ts` used `feedback` as its generic "another table" example, asserting such a row stays unsynced. That example is now `products` — a table a pull genuinely can reconcile, which was the test's actual intent — and `feedback` has its own case asserting it settles and that `requeueOrphanedRows()` cannot resurrect it.
- **Regression coverage:** `push-never-rewinds-for-telemetry.test.ts` — a conflicted `feedback` row leaves its window and `rewind_count` untouched, a conflicted `products` row still nulls the window and spends a rewind (feedback case confirmed RED at `[null, 1]`). Plus two cases in `pull-window-rewind-budget.test.ts` — an exhausted `feedback` window is dropped and reports nothing, while an exhausted `products` window still reports on every round.

### A-257 — a stale chunk reloaded forever instead of once, because the guard was cleared on every boot
- **Found:** 2026-10-12, minutes after a deploy. One device reloaded `/inventory` every ~3 seconds indefinitely, on the **current** build, throwing `SyntaxError: Unexpected token '<'` each time — a chunk request answered with `index.html`.
- **Was:** `isChunkLoadError()` matched and `reloadOnChunkError()` reloaded, which is right: one reload fetches the new shell. But the guard lived in `sessionStorage` and `global-error-listener.tsx` **removed it on every mount**, on the reasoning that a clean boot proves the previous reload worked. It does not: the app can boot fine and then fail on a *route* chunk. So each reload cleared its own protection — boot, clear, navigate, fail, reload, boot, clear — and the one-time guard became a permanent loop. The crash screen, which is what should have appeared, was never reached.
- **Fix:** `shouldReloadForChunkError(buildSha)` in `chunk-error.ts` caps reloads at **one per deployed build**, in `localStorage` so it survives the reload. Keyed by build, so a genuine new deploy earns a fresh attempt; a storage failure permits the reload, because the reload is the remedy and a guard must never be the thing that blocks it. Both call sites use it — `global-error-listener.tsx` (dynamic-import rejection) and `error-boundary.tsx` (React render throw) — so they can no longer disagree.
- **Why per build and not per tab:** one reload is the entire remedy. If the same build fails again, reloading it cannot help, and the second failure is real and should be reported.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/chunk-reload-is-bounded-per-build.test.ts` — first reload allowed, second and third refused, a new build allowed again, and a throwing storage still permits the reload. All confirmed RED.
- **Still open:** why a chunk 404'd on a build whose own deploy reported success. `FTP-Deploy-Action` keeps a sync-state file on the server and only uploads what it believes changed, so a wrong state can leave one hashed chunk missing. Not established; the operator remedy is to delete `.ftp-deploy-sync-state.json` and re-run the client deploy, forcing a full upload.

### A-253 — onboarding told a real store owner she had no staff account, because the setup pull was narrowed to `stores` and never sent `users`
- **Found:** 2026-10-12, live, while onboarding a new customer. Cloud setup finished and showed *"Synchronization finished, but no staff accounts were found. Let's set up your local account."* Her server data was perfect — `role = store_owner`, `deleted_at` NULL, and `stores.user_id` pointing at her own id — and a fresh browser, so no stale bundle.
- **Was:** `isStoresOnlySetupOverridePull()` identified the client's privileged subscription-status pull by **shape alone**: `setup=1` plus a `last_synced` map naming only `stores`. Its own doc comment claimed that shape was unique to that call. It is not. `syncSubscriptionStatus()` finishes by stamping `_sync_state.stores` and nothing else (`sync-engine/index.ts:337`), and something always runs it before onboarding's main pull: the **new-account** path calls it directly (`app/setup/use-onboarding.ts:131`), while the **existing-account restore** path — which is the one that failed here — gets it from `StoreProvider`'s effect (`lib/context/store-context.tsx:470-506`), which re-fires when `setActiveStoreId()` changes the identity query key at `use-onboarding.ts:393`. So on a fresh device `stores` is the only stamped window, the main setup pull sends `setup=1` with `{stores: <timestamp>}`, the override fires, `$tables` collapses to `['stores']` (`SyncController.php:910`) and **`users` is never pulled**. `users` then "drains" with nothing, `onCriticalTablesReady` fires with success, and `getTotalUserCount()` (`queries/setup.ts:89`, unscoped) returns 0.
- **Why it was dangerous, not just wrong:** the message routes to registration, and that path inserts a new `users` row with a fresh UUID and `role='admin'` queued as an INSERT — so following the prompt would have created a **second admin identity** on the server for an owner who already had one, which is the duplicate-user shape behind several other defects in this file.
- **Fix:** the override now also requires an **empty** cursor — `($lastSyncedMap['stores'] ?? null) === ''`. The privileged call has sent the literal `{stores: ""}` since it was written (`126a7ec3`) and a real sync always sends a timestamp, so the two are now distinguishable by the one thing that actually differs. §11 checked before shipping: `git log -S` over that call shows `""` is the only form any bundle has ever sent, so no released client loses its gate bypass.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPullSetupOverrideTest.php` — a setup pull whose only stamped window is `stores` receives `users` (confirmed RED: it asserted the key was missing); the privileged `{stores: ""}` call stays narrowed to `stores` and gains neither `users` nor `products`; an ordinary non-setup sync is unaffected.
- **Corrected after the fact: the subscription was the blocker, and this defect's role in that one failure is unproven.** Extending her plan made cloud setup succeed **without this fix deployed** — which means the override did *not* fire on that attempt, so `last_synced` was not exactly `{stores: …}` that time. The stamp-before-pull ordering is therefore a **race**, not the deterministic chain first written here: `StoreProvider`'s effect re-fires on identity change and the stamp usually but not always lands before the main pull. What remains established is that the override **cannot** distinguish the two calls and **does** swallow `users` whenever the race goes the other way — pinned by a test, and worth fixing on its own. What is **not** established is that it caused her particular failure.
- **The gate she was actually behind.** Her `enterprise` subscription row read `status = active` with `end_date = 2026-10-01` — eleven days past — so `validateSync()` (`:2784`, `where('end_date','>',now())`) resolved her to the `free` tier, whose `cloud_sync` is false. The shape-only override had been *accidentally* carrying her past that gate (it widens `isSetup`, `:2841-2844`) while starving her of `users`. With this fix the gate applies properly, so a lapsed account now gets an honest `SYNC_DISABLED` instead of a misleading "no staff accounts". For her, extending the plan was **sufficient** — the fix was not deployed when her setup succeeded (see the correction above). The two are independent: this fix matters whenever the race leaves `stores` as the only stamped window, and the gate matters whenever a plan has lapsed. Worth remembering that a `status = active` row with a past `end_date` is invisible to the status check and reads as `free`.
- **Left alone deliberately:** `getTotalUserCount()`'s message is still reachable if a pull ever legitimately returns no users, and `syncSubscriptionStatus()` still stamps a window it arguably should not. Both are safe now that the override cannot swallow `users`; the stamp is worth revisiting if another table ever needs the same treatment.

# Fixed Bugs Log

A changelog of bugs that were tracked in `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` and have since been fixed. `KNOWN_BUGS.md` only ever holds *open* items — an entry is removed from it outright the moment it's fixed, not marked done in place — so this file is where the record of "what it was and when it got fixed" lives instead. Git history has the exact diffs; this is a scannable index into that history, one entry per fix, newest first.

## 2026-10-11

### A-237 — the sync indicator crashed the whole render tree on an unparseable last-sync timestamp
- **Was:** `client/components/dashboard/sync-indicator.tsx` called date-fns `formatDistanceToNow(new Date(lastSync))` raw in three places (the pending-sync tooltip, the sidebar "Last synced" label and the collapsed tooltip). date-fns throws `RangeError: Invalid time value` on an invalid date, and the first of those sits in the component's render body — so a `lastSync` value that does not parse took down the React tree rather than one label. Caught in Sentry as `DUMOSRX-CLIENT-24` (`fatal: true`, `/inventory/overview`, 2026-10-10), not by a test.
- **Fix:** `formatDistanceSafe()` added to `client/lib/utils/date-utils.ts` — the relative counterpart to the `formatDateSafe()` already there, and a copy of the one `web/` has had all along — returning a fallback for null, unparseable and invalid values instead of throwing. All three call sites now use it and the raw date-fns import is gone; `sync-indicator.tsx` was the only file in `client/` importing `formatDistanceToNow`, so the root cause is fixed once, in shared code.
- **Not changed:** `isSyncOverdue` (`:80`) does its own `new Date(lastSync).getTime()` comparison, which yields `NaN` and so reads as "not overdue" — wrong but not a crash, and treating an unreadable timestamp as not-overdue is the safe direction.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/date-utils.test.ts` — four `formatDistanceSafe` cases covering a real timestamp, an unparseable string, null, and a custom fallback.

### Merge conflict markers were committed into this file
`docs/FIXED_BUGS.md` carried a live `<<<<<<< HEAD` / `=======` / `>>>>>>> worktree-agent-a74af27faa2a029bf` block in `HEAD` on `dev`, from the 2026-10-10 batch. Both sides held distinct, wanted entries (A-226 on one side, A-229 and A-228 on the other), so the resolution kept all three, ordered by descending id to match the rest of the file. Noted here because a conflict marker in a doc survives every test suite — nothing reads this file — and the only thing that catches it is someone opening the file.

## 2026-10-10

### A-229 — the pull-window rewind budget's safety valve was unreachable from the paths that needed it
- **Was:** `RESTORE_SQL` ran only inside `rewindPullWindow()`, and both callers fire only on a live fault (`pull.ts`'s unresolved stock delta, `push.ts`'s terminal conflict). A device that rewound once or twice and then stopped hitting the fault kept `last_synced_at = NULL` and `rewound_from` set indefinitely — nothing else ever restored it, so it full-re-pulled that table every round, which is exactly the A-218 grind the budget exists to bound. `stock_batches` is the realistic victim: the re-stamp needs `!skippedTables.has(table)` and *any* pending `_sync_queue` row sets it, and `stock_batches` is queued by returns, procurement receiving, inventory adjustments and product import, so a busy pharmacy with one parked queue row never re-stamps it. Third, the "cannot converge" report fired once ever: the `logCrash` was guarded on `state?.rewound_from`, which `RESTORE_SQL` nulls without resetting `rewind_count`, so every later exhausted call took the `>= MAX` branch, found `rewound_from` null, and returned silently.
- **Fix:** `restoreExhaustedPullWindows()` runs unconditionally at the top of `pullChanges()` and restores the baseline of every table whose `rewind_count` has reached `MAX_PULL_WINDOW_REWINDS`, reporting each time. `rewind_count` is **not** reset by the restore — only a genuine skip-free drain (`PULL_PROGRESS.completeWindow`) clears it, so the budget stays bounded and a device that cannot converge re-reports once per round instead of once ever (`health-check.ts`'s "still logs every time either way" precedent). The restore is idempotent: `last_synced_at = COALESCE(rewound_from, last_synced_at)` with `rewound_from` nulled, so re-running it cannot lose a restored baseline, and `rewindPullWindow()`'s exhausted branch now shares the same helper rather than carrying its own guard.
- **Found by** an adversarial review of A-218, alongside A-228.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/pull-window-rewind-budget.test.ts` — "restores an exhausted baseline on a later round even though the fault stopped firing" (`restoreExhaustedPullWindows is not a function`, then the window stayed null), "reports again every round while the device still cannot converge" (`expected 1 to be 2` once the restore existed but still only logged on the first exhausted call), and "leaves a table inside its budget alone". `pull-resumes-across-rounds.test.ts` "puts a table's exhausted rewind baseline back before the round asks for anything" pins the call site: before it, the round's `last_synced` was `{}` — a pull from timestamp zero — instead of `{ products: "2026-08-15T00:00:00Z" }`.

### A-228 — the subscription sync reverted the pull-window rewind bookkeeping to its schema defaults
- **Was:** `syncSubscriptionStatus()` (`client/lib/db/sync-engine/index.ts`) stamped its stores window with `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO _sync_state (table_name, last_synced_at)`. `INSERT OR REPLACE` deletes the row and re-inserts it, so every unnamed column fell back to its schema default — `rewind_count` to `0` and `rewound_from` to `NULL`, the two columns A-218 had just added. `stores` does reach the rewind path (`push.ts` rewinds any terminal conflict whose reason is in `REASONS_NEEDING_REPULL`, and store-profile edits are pushed and version-checked), and this writer runs on its own schedule, so a `stores` rewind was routinely cancelled before the re-pull it exists to force ever happened — the conflicted row is never re-offered, which is the permanent divergence A-205 exists to prevent — and the rewind budget became unbounded, which is the grind A-218 exists to bound. Found by an adversarial review of A-218; the A-218 author never saw this writer.
- **Fix:** the stamp goes through `pull-window.ts`'s `stampPullWindowUnlessRewinding()` — a named upsert that touches `last_synced_at` only, with `WHERE rewind_count = 0` on the `DO UPDATE` so it also refuses to advance a window while a rewind is in flight. It lives beside the rewind it protects rather than in `index.ts`, which owns none of this state and was at the 350-line limit. Nothing else writes `_sync_state` outside `pull.ts`'s two `PULL_PROGRESS` upserts, `pull-window.ts` and `forceFullResync()`'s deliberate `DELETE`; all were checked.
- **Rule:** `INSERT OR REPLACE` is now called out in `client/AGENTS.md` under "Pull window rewinds" — a writer that owns one column of `_sync_state` must say so in SQL, because the table now carries state owned by someone else.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/sync-subscription-status.test.ts` "leaves an in-flight pull window rewind for stores alone" — failed before the fix with `rewind_count` 0 instead of 2, `rewound_from` null, and the window stamped over the rewind's NULL.

### A-226 — the A-214 repair command would have destroyed stock on exactly the batches A-214 hurt most
- **Was:** `HealthSyncReconciliationRepairService` rebuilt each batch from `SUM(all its movements) − SUM(the bad sync_reconciliation deltas)` (`:62`, mirrored in `writeBatchUnderLock()`). That equals the pre-incident quantity only if the movement log is complete and was never floored. Caught in review before the command was ever run against production; it was queued to run against a live pharmacy.
- **Two ways it was wrong, both silent:**
  - **The A-148 class** (batches whose opening stock never produced a movement row — still undissolved, `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` A-213). A batch holding **100 real units with no movement rows at all** is zeroed by the incident's one `−100` reconciliation. The repair then computed `logSum = −100`, `incidentDelta = −100`, `target = 0` and **wrote the zero straight back**. `target` is not `< 0`, so the `clamped` counter stayed 0 and nothing told the operator that batch needed a physical count — while `units_restored += −incidentDelta` reported **`+100` units restored** and the largest-corrections table printed `from: 0, to: 0, units: 100`.
  - **Any batch whose history was floored by an oversell.** Raw `SUM(quantity)` sits below the floored running balance the server actually maintained (A-217), so the target was systematically at or below the true pre-incident quantity and the repair wrote the batch **down**. The existing test covered only the `target < 0` sub-case; the `0 ≤ target < true` case had no coverage at all.
- **Fix — delta reversal, which is smaller than what it replaced:** `target = stored − incidentDelta`. The incident and every movement after it were both applied to the stored column, so `stored = pre_incident + incidentDelta + post_incident_deltas`, hence `stored − incidentDelta = pre_incident + post_incident_deltas` — the number the batch should hold. It undoes exactly the incident, preserves every post-incident sale, delivery and count that was not itself floored away (they are already inside `stored`, so they are not re-added — but a sale pushed against an already-zeroed batch was clamped to 0 by `applyStockBatchDeltas`, so it is absent from `stored` and the restored figure is high by exactly what was sold while the batch read zero; the per-movement floor makes the log replay agree with the over-count, so the auto-healer will never flag it, and only a physical count fixes it), and **cannot be wrong when the log is incomplete because it never reads the log**. This is the server-side twin of the client-side A-215 fix, where `client/lib/db/sync-engine/stock-integrity.ts` now refuses such batches as `unreconstructable`. The log sum is still read, but only to count `drifted` batches for the report.
- **Reporting fixed too, because that is how it stayed invisible:** `units_restored` is now `max(0, target) − stored` — the change actually written — per batch, per store and in the `largest` table. The old `−incidentDelta` described the size of the reversal, so a batch left exactly where it was still claimed units restored. A clamped batch now reports a negative figure, which is the truth. `explainArithmetic()` in the command was rewritten to match; a report describing arithmetic the code no longer uses is its own defect.
- **Existing tests, and what they said.** Three failed, all three for a reason worth recording. `it_keeps_movements_made_after_the_incident_and_still_lands_on_the_right_number` had a fixture that **never maintained `stock_batches.quantity`** — it left the batch at 0 while inserting post-incident movements that would each have moved the column — so it was asserting against a state the server cannot be in; it now maintains the column (and asserts `drifted === 0`, pinning that the two formulas *do* agree on a complete, unfloored log). `a_log_sum_below_zero_is_stored_clamped_and_reported` was unreachable for a *zeroing* incident under delta reversal — `stored ≥ 0` and `incidentDelta < 0` makes `target ≥ 0` always — so it became `a_reversal_larger_than_the_stored_quantity_is_clamped_and_reported`, driving the clamp with a positive bad delta. `a_quantity_that_moves_under_the_repair_is_skipped_rather_than_clobbered` expected `100` where the correct answer is `107`: the till's 7 units are in `stored`, and the old formula would have **discarded a real till push** because no movement row carried it.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/HealthSyncReconciliationRepairTest.php` — `an_a148_batch_with_no_movement_log_is_restored_not_left_at_zero` (`Failed asserting that 0 is identical to 100`) and `a_batch_whose_log_sum_sits_below_its_floored_balance_is_not_written_down` (`Failed asserting that 30 is identical to 50`), both failing before the fix. Suite: 1348 → 1350.
- **Deliberately not changed:** the three-layer floor divergence (client per-movement, `SyncController::applyStockBatchDeltas` per push delta, this repair once over the whole sum) stays tracked as A-217; the derived-quantity design resolves it and unifying it here was out of scope.

### A-225 — till-code generation wrote a column the host's schema did not have yet
- **Was:** `AdminTillCodeController::issue()` and `admin:till-code` both inserted `code_encrypted` unconditionally. On a host whose schema is behind the code — the permanent condition between an FTP deploy and someone running the migration, since `deploy-backend.yml` does not apply migrations (A-210) — the insert failed with `SQLSTATE[42S22]: Column not found` and a human clicking Generate got a hard 500. This is the same incident whose *rendering* A-224 fixed; A-224 stopped the exception being displayed, not the exception.
- **Cause:** the house pattern for this was already in the codebase and this path did not follow it. `SyncController::applyPullCursor()` and `stampSyncedAt()` probe `Schema::hasColumn()` before writing `_synced_at`/`store_id`, each with its own cache because the probe is a real INFORMATION_SCHEMA round trip; `DashboardService` and several admin services do the same.
- **Fix:** `AdminTillSessionService::newCodeAttributes($code, $adminId, $label)` builds the insert attributes and omits `code_encrypted` when a once-per-instance `Schema::hasColumn('admin_till_codes', 'code_encrypted')` says it is absent. Both writers now call it, so the duplicated literal array is gone and there is one place to delete when the shim retires. The code still issues with its bcrypt hash and still verifies; it is simply not recoverable afterwards, which `revealAll()` already reports as `null` for rows predating the column. The read path needed no change: `$row->code_encrypted` on a model whose table lacks the column is a missing attribute, so `decryptCode()`'s falsy check already covered it, and no query names the column in a column list.
- **Retirable when** `GET /health?schema=1` reports `true` on every environment, i.e. once `2026_10_10_000001_add_code_encrypted_to_admin_till_codes` has run everywhere.
- **Rule written down:** `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` → "A column a release adds must not be written unconditionally (A-225)", including the deliberate alternative (migration-only release first, code second) and when to prefer it over a probe.
- **Also checked:** every other migration in `main..dev` (170 commits) is a `Schema::create` — `device_stock_reports`, `device_queue_reports`, `sync_commands`, `admin_till_codes`/`admin_till_sessions`. A missing table has no graceful degradation, so the deploy gate is the only mitigation there; `code_encrypted` was the only added-column case.
- **Tests:** `AdminTillCodeApiTest::test_a_code_still_issues_when_the_encrypted_column_has_not_been_migrated_yet` and `AdminTillCodeCommandTest::test_the_command_still_issues_when_the_encrypted_column_is_missing` — both drop the column inside the in-memory SQLite database and both threw `QueryException` before the fix.

### A-224 — the admin panel rendered raw server exceptions, including a bcrypt hash and an encrypted credential
- **Was:** found by a browser smoke test on dev. Generating a till access code against a database missing `admin_till_codes.code_encrypted` rendered the whole `QueryException` message inside the Till Access card: the connection name, DB host, port and schema (`dumomvte_dumosrx_dev_db`), the full `INSERT` statement, the **bcrypt hash** of the code and its **encrypted value**. The panel was showing an operator the credential material the row was supposed to hide.
- **Cause:** A-207 correctly replaced an invisible failure with the server's own wording, and `base-client.ts`'s response interceptor promoted `error.response.data.message` onto `error.message` unconditionally. Every admin card renders `error.message`, so an unhandled 500 rendered verbatim. Going from invisible to unfiltered is not the fix.
- **Fix, at the shared layer so every card inherits it:** `presentableErrorMessage(status, data)` in `web/lib/api/base-client.ts` decides what a human may see. A 5xx (or a status-less response), a body carrying Laravel's debug keys (`exception`/`file`/`line`/`trace`), or a message matching `EXCEPTION_SHAPED_MESSAGE` (`SQLSTATE`, `(Connection:`, `SQL: insert|select|…`, a stack trace, a namespaced `\FooException`) all collapse to `SERVER_FAULT_MESSAGE`. A deliberate 4xx refusal — the 422 till-code cap, the 422 storefront-slug messages — still shows the server's own wording, which was the whole point of A-207. The interceptor keeps the **raw** message for the log buffer and the `/logs/client-error` report, so the detail is still diagnosable; only what reaches `error.message` is filtered.
- **Why not rely on `APP_DEBUG=false`:** that only swaps the disclosure for an unhelpful "Server Error", which is the A-207 failure again. The gate keys on status, not on body shape alone, so both environments produce the same actionable sentence. No server-side change was needed: `APP_DEBUG=true` on dev is deliberate and useful in the network tab, and production's `false` is a second layer, not the fix.
- **Second reader closed too:** `web/components/admin/views/email-templates-tab.tsx`'s `serverErrorMessage()` read `response.data.message` directly, bypassing the interceptor; it now asks `presentableErrorMessage()` and keeps its 422 `errors` preference ahead of it.
- **Tests:** `web/__tests__/admin-server-error-disclosure.test.tsx` — five of six assertions failed before the fix, including the real observed payload reaching `error.message` with `SQLSTATE`, the schema name and `$2y$12$` in it.

### A-210 — the deploy shipped migrations it never ran, and now refuses to call such a release done
- **Was:** `.github/workflows/deploy-backend.yml` is FTP-only, with no SSH step and no migration step, so code reached production and schema did not. `admin_till_codes` shipped with PR #149 on 2026-10-09 and its table was never created, so on-till inspection never worked in production; the same gap broke till-code generation on dev on 2026-10-10 against the `code_encrypted` column.
- **Already existed when this was fixed:** `AdminMaintenanceService::migrationStatus()`, `GET /admin/maintenance/migrations`, `POST /admin/maintenance/migrations/run` (both `role:super_admin`), the Operations "Database schema" card and the Maintenance "Pending migrations" panel. The capability was never the gap — **nothing told anyone to look.**
- **Fix:** `GET /api/v1/health?schema=1` reports `schema_current`, a single boolean, and the deploy workflow curls it immediately after the FTP sync and **fails the job** with a `::error::` naming Admin → Maintenance when it is anything but `true`. A deploy carrying an unapplied migration now goes red on the commit instead of waiting for a 500.
- **The deploy still does not run migrations, by decision.** Applying them automatically from CI against shared hosting is a real change in blast radius — an unattended `migrate --force` on a box with no SSH, no snapshot step and a destructive-pattern scanner that only "exists to make an operator look, never to certify safety". Detect-and-refuse keeps the human in the loop at the one moment they are guaranteed to be watching, and the apply button is two clicks away in Maintenance. `HealthSchemaGateTest::test_the_deploy_workflow_does_not_run_migrations_itself` pins that decision so it cannot be reversed silently.
- **Why opt-in, and why a bare boolean.** `/health` is the endpoint every till hits to anchor its clock (`client/lib/licensing/server-clock.ts`), and the comparison costs two queries plus a scan of 162 migration files, so it runs only behind `?schema=1`. It reports no names and no count: the endpoint is public, and a migration filename is a release note an attacker does not need. "Unreadable migrations table" reports `false`, not `true` — "don't know" must gate a deploy exactly like "behind".
- **Backward compatible (§11):** an added field on an opt-in query parameter. Every existing `/health` caller sees the response it always saw.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/HealthSchemaGateTest.php` — four of seven failed before the fix (the field did not exist and the workflow had no gate). Override the URL with the `API_HEALTH_URL` repository variable if the API ever moves.

### A-223 — an unparseable `last_sync_time` crashed every screen, and the crash screen's remedy wiped unsynced sales
- **Was:** `getLastSyncTime()` (`client/lib/storage-keys.ts`) returned whatever was in `localStorage` unvalidated, and `components/dashboard/sync-indicator.tsx:307`, `:472`, `:484` passed it to `formatDistanceToNow(new Date(lastSync))` guarded only by truthiness. date-fns throws `RangeError: Invalid time value` on an unparseable date, and `SyncIndicator` renders in the dashboard shell — so one bad stored value (`"undefined"` from a failed write, a truncated string, a value from another app version) was the full-screen error boundary on *every* page. `:80`'s `getTime()` comparison silently read `NaN`. Reproduced in the browser during the release smoke test.
- **Why it was severe:** the boundary offered **Reload Application** (useless — the value is in `localStorage` and survives) and **Reset App Data** as a visually equal sibling, behind a `window.confirm` that said "all local data" without saying that includes sales not yet pushed. A user following the only instruction that appeared to work destroyed their own revenue data.
- **Fix:** validated at the boundary, once. `getLastSyncTime()` returns `null` for any value failing `Number.isNaN(new Date(v).getTime())`, so all three date-fns sites, the overdue check, `use-count-freshness.ts`, `health-check.ts`, `data-settings.tsx` and `device-diagnostics.tsx` inherit the guard through null handling they already had. No call site changed.
- **Error boundary:** `components/tauri/error-boundary.tsx` — Reload is now the only button; "Reset App Data" is a small underlined link that reveals an in-page panel stating it deletes all data on the device *including sales, stock changes and counts not yet synced to the cloud*, with "Delete local data and reset" beside "Keep my data". The native `window.confirm` is gone (`.agents/AGENTS.md` §9); the panel is deliberately plain local state rather than the house `AlertDialog`, because this boundary must not pull a module graph back in after the app has already crashed. The escape hatch is intact, just no longer the obvious next click.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/last-sync-time-corrupt-value.test.tsx` (seven assertions failed before the fix; the render one reproduced `RangeError: Invalid time value` at `sync-indicator.tsx:307`) and `client/__tests__/error-boundary-reset-is-not-the-obvious-click.test.tsx` (three failed: the link was a bordered button, no cost statement, `window.confirm` called).
- **Checked and deliberately not changed:** the other stored timestamps named in the original entry are not exposed. `dumos_last_audit_log_prune` already rejects unparseable values via `Number.isNaN(Date.parse(...))` in `retention.ts`'s `isPruneDue()`. `dumos_last_sync_outcome`'s `at` reaches `diagnostics-detail-cards.tsx`'s own `ago()`, which returns "unknown" on `NaN`, and `diagnostics-report.ts` prints it as a raw string. Neither can throw, so neither got a redundant second guard.

### A-221 — the A-214 repair's reversal movements would have shown in the owner's stock history
- **Was:** `client/lib/db/movement-types.ts` defined only `RECONCILIATION_MOVEMENT_TYPE = "sync_reconciliation"`, and that one constant drove all three exclusions — `getStockMovements()` (`local-database.ts`), the dashboard recent-activity feed (`queries/reports.ts`) and per-product history (`queries/products.ts`). The repair command's `sync_reconciliation_reversal` type was in none of them, so the 146 rows reading "A-214 repair: reverses Health Sync `sync_reconciliation` movement &lt;uuid&gt;" would have appeared in the owner's movement list, dashboard feed and product histories — exactly what A-148's entry went to trouble to prevent, because staff read them as an accusation.
- **Fix:** `HIDDEN_MOVEMENT_TYPES` (both types) plus `HIDDEN_MOVEMENT_TYPES_SQL` (its placeholder list, so arity cannot drift) replace the single constant in all three queries. `getStockMoM()`'s explicit `IN (…)` allowlists already excluded it and were not touched; a test now pins that.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/sync-reconciliation-movement-visibility.test.ts` gains a `sync_reconciliation_reversal` block mirroring the existing `sync_reconciliation` one — three of its four assertions failed before the fix (`expected [ 'mv-reversal', 'mv-sale' ] to deeply equal [ 'mv-sale' ]` and the feed's `expected true to be false`).
- **Not fixable:** a bundle already in the field keeps the single-constant filter and will show these rows. Nothing can reach it; the repair's rows are correct either way.

### A-220 — the count-freshness warning would have fired on every till, on every count, for ever
- **Was:** `PeerSyncFreshnessService` listed peers from `user_devices`, which has no deactivation, retirement or `is_active` column (`2026_10_02_000005_create_user_devices_table`) — rows are only ever created or touched. A phone replaced three months ago keeps a frozen `last_synced_at` and reported as a stale peer indefinitely. Since `shouldWarn = isLocalStale || stalePeers.length > 0` (`use-count-freshness.ts`) gates the submit button (`stock-audits.tsx`), the banner and its "Submit anyway" checkbox would have appeared on every count on every till, permanently — and a guard everyone reflex-clicks is not a guard. One owner has 12 registered devices, most of them phones and laptops that will never sell anything.
- **Fix:** `PRESUMED_RETIRED_AFTER_DAYS = 7`. A device whose last sign of life — `last_synced_at`, or `created_at` for a row that has never synced — is older than the cutoff is rejected before the staleness filter. Justification for 7 days and why a cutoff rather than real retirement: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Peer sync freshness".
- **Found while fixing it:** every "not reported" case in `SyncPeerFreshnessTest` asserted `assertJson(['stale_devices' => []])`, which subset-matches and therefore cannot fail — all four passed against a response listing the device. They now assert the array itself, and a one-line note on the helper says why.
- **Tests:** `SyncPeerFreshnessTest` — a device last seen three months ago and a never-synced row registered three months ago are not reported (both failed before the fix once the assertion was made real: `Failed asserting that two arrays are identical`, with `DRX-OLD-PHONE` at `minutes_behind: 132480`), and a device three days behind still is, which pins the band between the two cutoffs.
- **Verified sound and left alone:** `groupBy('device_id')->sortByDesc('last_synced_at')->first()` correctly collapses the one-row-per-(user, device) shape A-202 creates.

### A-219 — a sale was not atomic, so a concurrent stock fold could lose its stock deduction
- **Was:** `use-pos-payment.ts` has no `transaction()` call at all, and `recordSaleItemStock()` decremented `stock_batches.quantity` per batch in one loop and inserted the matching `stock_movements` rows in a separate loop afterwards. Each write reserved and released its own slot on `core.ts`'s FIFO queue, so there was a real window in which the quantity was down and the movement row did not exist. A fold starting inside it reads the batch as diverged by exactly the sale quantity and writes the pre-sale number back; `recordSaleItemStock()` never re-applies the deduction, so the sale's deduction was lost locally until the next day's heal. The fold's own half of the guarantee (re-verifying inside its own transaction) was correct — the spec's failure-mode table claimed that settled the whole question, which it did not.
- **Fix:** the body moved to a private `writeSaleItemStock()` and `recordSaleItemStock()` now wraps it in one `transaction()`. A nested `transaction()` deadlocks against the outer call's queue slot (the `product-import.ts` hazard), so it runs inline when `isInTransaction()` — the same shape as `base-helpers.ts`'s `softDelete()`/`remove()`. Nothing else changed: the FEFO loops, the double-touch fallback and the per-batch `sale_item_batches`/`stock_movements` deferral behave exactly as before, now all-or-nothing.
- **Spec corrected:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-stock-integrity-fold-design.md`'s "Folding mid-sale clobbers a concurrent write" row says what the fold's transaction does and does not cover.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/record-sale-item-stock-atomicity.test.ts` — a failing `stock_movements` insert must leave the quantity untouched (failed before the fix with `expected 7 to be 10`: the deduction had already committed), plus the happy path and a caller that already holds a transaction, which pins the no-deadlock branch.

### A-218 — a device could end up re-pulling a whole table every round, for ever
- **Was:** both pull-window rewinds (`push.ts`'s A-205 drop and `pull.ts`'s A-176b stranded-delta reopen) did `DELETE FROM _sync_state WHERE table_name = ?`. `pullChanges()` builds `lastSyncedMap` only from rows that *have* a `last_synced_at`, so a deleted row means "pull this table from timestamp zero" — and the re-stamp is gated on `!skippedTables.has(table)`, which *any* row with a pending `_sync_queue` entry sets. A `forbidden` push failure parks a `products` queue row indefinitely on purpose (A-165), so after one ordinary two-till `version_conflict` rewound the window, every later round re-requested the entire catalogue, the parked row kept the table skipped, the stamp was never rewritten, and the baseline it could have fallen back on was gone. ~38 pages of full-catalogue pull per round, for ever, on shared hosting, on a till with a single serialized DB connection — the condition behind the "No products found" incident. Invisible to the row-count health check, because the counts match. A-176b had the same shape with no give-up counter at all: once a `_pending_stock_deltas` row passed `REPORT_AFTER_ATTEMPTS` for a batch the server will never send, `stock_batches`' window was wiped, then again every `REREPORT_EVERY_ATTEMPTS`, indefinitely.
- **Half of it was already understood:** `REASONS_NEEDING_REPULL`'s docblock excludes `permission_denied` because "a plan-gated queue would retrigger the rewind every single round". The symmetric case — a queued row stopping the rewind from ever *completing* — was not.
- **Fix:** one shared `rewindPullWindow(table, cause)` in `sync-engine/pull-window.ts`, used by both call sites. It stashes the pre-rewind stamp in a new `_sync_state.rewound_from` and nulls `last_synced_at` instead of dropping the row, counts consecutive rewinds in `_sync_state.rewind_count` (reset to zero by `PULL_PROGRESS.completeWindow`, so a table that drains skip-free gets its budget back and the ordinary A-205 case is unaffected), and after `MAX_PULL_WINDOW_REWINDS` = 3 restores the stashed baseline, refuses further rewinds and reports to Sentry under `area: "sync-pull-window"`. Worst case is three full-table pulls, then incremental pulls again — a device that cannot converge says so rather than grinding. **As shipped the bound was reachable around, and the restore was not reachable at all from the case it was written for — see A-228 and A-229 above**, which fixed a stray `INSERT OR REPLACE` that reverted the two new columns to their schema defaults, and moved the restore to the start of every pull round instead of only inside the fault-triggered rewind.
- **Why the budget and not the by-id re-send** both `shortcut:` comments name: there is still no by-id re-send endpoint on the sync API, so the targeted fix is a server change A-176 needs too. The budget is local, bounded and removes the unbounded behaviour today; the `shortcut:` on `rewindPullWindow()` now also names the condition for deleting the whole mechanism.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/pull-window-rewind-budget.test.ts` (first rewind, budget exhaustion restoring the baseline and reporting once, and the budget refreshing after a skip-free drain — the middle one failed before the fix because the module did not exist and the window was simply deleted). `push-version-conflict-handling.test.ts`'s A-205 assertion now checks the row survives with a null window instead of being gone.
- **Schema:** `_sync_state` gains `rewind_count` and `rewound_from` (`schema.ts` plus `schema-migrations.ts`'s `_sync_state` entry for existing devices). Local-only table, nothing to mirror server-side.

### A-215 — the automatic stock heal destroyed an A-148 legacy batch's unlogged opening stock as soon as one adjustment was logged against it
- **Was:** `stock-integrity.ts`'s classifier refused a batch as `unreconstructable` only when it had **no inbound movement at all** (`batchQuantity > 0 && inboundCount === 0`). The spec's failure-mode table claimed this covered "a batch whose opening stock was never logged". It did not. A pre-`a36b00e7` bulk-imported batch holding `quantity = 100` with no movement row (the A-148 class, still undissolved per `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` A-213) needed only one `+2` cycle count or Adjust Stock — `applyStockAdjustmentDelta()` targets the soonest-expiring existing batch — to become `diverged`, and the next 24-hourly `healStockIntegrity()` then wrote it to **2**. 100 real units destroyed, unattended, silently, with no undo and no blast-radius cap.
- **Not caught by the rollout gate.** The field measurement that cleared it (`checked 1952, diverged 255, unreconstructable 0, netUnitDelta 841`) is blind to this: such a batch counts in the 255, not the 0.
- **Fix:** `unreconstructable` is now a direction, not a movement count — a batch is refused whenever its stored quantity exceeds **the sum of its inbound movements**. Outbound movements only subtract, so no run of logged movements can leave a balance above their total inbound; a stored quantity above it provably contains stock that was never logged, and the replay cannot see it. The old no-movements case is subsumed (inbound total 0).
- **How the repairable case is distinguished from the destructive one:** an over-count the log *accounts for* — an outbound delta that never reached `quantity` (A-197/A-176's stranded deltas), or a per-movement floor making the stored value path-dependent — always leaves `stored <= total inbound`, and still folds. An over-count *larger than everything ever logged inbound* cannot be told apart from an A-148 surplus by any data on the device, so it is refused. That deliberately includes the 2026-10-08 "exactly twice the opening stock" shape (10 held against a single `+5`), which used to fold: a refused batch is a visible, repairable state and destroyed stock is not. Retirement condition in `client/AGENTS.md` — once Stage 4 `opening_quantity` puts every opening stock in the log, `stored > inbound` can only mean drift.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/stock-integrity-auto-heal.test.ts` "preserves an A-148 legacy batch's unlogged opening stock once an adjustment is logged against it" (failed before the fix with `expected 2 to be 102`), plus the refusal and the still-repairable over-count in `stock-integrity-fold.test.ts` / `stock-integrity-verify.test.ts`.

### A-216 — the Health Sync repair command's incident window had no lower bound, so `--apply` would have reversed the legitimate A-148 restoration
- **Was:** `HealthSyncReconciliationRepairService` selected incident rows with `movement_type = 'sync_reconciliation'` and `movement_date <= INCIDENT_THROUGH` ('2026-10-10 10:00:00') and nothing else, so **every `sync_reconciliation` movement ever written** was in scope — including the legitimate A-148 repair run of 2026-10-02 that restored a customer's bulk-imported opening stock (A-148 below) and any owner-pressed Health Sync before the incident. `--apply` against the live pharmacy would have reversed those and set those batches back to a figure excluding the restored stock. There was a test for the upper boundary and none for the lower one. A secondary hole: any of the 146 incident rows landing after 10:00:00 was silently out of scope, and the command would have reported "nothing to repair" for it.
- **Fix:** the scope is an explicit window `INCIDENT_FROM`–`INCIDENT_THROUGH` = `2026-10-10 08:53:00`–`08:54:00` on `COALESCE(created_at, movement_date)`, the run's own write time — the 146 rows all share `08:53:29`, and the minute's width only covers a run straddling a second boundary. Every run, report or apply, prints the window and warns how many un-reversed `sync_reconciliation` movements exist outside it with their earliest and latest write times, so the key can never silently miss rows. Preview stays read-only.
- **Tests:** `tests/Feature/HealthSyncReconciliationRepairTest` — "the legitimate a148 reconciliation run before the incident is not reversed" (failed before the fix: `1 is identical to 0`, and the batch went 500 → 0), "an incident movement written a second late is still in scope", and "the report names the window and the reconciliations it leaves alone".


### A-213 (partial) — Health Sync is refused outright, and every sync call now names its bundle
- **Was:** `POST /app/sync/reconcile-quantities` set a batch to the **device-reported** quantity and wrote the signed difference as a `sync_reconciliation` movement. Harmless-looking while quantity was a number; fatal once quantity means "the sum of the log", where that fabricated movement becomes permanent truth for every replica. It then did real damage the same day (A-214).
- **Fix:** the endpoint refuses unconditionally with **410** and `SyncController::HEALTH_SYNC_DISABLED_MESSAGE`, gated on `config/dumos.php`'s `health_sync_enabled` — hard `false`, deliberately with **no env override**, flipped on only by its own test suite so the reconciliation behaviour stays specified. A version gate was built first and discarded: usage data showed exactly one invocation per store in the product's lifetime, and that one was the incident, so nothing depends on it. The client control stays where it is and surfaces the server's wording in a 12-second toast (`hooks/use-settings-sync.ts`) — A-207's lesson: an invisible refusal costs more than the refusal. **(Superseded the same day — see the UI-retirement bullet below: the control itself is gone, so there is no longer a press to surface a refusal for.)** **`HEALTH_SYNC_DISABLED_MESSAGE` is read by a store owner on a till**, so it says only that the feature is retired, that nothing was sent or changed, and what to do instead; the incident, the units lost and the cause stay here, in `KNOWN_BUGS.md` and in the config comment, where the reader can use them.
- **Also shipped, independently useful:** `syncHeaders()` (`client/lib/api/client.ts`) now stamps `X-App-Version` and `X-Build-Sha` on every sync call. Before this the server could not tell which bundle was talking to it at all, which is why A-214's asserting device could not be identified after the fact.
- **Tests:** `tests/Feature/SyncReconcileQuantitiesTest` — refused with 410 and nothing written (batch, movements and activity log all unchanged), plus `health_sync_is_off_by_default` reading the shipped config file so a future edit cannot quietly re-enable it. `client/__tests__/sync-headers-carry-app-version.test.ts` pins the headers; `client/__tests__/reconcile-stock-quantities.test.ts` pins that the refusal reaches the caller.
- **No version floor exists, and a comment claimed one did.** `client/lib/api/client.ts` described `X-App-Version`/`X-Build-Sha` as "load-bearing: the server refuses Health Sync below a minimum version (A-213), and an absent header means 'too old'". The gate was discarded (above); the headers' only server-side reader is the activity-log property, and the refusal is unconditional. Corrected 2026-10-10 (A-222) — it mattered because it told the next agent there was a version floor to lean on.
- **UI control removed (2026-10-10, after a browser smoke test):** surfacing the refusal was the wrong half of the fix. The card still read "Makes sure this device is fully caught up and in agreement with the cloud… Good to run occasionally" and the confirm dialog "Fully syncs this device with the cloud, then double-checks every detail agrees… Takes a few seconds" — two confident descriptions and a confirmation step in front of a 410. The Health Sync card and dialog are out of `client/components/settings/data-settings-sync-maintenance.tsx` (Force Full Resync is all that remains there), and `handleReconcileStockQuantities` is out of `hooks/use-settings-sync.ts` and the prop chain through `data-settings.tsx` and `settings/[tab]/panels/data-panel.tsx`. `reconcileStockQuantities()`, the `window.__reconcileStockQuantities` DevTools hook and all their regression tests stay, deliberately — the endpoint still exists behind `health_sync_enabled`. Tests: `client/__tests__/health-sync-control-retired.test.tsx`; two assertions failed before (the card's text was still rendered, and the hook still closed over the handler).
- **A deliberate refusal is no longer reported as an error:** the 410 was logged at `console.error` and POSTed to `/logs/client-error`, so every press filed a Sentry issue for the endpoint working as designed. `UNREPORTED_STATUSES = {401, 410}` in `client/lib/api/base-client.ts` now skips both the report and the error-level log. Fixing it exposed a second, broader bug in the same `catch`: it decided whether an error had already been reported by string-matching `"HTTP error!"` on the message, so **every** HTTP error carrying a server `message` failed that match and was reported a second time as a status-0 network error. It now reads the `status` stamped on the thrown error. Test: a 410 POSTs nothing, a 500 still does.
- **Still open:** the device-quantity code itself remains behind the switch, and the A-148 class it exists for is undissolved — see `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` A-213 for the retirement condition (the spec's `opening_quantity` work).

### A-214 (partial) — Health Sync's pre-flight now proves completeness, not just a successful round, and the endpoint records who asserted
- **Was:** `reconcile-quantities.ts` ran a sync and, on success, asserted this device's quantities. A successful round is not a complete one: shortly after `forceFullResync()` batches arrive at 0 and their movements replay afterwards, so `Math.max(0, …)` sent those zeros up as confident truth. That removed 13,104 real units from a live store on 2026-10-10. Separately, `SyncController::reconcileQuantities()` received `X-Device-Id` and discarded it, so nothing recorded which device made the assertion.
- **Fix:** the pre-flight now also requires `movementLogIsComplete()` — the same stamped-window-with-no-leftover-cursor check `healStockIntegrity()` already used, now exported from `stock-auto-heal.ts` rather than re-implemented — and refuses with "This device is still rebuilding its stock history" while the `stock_movements` pull window is mid-stream. The reconciliation activity log now carries `device_id`, `device_label` and `app_version`. Both hold even if the endpoint is ever re-enabled, which is why they shipped despite the hard refusal above.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/reconcile-stock-quantities.test.ts` "refuses while the movement-log pull window is still mid-stream"; `tests/Feature/SyncReconcileQuantitiesTest::the_activity_log_records_which_device_asserted_the_quantities`.
- **Still open — and deliberately not touched here:** the 146 movements written by the incident are still in the log and need compensating movements, not deletion. `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` A-214 keeps that bullet.

### A-211 (partial) — a stock count taken on a device behind sync now says so, on both halves of the question
- **Was:** `submitStockAudit()` writes `countedQty - currentSystemQty`, a delta against whatever this device believes. Missing a sale of 2 from another till, a count of 8 against a displayed 10 writes −2, the sale then syncs for another −2, and the product settles at 6 with 8 on the shelf. The count made it worse, silently.
- **Fix, the cheap guard the entry called for, not the absolute-count model:** the cycle-count review step (`components/stock-batch/count-freshness-warning.tsx`) warns when this device's last **successful** sync is older than `COUNT_STALE_AFTER_MINUTES` (60) or has never happened, and when the server reports a sibling till in the same store that is behind by the same threshold. The submit button is held until the counter ticks "Submit anyway — I understand the correction may be off".
- **Why a warning and not a refusal.** Offline-first is non-negotiable: a till with no connectivity for two days must still be able to count its shelves, and a hard block would make the product useless exactly when a count is most needed. The acknowledgement makes the risk explicit instead of implicit.
- **Why 60 minutes.** The auto-sync interval is a store setting whose options are instant, 5, 15, 30, 60 and 360 minutes. Anything below 60 would warn constantly on a store set to 30; 360 would call a day-long gap fresh. 60 clears every common setting while keeping the unsynced window short enough that a missed sale is unlikely to be older than it.
- **The server half:** `GET /app/sync/peer-freshness` (`App\Services\Sync\PeerSyncFreshnessService`) reports which **other** devices in the caller's store are behind, from `user_devices.last_synced_at` — a question the counting device cannot answer about itself. Scoped exactly like a push (`resolvePushStoreId()`), the calling device excluded, newest row per device, a never-synced device reported with a null age. A warning, never a block; the query failing (offline) simply produces no peer warnings.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPeerFreshnessTest` (5 cases: a stale sibling, a fresh sibling and the caller itself, never-synced, another store's device, newest-row-wins) and `client/__tests__/count-freshness.test.ts` (never-synced and unparseable timestamps are stale, the threshold boundary, no negative ages).
- **Still open:** the delta itself. `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` A-211 keeps the absolute-count model and the two unwarned callers of `submitStockAudit()`.

### A-207 — generating a till access code "stopped working" in production, because every failure of that button was invisible
- **Reported as:** "generating a 12-digit till code didn't work any longer", with no error captured — because there was no error to capture.
- **Root cause, two halves, each enough on its own.** `TillCodesCard.handleIssue()` did `await issue.mutateAsync(...)` from an `onClick={() => void handleIssue()}` with **no catch and no error UI**: any rejection — the three-active-codes cap (422), a 403, an expired session, a network drop — became an unhandled promise rejection. The spinner stopped and nothing else changed, which is exactly "it didn't work". And the cap refusal was returned as `{"error": "..."}`, while `web/lib/api/base-client.ts`'s interceptor only promotes `data.message` onto the thrown error — so even a caller that *did* surface the server's wording would have shown axios's "Request failed with status code 422".
- **The cap itself is working as designed** (`EQUALIZED_CHECKS` bounds how many codes `verify()` will ever test, so a fourth could never authenticate). The bug was the silence, not the refusal.
- **Fix.** The controller returns the refusal under `message` (keeping `error` beside it for an older cached bundle), and the card catches the rejection and renders it in a `role="alert"` line above the cap note. The button stays disabled at three codes, so the refusal is now reachable only when the list query itself is wrong — and when it is, the operator finally sees why.
- **Tests.** `tests/Feature/Admin/AdminTillCodeApiTest::test_the_cap_refusal_carries_a_message_the_panel_can_display`, and `web/__tests__/admin-till-codes-card.test.tsx` "surfaces the server's reason when generating fails" (which reproduced the unhandled rejection before the fix).
- **Shipped with** super_admin code visibility; see `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`, "Till codes are recoverable by a super_admin, on purpose".
### A-197 — the deferred-delta drain ran per round, not per page, so a device ended up permanently over the server's stock
- **Was:** `applyDeferredStockDeltas()` ran only at the start of a pull round and after the whole page loop. A delta deferred on page 1 (its `stock_batches` row had not arrived yet) was therefore applied **after** page N's movements for that same batch. The floor is `MAX(0, quantity + delta)` *per movement*, so the order is not commutative: a batch's opening receipt deferred while its later sales applied against 0 left every sale clamped away and the device holding its full opening stock.
- **Confirmed live, 2026-10-10,** on the Agidi store: 994 units sold store-wide, the device reading **+823 over** the server; `PENTAZOCINE INJ` at its opening 80 where the server said 52, short by exactly its 28 sold. 255 of 1,952 batches diverged, 841 units overstated. Also reproduced by differential fuzzing — 34 of 300 randomised histories.
- **Fix:** the drain now also runs **inside each page transaction**, as soon as a page carrying `stock_batches` rows has applied them and before any other table of that page is touched, so every earlier delta lands ahead of the movements that depend on it. The flag survives the page boundary, because a page can carry batches and no movements. Two supporting details: the mid-round drain passes `countAttempts: false` (it runs several times a round and would otherwise race a long first sync to the chronic-reporting threshold while the batches were still arriving), and it discards deltas for movements the *same page* soft-deletes via the new `discardDeferredStockDeltas()` — it runs before those soft-deletes are written, and without this it applied a delta the server had voided, caught by an existing test rather than by inspection. The drain's own `SELECT` is now `ORDER BY rowid`, explicit about the order the non-commutativity depends on.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/pull-deferred-delta-order-across-pages.test.ts` — the real Pentazocine case folded to 52, plus a 60-case fuzz over randomised page splits, each checked against the server's own derivation. Both failed before the fix (the fuzz on 31 of 60).
- **The symptom was repairable before this, and the repair is now automatic.** `foldStockQuantities()` converges on the server's value, and the field run on `DRX-ZKI5K81UG` repaired all 255 and refused 0. This entry is the cause; the drift stops being created now.

### A-205 — a terminal push conflict dropped the queue row and left the device on the losing value forever
- **Was:** `push.ts` dropped the `_sync_queue` row on a terminal conflict and both the code comment and `client/AGENTS.md` asserted "the next pull brings the server's version down". It does not. Dropping the row changes nothing server-side, so nothing bumps that row's `updated_at`; once the device's pull watermark passes it — minutes of ordinary syncing — the incremental pull never offers the row again. The user had already been told the server's version was kept, which was true on the server and false on their screen.
- **Confirmed live:** a store's catalogue read "Uncategorized" on a till for four days after the A-189 repair had corrected all 2,495 products server-side, with `products.category_id` on the device still carrying the pre-repair value across repeated syncs.
- **Fix:** for the reasons where the server kept a row this device disagrees with (`REASONS_NEEDING_REPULL` — `version_conflict`, `stale_timestamp`) the drop now also deletes that table's `_sync_state` row, reopening its pull window so the server re-offers the row. `permission_denied` is deliberately excluded: no pull can settle it, and a plan-gated queue would rewind the window every round. Both misleading comments corrected, here and in `client/AGENTS.md`.
- **Known limit, labelled in the code:** the rewind re-pulls the whole table, not the one row, because the sync API still has no by-id re-send endpoint. Marked `shortcut:` in `push.ts` with that upgrade condition. It is bounded by how rare a terminal conflict is, it converges (after the re-pull the local row matches the server, so it cannot conflict again), and a re-pull is idempotent.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/push-version-conflict-handling.test.ts` — the conflicted table's window is rewound and no other table's is; a `permission_denied` drop rewinds nothing.

### A-203 — a bulk pass over the catalogue queued one uncollapsed `_sync_queue` row per product, delaying sales and blocking incoming pulls
- **Was:** `addToSyncQueue()` was a plain `INSERT`. A maintenance pass over the whole catalogue queued one row per product, and a second pass another — one device reported 4,350 rows, all pushing successfully. The queue is strictly FIFO and drains 50 at a time, so a sale rung behind that backlog waited ~87 rounds. **Worse, it blocked incoming updates:** `pull.ts` skips any row with a queued local edit, so a successful server-side category repair was invisible on a till for four days. It also swamped every queue-depth signal, hiding genuinely stuck rows.
- **Fix:** an `UPDATE` whose record has exactly one pending row, and that row an `UPDATE`, is collapsed — payloads **merged** (an `update()` payload carries only the fields that edit changed, so replacing would drop the earlier ones) and the row deleted and reinserted carrying its original `created_at`, `retry_count` and `next_retry_at`.
- **Why delete-and-reinsert rather than `audit_logs`' in-place rewrite.** `markSynced()` deletes the pushed row by id and flags the record `_synced = 1`. Rewriting the payload of a row already in flight would have the new edit deleted and the record marked synced without it — the silent divergence this area exists to prevent. A new row survives that race, and `remove()` already deletes pending rows mid-flight the same way. (The `audit_logs` path still carries that race; logged as A-209.)
- **The two complications the open entry named, both handled:** a pending `INSERT` is never collapsed onto, so it stays an `INSERT` and is never re-sent as one after landing; and `push.ts`'s backed-off-sibling hold-back is **not** deleted — old devices still carry multi-row backlogs, so push-time `coalescePendingUpdates()` and the hold-back remain live, and the two tests covering them now seed the sibling row directly because a current device no longer produces one.
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/sync-queue-collapses-updates.test.ts` (6 cases: field merge, different records untouched, pending `INSERT` left alone, pending `DELETE` not collapsed onto, backoff carried over, a whole-catalogue second pass staying at one row per record), plus the two adapted push tests.

### A-176b — a `stock_batches` row older than the pull cursor was never re-sent, so its deferred delta could never resolve
- **Was:** a pulled `stock_movements` row whose batch has not arrived defers its delta, correctly. But the pull is incremental: once the cursor passes a batch, it is only re-sent if updated again. A batch created long ago and never touched since never arrived, and its delta stayed pending forever, understating that batch's on-hand quantity indefinitely. The only way out was the operator knowing to run Settings → Data → Force Full Resync.
- **Fix, using machinery that already existed rather than the new API surface the open entry called for:** when the chronic-reporting threshold fires (`REPORT_AFTER_ATTEMPTS`, then every `REREPORT_EVERY_ATTEMPTS`), the pull also clears `_sync_state`'s `stock_batches` row, reopening that table's window so the server re-offers the stranded batch. Scoped to the one table that can settle the delta, rate-limited by the same threshold that reports it (roughly one re-pull per ten rounds, not per round), and safe because the pull strips `quantity` from a pulled batch anyway.
- **Known latency:** resolution takes ~10 sync rounds rather than being immediate, since `attempts` only increments once per round. That is the price of not adding a by-id endpoint; a targeted request would be instant and is still the better long-term shape (shared with A-205's `shortcut:`).
- **Tests:** `client/__tests__/pull-deferred-stock-delta-survives-failed-round.test.ts` — the window is rewound once a deferral goes chronic, only for `stock_batches`, and is left alone while the deferral is still young.

## 2026-10-09

### A-202 — every staff account read "Never synced" in the admin panel, because a sync was attributed to the account that linked the device rather than the staff member using it
- **Found:** 2026-10-09, while trying to identify which of one owner's devices held a store's overstated stock. Spotted by the owner, not by review: five demonstrably active staff all showing "Never synced".
- **Root cause.** `UserDeviceTracker::touch()` attributed the row to `$request->user()`, the bearer. The API token is minted once by `linkCloudAccount()` in `client/lib/context/auth-context.tsx:797` — the client's only `setToken()` call, and it refuses a second account outright. A staff PIN login authenticates against the device's own SQLite `users` table and never obtains a token, so the bearer is always the linking account (the owner). `user_devices` could therefore only ever hold owner rows, and `AdminUserDeviceService::getUserDevices($staffId)` returned `[]` **by construction** — the Store Staff list's "Never synced" and its `StaffDeviceHistory` drill-down were dead UI for every staff member of every store, permanently.
- **Fix.** The client stamps `X-Acting-User-Id` from `getStoredUser()?.id` alongside the existing device headers (`client/lib/api/client.ts`, now via one `syncHeaders()` helper instead of the same block copied into four call sites). `UserDeviceTracker::resolveActingUserId()` accepts it **only** for a user whose `store_id` already matches the caller's ownership-verified `$storeId`, and falls back to the bearer otherwise. The header is self-asserted, so it never reaches an authorization decision — which was already this table's documented contract.
- **Backward compatible by construction (§11).** A device on an older build sends no header and keeps the bearer attribution it has always had; no column changed, no backfill, nothing for an old client to misread. The shim can go when no build without the header is still syncing — i.e. never deliberately, since the fallback is also the correct behaviour for an owner login (an owner has no `store_id`, so they resolve to the bearer anyway).
- **Not a data-attribution bug, and must not be "fixed" as one.** Business rows already carry their own local actor (`performed_by` / `user_id`, written client-side; commit `bc0fc96e` stopped a pushed audit row being reattributed to whoever synced). Only this visibility table was affected.
- **Tests.** `tests/Feature/UserDeviceTrackingTest.php` — the staff member is credited over the bearer; a header-less client still credits the bearer; a user of another store is refused; an unknown id falls back; two staff on one terminal get two rows. The end-to-end leg (a sync with the header, then the admin staff list that reads it) is `tests/Feature/Admin/AdminUserSyncVisibilityTest.php`'s `a_sync_from_a_staff_login_clears_never_synced_on_the_staff_list`, added 2026-10-10 when the founder reported still seeing "Never synced": the fix verified end to end, the remaining explanation being a till on an older build or an owner (correctly) signed in at it.
- **Consequence for device identification:** `user_devices` could not previously tell you which terminal is on-site, and `user_devices.store_id` is only the device's *last active* store in any case. `device_stock_reports` remains the reliable signal for "which device holds this store's data".

## 2026-10-08

### A-184 — `DELETE /admin/users/{id}` deleted whatever id it was given, including the caller's own account and other platform accounts
- **Was:** the only friction on this destructive super_admin endpoint was a client-side dialog asking the admin to type the target's email. `AdminUserService::deleteUser()` itself had no guard: a super_admin could delete their own account (including as the last reachable super_admin) or another `super_admin`/`platform_admin`/`agent` account, through a direct API call or a UI bug, with no server-side check to catch it.
- **Fix:** `AdminUserService::assertDeletionAllowed()` now runs first inside `deleteUser()`'s transaction, before the target's stores are archived or the user row is touched, so a refused deletion leaves no partial side effect and writes no `USER_DELETION` activity log entry. It refuses outright when there is no authenticated actor (`Auth::id()` is `null`); when the target id equals `Auth::id()`; and when the target holds a platform role, checked against both the legacy `users.role` column and the resolved RBAC role (`userRole->slug`). The platform set is `self::platformRoleSlugs()` (shared with the profile-edit guards via the `UpdatesUserProfiles` trait) — `super_admin`/`platform_admin`/`agent` plus every custom (`is_system = false`) role, not a separate hardcoded list, since `AdminRoleService::createRole()` is the only creator of non-system roles and is itself super_admin-only. All three branches throw the existing `App\Exceptions\StoreActionBlockedException` — the same one `AdminStoreDeletionService` already throws for store archive/purge refusals — rather than a new exception type, so the two destructive admin flows share one error shape. `AdminUserController::deleteUser()` catches it and returns 422 with the message intact, mirroring `AdminStoreDeletionController`.
- **Ordinary deletions are unaffected.** A store owner and a staff account can still be deleted; staff own no stores, so the owner-stores archive loop never touches their employer's store.
- **Caught on review, not in the first pass:** the first version hardcoded a three-slug list and checked only the legacy `role` column — missing custom platform roles entirely, and missing any user whose `role` column disagreed with a `role_id` that resolved to a platform `Role`. It also had no test that isolated the self-deletion branch from the role branch; every test's acting admin had `role = 'super_admin'` literally, so the role branch always fired first and the self branch's own logic went unexercised, confirmed by disabling it and watching all tests still pass.
- **Regression coverage.** `tests/Feature/Admin/AdminUserDeletionGuardsTest.php`: self-deletion refused (including as the last super_admin, and in a case that isolates the self branch alone by giving the actor no platform role on either signal); every protected role refused, including a custom platform role and a legacy/resolved-role mismatch; deletion refused with no authenticated actor; a refusal writes no `USER_DELETION` audit entry; and an ordinary owner/staff deletion still succeeds.

## 2026-10-07

### A-181 — installing the admin panel from `/admin` on iOS installed the marketing PWA instead, which launches `app.dumosrx.com`
- **Reported as:** "we built PWA into admin, but when I do Add to Home Screen what gets added (`dumosrx.com/dashboard`) ends up opening `app.dumosrx.com`."
