- **Root cause.** `license-guard.tsx` built the lock-screen title from three independent `&&` expressions instead of one mutually exclusive chain. The third read `!(!clockTampered && isSuspended)`, which negates only the SECOND branch — so with `isClockTampered = true` it evaluated to `!(false && …)` → `true` and rendered alongside the first, concatenating into one string with no separator. The icon block directly above was a correct two-way and so looked fine, which is why this survived review.
- **Fix.** Both blocks are now single ternary chains, mutually exclusive by construction. No licensing decision changed: the guard's own comment notes the title is display copy only, and branching stays driven by `LicenseInfo.isSuspended` / `isClockTampered`.
- **Noted while fixing:** `"Subscription Expired"` is unreachable on this screen. The lock screen is only reached when `!isValid && (isClockTampered || isSuspended)`, because an ordinary expired subscription is deliberately downgraded to free and rendered through to the app. It is kept as a defensive default.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/license-guard-lock-screen-title.test.tsx` — three cases asserting exactly one title per state, including an explicit assertion that the concatenated string never appears.

### A-169 — crash `content` had no length cap at all, so the crashes that most needed reporting were the ones that could never be delivered
- **Found:** live, in Sentry — several `Sync item stuck after 5 attempts on feedback/<id>: SQLSTATE[22001]: String data, right truncated: 1406 Data too long for column 'content'` issues against production, most recently 2026-10-06.
- **Root cause.** A-149 capped `fingerprint` and `MAX_CRASH_MESSAGE_LENGTH` capped the message, but `logCrash()` built `content` by interpolating the **raw** `stack` and a **raw** `JSON.stringify(context)`. The server's `feedback.content` is a MySQL `TEXT` column: 65,535 **bytes**. A stack-overflow crash (`RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded`) carries a stack far past that, as does any context holding a payload. The push failed, the item retried to its 5-attempt ceiling, and the report was never delivered — so the most severe class of crash was precisely the class that could not be reported. The one metric that would have shown it (`feedback` push failures) was itself the thing failing.
- **Why the fingerprint fix did not cover it.** A-149 reasoned about one column and widened it server-side. `content` is a different column with a different limit, and a byte limit rather than a character one, so nothing about that fix generalised.
- **Fix.** `MAX_CRASH_CONTENT_LENGTH` (16,000), `MAX_CRASH_STACK_LENGTH` (8,000) and `MAX_CRASH_CONTEXT_LENGTH` (2,000) in `client/lib/utils/error-truncation.ts`, applied by a new `buildCrashContent()` in `error-logger.ts`. The character cap is a **quarter** of the byte limit, because a character costs up to 4 bytes in `utf8mb4` and a JavaScript string's `length` says nothing about its encoded size — any cap above 16,383 fails for some input. Per-part caps exist so one oversized part cannot crowd the rest out of the report. Truncating client-side was chosen over widening the column to `MEDIUMTEXT`: a 64KB stack is not worth storing or syncing, and widening would still have left a limit to overflow.
- **A second defect in the first draft of this fix.** Capping the built content then appending the coalescing branch's `(Repeated N times, most recently …)` line put the row back over the cap, and truncating afterwards silently ate the occurrence line — SF-CRASH-1's growth shape in miniature. `withOccurrenceLine()` now reserves the line's budget before truncating, so the count survives on an arbitrarily deep stack.
- **New helper, deliberately distinct from `truncateForLog()`.** That function appends the marker *past* `maxLength`, so its result is `maxLength + 12` — fine for an embedded excerpt, wrong for a fixed-width column. `truncateToLimit()` accounts for the marker inside the budget, so its output can go straight into one.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/crash-report-dedup.test.ts` — a 5,000-frame stack plus a 40,000-character context stays under the cap and under 65,535 encoded bytes; the message and the top stack frame survive truncation (a truncated report must still be diagnosable, not a stub); and three repeats of a deep crash stay capped *and* keep their `(Repeated 3 times` line. Confirmed RED before the fix.

### A-174 — the `migrate-db` endpoint reseeded production on every call and reported its own failures as HTTP 200
- **Found:** 2026-10-07, while deciding whether CI could call this endpoint to close A-170.
- **Root cause.** `routes/web.php`'s `GET /migrate-db` ran `migrate --seed --force`, so `DatabaseSeeder` — which *creates a super-admin account* — fired on every production migration; its `catch` returned the string `'Error: …'` with a **200** status, so any caller checking the status code was told a failed migration had succeeded; and the shared secret rode in a query string, landing in the host's access logs and browser history.
- **Fix.** Deleted outright and replaced by the Phase 5 Maintenance page: `POST /api/v1/admin/maintenance/migrations/run` (`role:super_admin`) → `AdminMaintenanceService::runPendingMigrations()`, which runs `migrate --force` and nothing else, returns a non-2xx on failure, caps the migrator output, and writes a `MIGRATIONS_RUN` activity-log entry on **both** success and failure. `MIGRATE_DB_KEY` is gone from `config/app.php` and `.env.example`.
- **The regression this fix nearly introduced.** Dropping `--seed` would have silently ended the only path by which a newly declared permission reached production, since `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` ran on every old `/migrate-db` hit — `DatabaseSeeder`'s own comments depend on that. Rather than restore the bundling, roles/permissions got their own separately-confirmed action (`POST /maintenance/roles/sync` → `db:seed --class=RolesAndPermissionsSeeder --force`, audited `ROLES_PERMISSIONS_SYNCED`) that invokes that seeder alone and never `DatabaseSeeder`. `SeederIdempotencyTest`'s and `DatabaseSeederTest`'s header comments, which documented the old path as production's, were rewritten in the same change.
- **A second bug this fix shipped green, caught only by a browser smoke test.** `AdminMaintenanceService` originally constructor-injected `Illuminate\Database\Migrations\Migrator`. Every one of the 16 backend tests passed, and the page 500'd on the first real page load: `MigrationServiceProvider` is **deferred**, so auto-wiring the concrete class fails on the unbound `MigrationRepositoryInterface` — but PHPUnit boots Artisan, which registers that provider, so the harness could not reproduce production. Fixed by resolving through `app('migrator')`, guarded by a source-level assertion (`AdminMaintenanceStatusTest::test_the_migrator_is_resolved_by_alias_not_constructor_injected`) for the same reason `PaymentProviderTimeoutTest` is written that way. This is `.agents/AGENTS.md` §9's "backend verification isn't UI verification" exactly as described.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/Admin/AdminMaintenanceEndpointGateTest.php` (super_admin-only on all three endpoints for `platform_admin`/`agent`/a custom `manage_platform` role, with the custom-role test asserting it clears the outer group gate first; `/migrate-db` now 404s), `AdminMaintenanceRunTest.php` (the invocation asserted to be `migrate --force` with no `--seed`; the roles sync asserted to invoke `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` and never `DatabaseSeeder`; audit entries on both success and failure — the failure case confirmed RED by returning early from the `catch`), `AdminMaintenanceStatusTest.php` (unknown ≠ zero pending, destructive detection, the alias guard — three confirmed RED by reverting the implementation). Verified end to end in a browser against a local API: 3 pending migrations listed, applied, output and applied-list rendered, pending list re-read to empty, `MIGRATIONS_RUN` logged with the actor; and a `platform_admin` deep-linking to `/admin/maintenance` got the refusal with **zero** requests to the endpoint.

### A-170 — production's schema sat behind `main` for weeks because nothing in the pipeline ran migrations
- **Found:** 2026-10-07, triaging Sentry: `Unknown column 'occurrence_count'` (567 events, 4 users) plus the A-149 `fingerprint` 1406 family, all still firing although their migrations were on `origin/main`.
- **Root cause.** The backend deploys by FTP, so the workflow uploads files and stops; production's schema only advanced when somebody manually hit `GET /migrate-db?key=…`. Nobody did. `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` had documented this exact mechanism, and warned "don't assume 'I wrote the migration' means 'it's live'", the whole time — the gap survived being correctly documented, which is why the fix had to be a mechanism rather than a reminder.
- **Fix.** Phase 5's Maintenance page (`/admin/maintenance`) lists pending migrations, flags the ones that alter existing data, and applies them with an audit trail, plus a "migrations pending" card on `/admin/operations` so drift is visible without being asked for. **Production was then migrated by the owner and reports zero pending (confirmed 2026-10-07).** The `GET /migrate-db` route is gone (A-174).
- **Still true, and worth carrying forward:** the schema being fixed does **not** un-stick rows already parked. A sync item that hit the client's 5-attempt ceiling stops retrying, so reports rejected while the column was narrow stay undelivered even now that the server would accept them.

### PG-18 — a store that synced nothing contributed nothing, so the platform success rate read 100%
- **Found:** code review of admin Phase 2; fixed 2026-10-07 as Phase 2 of the stuck-data work.
- **Root cause.** `SyncController::push()` has two exits that return before the recording hook, which sits after the outer commit: `validateSync()` refusing outright (plan lost `cloud_sync`, interval throttle, store limit), and the outer `catch` returning 500. Neither tallied anything. A store whose every push was refused therefore produced **zero** rows in `sync_health_daily` — and a success rate computed over "pushes we heard about" read 100% while that store synced nothing at all. These are failures that reached the server and were lost there, which is exactly what an operator checking a sync metric is looking for.
- **Fix.** `SyncFailureRecorder::recordRejectedPush()` records the whole attempt as one refusal — a `sync_failures` row carrying *why*, plus a tally with `pushes = 1` and the change count as refused. Called from both exits. Four whole-request reasons joined the canonical list (`sync_disabled`, `sync_throttled`, `store_limit_exceeded`, `server_error`) so these stay out of the unmapped-reason fallback.
- **The outer catch was also widened from `\Exception` to `\Throwable`.** An `\Error` — a TypeError in a payload handler, say — was not caught at all, so the request died with neither a rollback nor any record. That is the exact shape PG-18 described as "every push dies on an `\Error`".
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushRefusedAttemptIsRecordedTest.php` — a plan refusal is tallied as an attempt rather than vanishing, its reason is recorded against the store, and a push that does reach the hook is still counted exactly once (not double-counted by the new path). Confirmed RED on the first two.

### A-176a — a soft-deleted product's movements were pulled without its batches, stranding their deltas forever
- **Found:** 2026-10-07, in review of the A-173 work; one of the mechanisms behind a single store reporting different stock on different devices.
- **Root cause.** `pull()` sends soft-deleted rows (the outer query uses `withTrashed()` for any soft-deletable model) and scopes `stock_movements` by `store_id`, so a discontinued product's movements were always delivered. But `applyPullTenantScope()` scoped `stock_batches` through `Product::query()`, which carries Product's soft-delete global scope — so the batches those movements reference were silently withheld. The client defers a movement's delta until its batch arrives; for these it never arrived and never could, because `forceFullResync()` re-runs the same scoping. That device's on-hand quantity stayed understated indefinitely.
- **Why it looked deliberate.** A long comment on `counts()` explained that the pull-side subquery excludes these batches and that counting them would make a device look permanently "behind". That reasoning was sound about *consistency* but took the exclusion itself as given. Discontinuing a product is routine, and the client was already receiving the product and its movements — withholding only the batches left it holding an incoherent subset.
- **Fix.** Both the pull scope and `counts()`'s mirror of it now use `Product::withTrashed()`. The invariant the comment protects — counts must equal what pull delivers — is preserved; both sides simply include the orphaned batches now.
- **Deploying this does not retroactively fix already-synced devices.** The incremental pull filters on `updated_at`/`_synced_at > last_synced`, and these orphan batches carry old timestamps, so they do not arrive from the deploy alone. A device picks them up on its next **full** resync — which `client/lib/db/sync-engine/health-check.ts` now triggers automatically within ~24h, because the server count includes these batches while the device's local count does not, registering as a deficit. Settings > Data > "Force full resync" does it immediately. Until then the stuck deltas stay stuck, so expect divergence to resolve over a day rather than at deploy time.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPullSoftDeletedProductBatchesTest.php` — the batch is delivered alongside its movement, the product itself is still delivered, and the health count still agrees with the pull. Confirmed RED on the first of those before the fix. `SyncCountsTest::test_stock_batches_count_includes_batches_of_a_soft_deleted_product` was updated from asserting 1 to asserting 2, with its rationale corrected rather than silently flipped.

### A-173 — a deferred stock delta that never resolves is why one store's quantity differs across devices
- **Found:** 2026-10-07 in Sentry (`DUMOSRX-CLIENT-1Z`, 39 events in one burst, escalating), and connected by the owner to **stock values varying across 5 devices for a single store**.
- **The open question, answered.** The entry asked whether an unresolved delta is retried or dropped. It is **retried and never dropped**: the row stays in `_pending_stock_deltas` until its batch arrives. So on-hand stock is *incomplete*, never silently corrupted — the feared outcome was not happening.
- **What is actually wrong, and why devices diverge.** `pull.ts` **deletes `quantity` from every pulled `stock_batches` snapshot** (deliberate, documented: a pulled value is only as current as the movements the server had processed, and writing it clobbers real local state). Local quantity is therefore a purely local accumulation of movement deltas. A delta whose batch never arrives — a batch older than the pull cursor is never re-sent — leaves that device permanently understated, and **nothing corrects it**, because the authoritative server value is discarded on every pull. Five devices accumulate independently; five numbers.
- **A corruption path that was proposed, and does not exist.** The first fix added a guard refusing "Health Sync" while any delta was pending, on the theory that the device would otherwise assert understated quantities over the server's. Review showed that cannot happen: a delta is pending only while its batch row is **absent locally** — that absence is what defers it — and an absent batch is not in the payload the repair sends, which the server only applies to batches it receives. The guard protected nothing while refusing repairs, permanently for any device whose pull scoping means the batch never arrives. **It was removed**, and the test rewritten to pin the real property: a batch with an unapplied delta is excluded from the payload by construction.
- **Fix.** `applyDeferredStockDeltas()` re-reports a chronic delta every 25 attempts after the first report at 10. The old `attempts === REPORT_AFTER_ATTEMPTS` strict equality reported exactly once and then went silent forever, so a permanent divergence looked like a blip that had fixed itself.
- **Not fixed, and logged as A-176:** the root cause — a batch older than the pull cursor is never re-sent, so some deltas can never resolve. Closing that needs a targeted by-id backfill, which the sync API has no endpoint for.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/deferred-stock-delta-chronic-reporting.test.ts` (pending-not-discarded; re-reports more than once; does not report every attempt; still applies the moment the batch arrives), confirmed RED first, and `client/__tests__/reconcile-proceeds-with-pending-deltas.test.ts`, which now asserts the repair still runs and that the pending delta's batch is absent from the payload.

### A-206 — a non-JSON 2xx from the sync API was reported without the one detail that identified it
- **Was:** `parseJsonResponse()` named the endpoint, status and content-type and stopped there, by design: A-171's entry recorded that a body excerpt was impossible because `.json()` had already consumed the body. True of that approach, but the body is recoverable by reading `response.text()` **once** and parsing the string, which never needs `clone()`. Without it the report could not separate the three faults that produce this on shared hosting — a body truncated mid-stream, a valid body with PHP output appended, and an HTML page served as 200. The live events carried `content-type: application/json`, which ruled out the third and left two indistinguishable.
- **Why it mattered:** the throw aborts the whole pull round, and `pull.ts` persists progress only inside a committed page transaction — so a device whose first full page never parsed made **zero** forward progress every round. On 2026-10-12 this is what stranded `DRX-BKDBKL4Q5`: the health check found it 30 sales behind but syncing, fired `forceFullResync()` (its only recovery action), and the resulting full pull died on this error twice, leaving a till that could not pull at all. See A-243, fixed alongside, for the half of that story this error triggered.
- **Fix:** read the body as text once and `JSON.parse` the string. The error now carries the body's length, its first and last 120 characters (JSON-stringified, so whitespace and the truncation point are visible) and **the failure's own `name`** — the field that actually discriminates: a `SyntaxError` is a real parse failure of a body that arrived, a `TypeError` means the stream was cut after the headers (a mobile network drop or a proxy idle timeout), and those two call for opposite investigations. A body that cannot be read as text still produces the same error with `body unreadable`, so the handler can never throw out of itself and lose the endpoint. Rationale recorded in `client/AGENTS.md`, "Non-JSON 2xx responses".
- **Regression coverage:** two cases in `client/__tests__/base-client-html-response.test.ts` — a truncated body reports its byte count, its ends and `SyntaxError`; an unreadable body still names the endpoint and reports `TypeError`. Both confirmed RED. The three existing cases (bare SyntaxError not surfaced, endpoint and status named, normal JSON still parses) stayed green, which is what proved the single-read rewrite did not change the happy path.
- **ANSWERED by the first field event, 2026-10-13 11:06Z.** `DRX-8DY6G5XH8`, build `9f937f1e`: *"Expected JSON from …/sync/pull but got a non-JSON 200 response (content-type: application/json, **TypeError, body unreadable**)"*. `TypeError` rather than `SyntaxError`, and `response.text()` itself threw — so **the body stream was cut after the headers arrived**. That is a transport abort, and it **rules out both hypotheses this entry was written around**: there was no truncated body to measure and no appended PHP output, because there was no readable body at all. The shared-host `memory_limit`/`max_execution_time` theory is dead.
- **So the follow-up is not what this entry assumed.** "Retry the page at a smaller size" addresses truncation and would not help a cut stream; the fault is at the network or proxy layer — a flaky mobile connection, or a host/proxy idle timeout on a long-running large response. The useful next step is making the pull **survive** it: treat a `TypeError` from the body read as a retryable transport failure for that page rather than a fatal round, since the round currently dies before page 1 commits (which is what A-243 exists to limit the damage of).
- **Method note worth keeping:** one line of extra diagnostic output settled a question that two days of reasoning could not. `err.name` was the field that did it.

### A-243 — `forceFullResync()` destroyed a working pull baseline before proving it could replace one
- **Was:** `forceFullResync()` did `DELETE FROM _sync_state` and then pulled (`sync-engine/index.ts:60-61`). `pull.ts` persists progress only inside a committed page transaction (`:535` `savePosition`, `:546` `completeWindow`), and A-206's throw happens on the fetch at `:215` — before page 1 commits. So a resync whose first page never landed left the device with **no window and no cursor**, turning every later ordinary `sync()` into a full pull that failed the same way. There was no `rewound_from` to fall back on (that belongs to `pull-window.ts`'s rewinds, not to this delete), and `MAX_NON_IMPROVING_RESYNCS` only stops further resyncs, restoring nothing. It also disabled the stock auto-healer on that device permanently, since `movementLogIsComplete()` needs a stamped `stock_movements` window.
- **Why it mattered in the field:** on 2026-10-12 the health check found `DRX-BKDBKL4Q5` 30 sales behind **but syncing** (the check only runs after a successful round, `store-context.tsx:430-437`), fired this function as its one recovery action, and the resulting full pull died twice on A-206 — converting a slightly-behind till into one that could not pull at all. `docs/FIXED_BUGS.md` had previously ruled the delete acceptable because a resync is resumable across rounds; that holds only once a page has committed and was silent on a page that never does.
- **Fix:** `clearPullWindows()` and `restorePullWindows()` in `pull-window.ts` — the module that already owns every write to `_sync_state` outside pull.ts's own progress upserts. `forceFullResync()` stashes what it clears and restores it in a `finally`, so a resync that throws is covered too. The restore is conditional on `_sync_state` still being **empty**: any row means a page committed and its fresh positions must win, so partial progress is never rolled back. `rewind_count` and `rewound_from` are carried through the stash, so a resync does not silently hand a device a fresh rewind budget it had already spent.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/force-full-resync-restores-baseline.test.ts` — clears and hands back the stash; restores when nothing committed; declines when a page did commit; and no-ops when there was no baseline to protect (so an empty row can never be resurrected). Confirmed RED before the helpers existed.

### A-248 (part b) — the legacy store_id backfill claimed rows for an arbitrary store on a two-store device
- **Was:** `backfillStoreIdOnLegacyRows()` ran on every boot and stamped every NULL `store_id` with `(SELECT id FROM stores LIMIT 1)` — **no `ORDER BY`** — so on a device holding two stores SQLite's choice was arbitrary, silently, across every `STORE_SCOPED_TABLES` entry. Its comment said "this device's one pre-migration store", true when written and false from the day an owner had a second store. Reproduced in a test: with two stores present a legacy product was claimed by whichever row came first.
- **Fix:** the backfill reads `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM stores` first and returns unless the answer is exactly 1. A device that cannot know which store a legacy row belongs to leaves it NULL rather than guessing. The `LIMIT 1` is gone from the UPDATE as well, since with one store it was never doing anything.
- **The rule is coarser than it reads, found in review 2026-10-12.** `stores` is pulled **account-wide** on every device (`SyncController.php:1104`, `whereIn('id', $ownedStoreIds)`, deliberately not narrowed by `X-Store-Id` because it is the store switcher's discovery list). So every till of a two-store owner holds two `stores` rows even if it only ever served one — and `COUNT(*) FROM stores = 1` is therefore false on **all** of that owner's devices, not just the ones that switched. The backfill consequently never runs for a multi-store account, leaving legacy rows NULL on tills that would have been safe to attribute. That is the conservative direction (see the next bullet) so it is not a defect, but "exactly one store on this device" is not the same test as "this device serves one store", and anything that needs the latter must read the active store instead.
- **Why leaving it NULL is the safer failure:** a NULL `store_id` row is invisible to store-scoped reads, which is a visible absence an owner can report; a wrongly-claimed row is silent corruption that later moves a product between tenants — the defect this is part of.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/store-id-backfill-two-stores.test.ts` — one store claims its legacy rows, two stores leave them alone. The two-store case was confirmed RED (it returned `store-a`).
- **Not fixed here:** A-248's other client path, the `update()`/`softDelete()` claim that stamps the *active* store into a NULL row and its payload. Still open in `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md`; that is the one that moved two products in production.

### A-251 — the Settings diagnostics console had no nav entry, so it was unreachable by clicking in the very sessions that gate it
- **Was:** `settings-client.tsx:243` rendered `<TabsContent value="diagnostics">` for a till-inspection or impersonation session, and `diagnostics` was in `ALL_SETTINGS_TABS` with `canAccessSettingsTab()` granting it to both — but **neither** nav list had an item for it, so no `<TabsTrigger>` with that value ever rendered. The label was even present in the title map, which is what made the omission easy to miss. The only way in was knowing to type `/settings/diagnostics`; support reached for it mid-incident, found nothing in the rail, and concluded the feature was broken. §9's named failure exactly: the session gate was verified, the nav's visibility list was not. `/inspect` worked because it mounts the panel directly, so the feature looked tested.
- **Fix:** a "Support" group with a `diagnostics` item in **both** lists — `NAV_GROUPS` (desktop rail) and `MENU_GROUPS` (mobile menu), which are deliberately separate because their copy differs. Visibility comes from the shared `canAccessTab`, so it still appears only in a support session; `adminOnly: true` is set as well so the legacy `isAdmin || !item.adminOnly` fallback (used when no `canAccessTab` is passed) cannot leak it to a non-admin.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/settings-tabs-have-nav-entries.test.ts` asserts the tab values in each list equal `ALL_SETTINGS_TABS`, so **any** future tab added without a nav entry fails the build rather than shipping invisible. Both lists now export their values for that check. Confirmed RED for both lists before the fix.

### A-250 — the manual "Rebuild stock from movement history" button skipped the completeness guard the automatic healer applies
- **Was:** `device-diagnostics.tsx:308` called `foldStockQuantities()` directly, while the refusals lived one layer up in `stock-auto-heal.ts`'s `skipReason()`, which only `healStockIntegrity()` consulted. So the *more* dangerous invocation had the *weaker* guard, on a page an operator reaches during exactly the incident where a device is mid-catch-up — folding then rewrites every diverged quantity from a log the next pull is about to change.
- **Fix:** the guard moved into `foldStockQuantities()` itself (`stock-integrity.ts`), so every caller shares one gate. It refuses when `_sync_state` holds a leftover `server_cursor` for `stock_movements` — proof the window is half-drained — and is deliberately **narrower than the healer's**: it does *not* refuse on a merely never-stamped window, because a device that has never synced has only its own log and that log is the truth. The healer keeps its stricter `movementLogIsComplete()` rule, which is right for an unattended 24-hourly run.
- **Considered and rejected: refusing the fold during a till-inspection session.** The first version of this fix did that, on the reasoning that `/inspect` advertises itself as read-only. It is wrong, and the test suite said so: `till-repair-audit.test.ts` is an entire suite about auditing a fold **performed by an admin during an inspection session** ("names the admin, not the signed-in staff user, on a fold"; "sets the admin as the row's actor"; "queues the pushed row with the admin as actor too"), and `till-inspection-read-only.test.ts` states outright that it "does not block the fold, which bypasses update() by design". The fold is a deliberate, audited exception to read-only — that is what the whole `logTillRepair`/`TILL_REPAIR_ACTIONS` machinery exists for. An admin repairing a till they are inspecting is the intended use, not a leak. **If the `/inspect` page's "nothing is changed by opening this page" wording bothers anyone, fix the wording, not the guard.**
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/stock-integrity-fold.test.ts` — a half-drained pull leaves the quantity untouched. Confirmed RED, and the 16 existing fold tests stayed green, which is what proved the narrower rule does not refuse an ordinary never-synced device.

### A-171 — every stale-chunk reload was first filed as a fatal crash, and a non-JSON 2xx threw an error that named nothing
- **Found:** 2026-10-07 in Sentry (`DUMOSRX-CLIENT-F`, 260 events, 4 users, a month running, `fatal: true`, **no stacktrace and no `area` tag**).
- **Root cause — corrected in review.** The first diagnosis here was wrong and is worth recording, because it was wrong in an instructive way. `Unexpected token '<'` was read as an unguarded `response.json()` meeting an HTML error page. It is not: `client/lib/utils/chunk-error.ts` already documents this exact shape — a static-export host serves `index.html` for a missing chunk and the browser executes HTML as a script. The two are distinguishable, and the file already distinguishes them (`JSON_PARSE_ERROR_PATTERN`): a JSON failure carries a stack and V8's longer `… is not valid JSON` wording; the production events had neither. The real defect was **ordering** in `components/tauri/global-error-listener.tsx`: `logCrash(…, true)` ran *before* `reloadOnChunkError`, so a condition the app silently self-heals was filed as a fatal crash every single time.
- **Fix.** The handlers now attempt the reload first and report only if it did not take. A chunk error that survives the one-time reload guard is a genuine failure and still reports — that distinction is what the guard already encoded.
- **A second, unrelated gap fixed alongside.** `base-client.ts`'s success path called `.json()` unguarded while the error path used `.json().catch()`. A 2xx carrying HTML threw a bare `SyntaxError` from inside the API layer naming neither endpoint nor status. `parseJsonResponse()` now throws an error naming the endpoint, status and content-type. **It deliberately carries no body excerpt:** an earlier draft promised a 120-character snippet via `response.clone()`, which always threw `TypeError: Body has already been consumed` because `.json()` had consumed the body — verified against Node's WHATWG `Response`. The dead branch and the false claim were both removed rather than buying a snippet by pre-reading every response.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/stale-chunk-not-reported-as-crash.test.tsx` — a stale chunk reloads without reporting; one that survives the reload still reports; a JSON parse failure reports and never reloads; an ordinary crash still reports. Plus `base-client-html-response.test.ts` for the non-JSON 2xx error text. Both confirmed RED.

### A-172 — a plan-refused `feedback` row was reported to Sentry, bypassing the filter that exists for exactly that
- **Found:** 2026-10-07 in Sentry (`DUMOSRX-CLIENT-D`, `-N`, `-C`, `-18`), last seen 2026-09-17 in `development`.
- **Root cause — corrected in review.** The first writeup claimed the stuck-item reporter had no expected-restriction filter and that this fired indefinitely on every free-plan device. Both were wrong. `logCrash()` has filtered these since 2026-09-17, and since 2026-09-28 `push.ts` rethrows a plan restriction before `recordSyncFailure()` is reached at all. What is genuinely unfiltered is `reportStuckCrashLog()`, which calls `Sentry.captureException` **directly** for `feedback` rows — the exact table those issues were about — and so never passes through `logCrash()`.
- **Fix.** `recordSyncFailure()` checks `isExpectedSyncRestriction()` **before both** report paths, rather than after the `feedback` branch has already returned.
- **A test that proved nothing, also corrected.** The original regression test replaced `logCrash` with a bare mock, discarding the real filter — which is the only reason it went RED. It now asserts against the real `logCrash` and the real `Sentry.captureException` boundary, on a `feedback` row, and was confirmed RED by restoring the old check ordering.

### A-151 — two more indexed columns were silently VARCHAR(191), the A-149 defect repeated
- **Found:** logged while fixing A-149; fixed 2026-10-07.
- **Root cause.** `Schema::defaultStringLength(191)` made `products.barcode` and `activity_logs.correlation_id` VARCHAR(191) rather than 255. Neither has a client-side cap (unlike `fingerprint`), so a longer value fails a sync push with "Data too long for column" and parks the row.
- **Fix.** `2026_10_07_000010_widen_barcode_and_correlation_id_columns.php` widens both to an **explicit** 255, so the global default cannot move them again.
- **Regression coverage:** `FeedbackFingerprintColumnWidthTest` gained a data-provider case per column. It connects to real MySQL because SQLite does not enforce VARCHAR length — the fidelity gap A-149 documented. **Confirmed RED against the live local database before the migration, GREEN after.**

### A-153 — account-scoped localStorage caches survived a store switch
- **Found:** logged while fixing A-152; fixed 2026-10-07.
- **Root cause.** `clearDatabaseForNewStore()` wiped every table but left localStorage untouched beyond `recentUsers`, so the previous account's state leaked into the new one — most visibly the old store's product-name autocomplete (`dumos_suggestions`).
- **Fix.** `clearAccountScopedCaches()` clears the keys describing *this device's relationship with the replaced account*: `suggestions`, `syncUniqueSkipCounts`, `syncHealthDeficitState`, `lastSyncHealthCheck`, `orphanRequeueMarker`, `lastAuditLogPrune`, and `posCart`. **`posCart` was not in the original writeup** — the old store's cart holds product ids that no longer exist after a wipe.
- **And `removeItem` alone is not enough for the cart, as `use-pos-cart.ts` already warned.** That zustand store rehydrates from storage once at creation, so the outgoing cart survives in memory and the next mutation re-persists it. `confirmCloudRestoreSwitch()` now also calls `clearPOSCartStorage()`, which resets the store itself. It is imported dynamically and wrapped, so a cart-clear failure can never abort a store switch.
- **What deliberately survives, and one that matters.** Device-level keys stay: `deviceId`, `apiUrl`, UI preferences, and **`loginLockout`**. That last one is security, not tidiness — clearing it would make "switch store" a way to reset a brute-force lockout without knowing any credential. Pinned by its own test.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/clear-database-clears-account-scoped-keys.test.ts` — account-scoped keys cleared, device-level keys untouched, lockout never cleared.

### A-150 — a brand-new signup never cleared a device's leftover local data, so a device that had used a different (now-deleted) account pushed that account's stale writes under the new one
- **Found:** live, by the user — after purging a test store and its owner account via the admin panel, registering a fresh account on the same browser showed "17 unsynced changes" that dropped to 2 on reload, the new account's sync response rejected two `audit_logs` rows with "store_id in payload is outside the caller's allowed stores", and the Action Center showed a stale trial countdown ("18 days left") that disagreed with the live Billing Settings page ("13 Days Remaining").
- **Root cause.** `_sync_queue` (and every local table) has no `user_id`/`store_id` column — it's scoped to the device, not to whichever account is currently authenticated. `useOnboarding()`'s `handleRegister()` has two branches: logging into an *existing* cloud account whose store differs from what's stored locally already calls `clearDatabaseForNewStore()`, gated behind a confirm dialog; the *brand-new signup* branch ("Brand-new setup (no cloud account linked yet)") never did either, since it implicitly assumed a fresh account always means a fresh device. That assumption breaks the moment a device previously linked a now-abandoned or deleted account: the old account's queued `feedback`/`audit_logs` rows (and any other unsynced writes) were still sitting in local SQLite, and the next sync pushed them under the new account's bearer token.
- **Fix.** After the new store is created server-side but before seeding any local rows, `handleRegister()` now checks `getLocalStores()`. On a genuinely fresh device (no local store) it proceeds immediately. **First draft wiped unconditionally here, with no confirm dialog — caught by an independent fable review as a real data-loss regression before anything was committed:** `?tab=setup&step=register` is a documented safe entry point for an *already-set-up* device (`app/login/use-login-page.tsx`, `userCount > 0`), reachable from the traditional login screen's "Create account" link — a logged-out owner with real unsynced local work (offline sales, etc.) could land on this exact branch, and an unconditional wipe would have silently destroyed it with no warning. Fixed to instead stash everything needed to finish setup (`pendingNewRegistration`) and route through the *same* `showConfirmSwitch` confirm dialog the existing-account store-switch flow already uses, rather than inventing a second one — `confirmCloudRestoreSwitch()` now completes a pending new-account setup after the user explicitly confirms, and `cancelCloudRestoreSwitch()` leaves local data untouched (and logs out) if they don't.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/onboarding-register-clears-stale-local-data.test.ts` — a leftover local store triggers the confirm dialog without wiping or inserting anything; confirming wipes (before the new rows are inserted, not after) and completes setup; cancelling leaves local data untouched and logs out; a genuinely fresh device proceeds immediately with no dialog. Confirmed RED on the "asks for confirmation" case by reverting to the unconditional-wipe draft and re-running.

### A-149 — `feedback.fingerprint` was silently 64 characters shorter than the client has always assumed, failing a real sync push
- **Found:** live, by the user — a sync push's `failed` array showed `SQLSTATE[22001]: String data, right truncated: 1406 Data too long for column 'fingerprint'` for several queued crash reports.
- **Root cause.** `AppServiceProvider::boot()` sets `Schema::defaultStringLength(191)` globally (a compatibility shim for older MySQL/MariaDB's 767-byte index-prefix limit under `utf8mb4`). `2026_09_27_000002_add_dedup_columns_to_feedback_table.php`'s `$table->string('fingerprint')->nullable()->index()` never specified an explicit length, so it silently became `VARCHAR(191)` instead of Laravel's native 255 default — but the client's `MAX_FINGERPRINT_LENGTH` (`client/lib/utils/error-truncation.ts`) has always capped a generated fingerprint at 255, on the assumption the column matched. Any fingerprint landing between 192 and 255 characters failed the insert outright.
- **Fix.** `2026_10_02_000006_widen_feedback_fingerprint_column.php` explicitly widens the column to `VARCHAR(255)`, matching the client's existing assumption (chosen over shrinking the client's cap, since fingerprint entropy matters for crash-dedup accuracy).
- **A real test-fidelity gap, found while fixing this.** `phpunit.xml` runs the whole suite against SQLite in-memory, which has no real `VARCHAR` length enforcement regardless of the declared length (confirmed: `Schema::getColumns()` reports a bare `"varchar"` there, never `"varchar(N)"`) — an insert-based regression test for this exact bug passes identically whether the column is 191 or 255, so it would never have caught this, and won't catch a future regression either. Same shape of gap `SyncPushRowLockTest` already worked around for `lockForUpdate()`.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncPushOwnershipTest.php::test_a_fingerprint_between_191_and_255_characters_is_accepted` (general coverage, but not a true regression guard per the note above). The real guard is `laravel-server/tests/Feature/FeedbackFingerprintColumnWidthTest.php`, which connects to a real MySQL instance (same pattern as `SyncPushRowLockTest`, skipping itself if one isn't reachable) and asserts the live column width directly — confirmed RED (191) before the migration, GREEN (255) after.

### A-148 — the server had no way to accept an authoritative `stock_batches.quantity` from the client, so a pre-`a36b00e7` bulk-imported batch stayed at server-side `quantity = 0` forever (fixes `A-145`)
- **Found:** logged 2026-10-01 as `A-145`, the dominant cause of the ~400x gap behind `A-143`; fixed 2026-10-02 once the reconciliation strategy it was blocked on was decided. Affects at least one live paying customer's store.
- **Root cause.** `SyncController::push()` never trusts a pushed quantity — it forces `quantity = 0` on every `stock_batches` INSERT, strips `quantity` from every UPDATE, and only ever moves the column by replaying accumulated `stock_movements` deltas in `applyStockBatchDeltas()`. That's the right design for commutative multi-device writes, but it means a batch whose opening stock never produced a `stock_movements` row (the bulk-import bug fixed going forward in `a36b00e7`, 2026-09-19) has no delta to replay and can never self-heal: re-auditing to the already-correct local figure emits a zero delta and pushes nothing. Client-side the divergence is invisible, because `pull.ts` deletes `data.quantity` from every pulled `stock_batches` row.
- **Fix.** A new explicit, human-confirmed repair action rather than any change to the delta invariant. `POST /app/sync/reconcile-quantities` (`SyncController::reconcileQuantities()`, same `app`/`subscription` route group and middleware as `push`/`pull`/`counts`) takes `{batches: [{id, quantity}]}` — every local non-deleted batch for the active store, not only suspected ones — resolves tenant scope with the existing `resolveAllowedOwnershipScope()`, batch-loads every `StockBatch` and its product's `store_id` in two queries total (not one query per batch — see the performance note below), skips any batch already matching the snapshot (which is what makes a second run a no-op), and for each real difference writes, in one transaction, the corrected quantity **and** a `stock_movements` row carrying the signed delta with `movement_type = 'sync_reconciliation'` and `reason = 'Automatic stock quantity reconciliation'`. Each store actually touched then gets one `ActivityLog` row, `action = 'STOCK_QUANTITY_AUTO_RECONCILED'`, with `properties => ['batches_reconciled' => N, 'total_quantity_delta' => N]` — a summary, matching `AdminStoreDeletionService::purgeStore()`'s own pattern, with the per-batch truth living in `stock_movements` where it belongs. Client side: `reconcileStockQuantities()` in `lib/db/sync-engine/index.ts` (exported, plus `window.__reconcileStockQuantities` alongside `__forceFullResync`), driven from Settings > Data > "Health Sync" with an ordinary yes/no `ConfirmDialog` and a `toast.promise`, mirroring Force Full Resync's friction level — deliberately *not* the store purge's type-the-word confirmation, since this is corrective, not destructive.
- **On the audit-log-tampering correction.** An earlier draft of this feature made the correction silent — no movement row, no activity log — and was rejected by Claude Code's own safety review as resembling audit-log suppression. That rejection was right, and the shipped design is the opposite: a real, permanent, honestly-labelled database record that is merely excluded from default list views by its own type, the same way a "Sales" report doesn't also list "Returns". Nothing is removed from the audit trail; the platform admin's own internal `stock_activity.movements` count on Store Details deliberately still counts these rows as the true, complete count it claims to be.
- **Which views exclude it, and why.** `getStockMovements()` (`client/lib/db/local-database.ts`) had no `movement_type` filter at all and now excludes `'sync_reconciliation'` — the one surface where it would otherwise have surfaced to the owner by default. `getStockAdjustments()` (`movement_type = 'adjustment'`) and `getStockMoM()`'s added/removed-value sums (explicit `IN (...)` allowlists) already excluded it; both are now pinned by tests so a future allowlist edit can't sweep it in.
- **Two more leaks found on review, before anything was committed.** The implementation pass's own exclusion list missed two owner-facing surfaces with no `movement_type` filter at all: `getDashboardOverviewData()`'s recent-activity feed (`client/lib/db/queries/reports.ts`) and `getProductHistory()` (`client/lib/db/queries/products.ts`, the per-product detail page). Both select every `stock_movements` row with no type restriction, so a reconciliation would have shown up directly on the dashboard and on the affected product's own history — exactly the "staff think they're being accused" exposure this feature exists to avoid. Both now exclude `RECONCILIATION_MOVEMENT_TYPE`, with test coverage added to `sync-reconciliation-movement-visibility.test.ts`.
- **N+1 found by an independent fable review, before anything was committed.** The first implementation called `StockBatch::find()` per batch plus `authorizeChangeTarget()`/`resolveChangeStoreId()` (another `Product` lookup) per batch, inside an unbatched loop — roughly 3 sequential queries per batch, or ~5,700 round trips for the exact 1,894-product store this feature exists to repair, on the one request path a slow catalog can least afford. Also flagged in the same pass: the endpoint's own fallback to `$batch->store_id` when the product lookup resolves nothing reintroduces trusting a column the codebase otherwise treats as non-authoritative — accepted as-is (the `in_array($storeId, $allowedStoreIds)` check directly below it still blocks any cross-tenant write; the worst case left is a multi-store owner's correction landing against the wrong one of their *own* stores for a batch whose product link is itself gone, which no available signal can resolve more precisely), but now documented in the code comment at the point the fallback is used. **Fix:** `reconcileQuantities()` now loads every requested batch in one `whereIn()` query and every needed product's `store_id` in one more, then resolves each row from those two in-memory maps — two queries total regardless of batch count, and the `authorizeChangeTarget()` call (whose own fail-open behavior for tables without a `user_id` column was the gap the ad-hoc `?? $batch->store_id` fallback was compensating for) is no longer called at all, since the direct `in_array` check it was duplicating is already strictly enforced afterward.
- **Stale-device risk, raised and resolved with the user before shipping.** The approved design asserts whichever device runs "Health Sync" as authoritative; a stale/rarely-used device would overwrite correct server data with its own outdated numbers, recorded as a legitimate-looking correction. The user chose to require a fresh full sync first over the alternatives (ship as-is, or an owner-only role gate): `reconcileStockQuantities()` now runs `sync(true)` (push then pull) and throws before reading or posting any local quantity if that sync fails, so a device catches up on every other device's movements before its snapshot is trusted. This narrows but does not eliminate the risk — a device that's been offline long enough to miss real stock changes can still be wrong — which is why the action stays explicit and human-confirmed rather than automatic.
- **A critical correctness bug, found by an independent fable vet after the implementation agent's own "fix" shipped, caught before commit.** The first guard against double-applying a `sync_reconciliation` movement on pull was a blanket `data.movement_type !== RECONCILIATION_MOVEMENT_TYPE` check in `pull.ts`, applied the same way on *every* device. That stopped the reporting device from doubling its own correction, but it also meant **no other device of the same store ever received the correction at all** — a second till or staff phone would pull the movement row (for the record) but never replay its delta, staying at the old (wrong) quantity forever. Worse, that other device's own next "Health Sync" run would then push its still-wrong number back up, and the server would adopt it — flipping the store's true quantity between right and wrong depending on which device last pressed the button, with a permanent `sync_reconciliation` audit trail of the flip-flopping. This is precisely the "numbers disagree with staff" scenario the whole feature exists to prevent, just moved one layer down. **Fix:** the server now returns each created movement's full row in the `movements` field of its response; `reconcileStockQuantities()` (`client/lib/db/sync-engine/reconcile-quantities.ts`) inserts each one locally as already-synced (`_synced = 1`) immediately, before this device's next pull ever runs. That device's subsequent pull of the server's echo then hits the existing `exists.length > 0` **UPDATE** branch in `pull.ts` — which, like every other movement type, never replays a delta — while every *other* device (which never had the row) hits the **INSERT** branch and correctly applies it. The movement-type special case in `pull.ts` is gone entirely; a `sync_reconciliation` row is now applied exactly like any other movement, with the actual fix being *which* device already has a local copy, not a type-based exemption. The one residual case this doesn't cover — a device seeded by restoring another device's backup, which already holds the reconciled quantity and would double it on replay — is the same general hazard any pulled movement already carries after a restore, not something new. Confirmed RED by reverting to the old blanket skip and re-running the new "a device that never saw this movement before applies its delta" test.
- **A real race condition in the write itself, found by the same fable vet.** `writeQuantityReconciliation()` read the batch's current quantity outside the transaction, then inside it did an unconditional `$batch->quantity = $target; $batch->save()` — an absolute overwrite, not a delta. A concurrent `push()` from another device moving that batch's real quantity between the read and the write would be silently clobbered, and the recorded movement's delta (`target - current`) would no longer match what actually happened to the row. **Fix:** the write is now a conditional `UPDATE stock_batches SET quantity = ? WHERE id = ? AND quantity = ?` keyed on the quantity actually read; if 0 rows are affected (the row moved between read and write), nothing is written and the batch is left for the next reconcile run rather than guessing.
- **Two more surfaces, flagged by the same fable vet, not blocking but fixed anyway.** A regular (non-admin) user's notification bell (`NotificationController::index()`'s non-`super_admin` branch) lists their own recent `ActivityLog` rows by raw `action` string with no exclusion for `STOCK_QUANTITY_AUTO_RECONCILED` — the owner who presses "Health Sync" would see a bell notification titled with that raw constant, on top of the toast they already got. Now excluded from that list (the admin's own global alert feed was already a `whereIn` allowlist that never included it, so that surface was fine). Separately, `GET /stock-movements` (`StockMovementController::index()`, no known client or web consumer today) had no type filter either; now excludes `sync_reconciliation` for consistency with every other movement list.
- **Also fixed in passing.** `2026_09_23_000005_widen_stock_movements_movement_type` assumed SQLite had no ENUM to widen. Laravel compiles `enum()` to a `CHECK` constraint there, so the test database had been silently rejecting every movement type added since 2024 — `'transfer_in'`/`'transfer_out'` included — while production MySQL accepted them, meaning the suite could never exercise the very types that migration shipped to fix. `2026_10_02_000001_relax_sqlite_stock_movement_type_check` rebuilds the column as a plain VARCHAR on SQLite only.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncReconcileQuantitiesTest.php` (11 cases — correction + movement + activity log, a negative signed delta, idempotency on a second identical run with no second movement or log row, an out-of-scope batch silently skipped and absent from the response, an unknown id ignored, a staff account reconciling its own store, one activity log per store for a multi-store payload, the response's `movements` field matching the written row, a concurrent-write race leaving the batch and movement count untouched, a query-count assertion pinning the batch/product lookups at exactly two queries regardless of how many batches are posted, payload validation); `NotificationControllerTest` and `StockMovementControllerTest` each gained a case for the new exclusion. Client: `__tests__/reconcile-stock-quantities.test.ts` (runs push+pull before posting anything, refuses to reconcile when that pre-flight sync fails, stores each returned movement locally as already-synced and swallows a UNIQUE-constraint retry, the right local rows scoped to the active store, posted in the right shape), `__tests__/sync-reconciliation-movement-visibility.test.ts` (hidden from `getStockMovements`/`getStockAdjustments`/`getProductHistory`/the dashboard recent-activity feed, inflates neither `getStockMoM` figure), `__tests__/pull-applies-reconciliation-movement-delta.test.ts` (renamed from `pull-skips-...`: a device seeing the row for the first time applies its delta like any other movement; a device that already has it locally — the reporting device — does not re-apply it; a reconciliation movement with no local batch still parks a normal deferral; an ordinary movement still replays).

### A-147 — `getBIMetrics`'s `stock_batchValueData` (BI/Analytics "Inventory Value") disagreed with the dashboard and Stock Batch Report's own inventory-value figures
- **Found:** 2026-10-02, while investigating a persistent gap between a real store's client dashboard "Inventory Value" and the admin panel's Total Stock Value (A-143/A-145 follow-up) — not itself the cause of that gap (the dashboard uses a different, already-correct query), but a real inconsistency found along the way.
- **Root cause.** Three client-side queries compute "inventory/stock value" from `stock_batches`: `getStockBatchStats()` (dashboard), `fetchStockBatchReportData()` (Stock Batch Report export), and `getBIMetrics()`'s `stock_batchValueData` (BI/Analytics). The first two join `products` and require both the batch's and the product's `store_id` to match (when a store is active) plus exclude a soft-deleted product. The third scoped only on `stock_batches.store_id` directly, with no product join at all — so it kept counting a deactivated/deleted product's leftover batch value, and any batch whose own `store_id` drifted from its product's, that the other two correctly excluded. Same class of bug as `A-120` (low-stock alerts) and `A-144` (admin panel counts): multiple places computing "the same number" from slightly different queries.
- **Fix.** `stock_batchValueData`'s query now joins `products` and applies the identical scoping as the other two.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/bi-stock-value-matches-dashboard-formula.test.ts` — a live product's batch value is reported, a soft-deleted product's batch value is excluded and matches the dashboard's own figure (0). Confirmed RED before the fix by reverting it and re-running.

## 2026-10-01

### A-146 — "Delete Permanently" 500'd on any store with enough related rows across enough tables, rolling back the entire purge
- **Found:** 2026-10-01, by the user, hitting `DELETE admin/stores/{id}/purge` on a real (test) store via the browser and getting `{"error":"Failed to permanently delete store"}`.
- **Root cause.** `AdminStoreDeletionService::purgeStore()` JSON-encoded the full per-table "rows removed" breakdown straight into `ActivityLog.description`, a plain `VARCHAR(255)` column. Once that string exceeded 255 characters — easy for a store with rows across several tables — MySQL's strict mode raised `SQLSTATE[22001]: String data, right truncated` on the `INSERT`, which rolled back the surrounding `DB::transaction()` (by design — `a_failed_purge_audit_row_rolls_the_purge_itself_back` already pinned that the purge can't commit without its audit row) and surfaced as a 500 with no indication of why. The store was never actually deleted by any of the failed attempts. Existing test coverage didn't catch it because the test suite runs on SQLite, which doesn't enforce column length the way MySQL does in strict mode — the same class of gap as `A-128`/`A-25`.
- **Fix.** The structured breakdown now goes in `ActivityLog.properties` (a JSON column that exists exactly for this), and `description` stays a short, fixed-shape sentence naming the store, defensively `mb_substr`'d to 255 regardless (an initial version left the sentence unbounded, which could still overflow from `name` alone — `stores.name` is independently validated up to 255 chars; caught in code review before merge). No information is lost — `properties.rows_removed` carries the same map `description` used to embed as text.
- **Regression coverage:** `AdminStoreLifecycleAuditTest::the_rows_removed_breakdown_goes_in_properties_not_the_bounded_description_column` — asserts the generated `description` is both short and free of the old "Rows removed" text (since SQLite won't reject an overlong value the way production MySQL does, this checks the actual generated content rather than relying on the DB to enforce it) and that `properties.rows_removed` carries the real per-table counts; `a_very_long_store_name_does_not_overflow_the_purge_audit_description` covers the name-alone case. Confirmed RED before the fix by reverting it and re-running.

### A-144 — `counts.products`/`counts.customers` on the Store Details page disagreed with `operational_metrics.inventory.products` by exactly the soft-deleted row count
- **Found:** 2026-10-01, by the user, who noticed the top metric row said "Products: 1895" while the Operational Metrics card's "Inventory" line said "1,894 products" for the same store.
- **Root cause.** `AdminStoreDetailService::countsPayload()`'s `products`/`customers` counts read `DB::table(...)->where('store_id', ...)->count()` directly, with no `deleted_at` filter, while `AdminStoreMetricsService::countScoped()` (the source of `operational_metrics.inventory.products`) does apply `whereNull('deleted_at')` when the column exists — which it does on both tables. A soft-deleted product was counted by one path and not the other.
- **Fix.** `countsPayload()` now reads `products`/`customers` straight off the already-computed `$operationalMetrics['inventory']` passed into it, instead of re-querying — the two numbers can no longer diverge because they're now the exact same value, and the store no longer pays for two redundant `COUNT(*)` queries on every Store Details load (an initial version called `AdminStoreMetricsService::countScoped()` a second time instead of reusing the value already computed; caught in code review before merge).
- **Regression coverage:** `AdminStoreSearchAndMetricsTest::soft_deleted_products_and_customers_are_excluded_from_every_count` — asserts both counts exclude a soft-deleted row and that `counts.products` equals `operational_metrics.inventory.products`; confirmed RED before the fix by reverting it and re-running.

### A-143 — the new admin "Total Stock Value" figure (added this pass, see `A-142`) undercounted a real store's inventory value by ~400x — the scoping fix here was real but secondary; the dominant cause was tracked separately as `A-145` and is now fixed as `A-148`
- **Found:** 2026-10-01, by the user, immediately after the feature shipped — they compared the admin Store Details page's new "Total Stock Value" (₦83,660) against the same store's own client dashboard "Inventory Value" (₦33,493,079) for the exact same store and noticed the massive gap.
- **First fix attempt (real, but not the dominant cause — corrected here after further investigation).** `AdminStoreMetricsService::stockValueRaw()` originally scoped `stock_batches` by its own `store_id` column directly, which is not the authoritative scoping column anywhere else in the sync engine (`SyncController::applyPullTenantScope()`/`counts()` both scope via `product_id -> products.store_id` instead, with an explicit comment warning against the naive approach). This was changed to match, which is a legitimate correctness fix for any batch whose own `store_id` is null/stale — but for this specific store, re-verification after the change showed the figure **completely unchanged** (still exactly ₦83,660), proving this was not the cause of the ~400x gap here.
- **The actual dominant cause, found by a follow-up investigation:** the server never accepts a `quantity` snapshot from the client for `stock_batches` — `SyncController` forces `quantity = 0` on every INSERT and only ever moves it afterward via `stock_movements` deltas. A batch created by a bulk import that predates commit `a36b00e7` (2026-09-19, which started writing the accompanying opening-stock movement) has no movement to replay its true opening quantity, so it sits at a permanent server-side `quantity` of 0 — correct-looking on the client (which trusts its own local value and discards the server's on pull) and silently wrong in every server-side value calculation. This was tracked separately as `A-145` and is now fixed as `A-148` (above) — see that entry for the reconciliation design and how to run the repair against this store.
- **Regression coverage:** `AdminStoreSearchAndMetricsTest::stock_value_counts_a_batch_even_when_its_own_store_id_is_missing` covers the real (if secondary) `store_id` scoping fix — a batch with `store_id => null` whose product belongs to the store must still count. It does not and cannot cover `A-145`, since it inserts `quantity` directly rather than going through the sync push path that actually loses it.

### A-129 — a staff account could pull a sibling store's data by sending that store's id in the `X-Store-Id` header, even though the same account was correctly blocked from pushing to it
- **Found:** 2026-10-01, while independently reviewing the `A-127` fix (pre-existing, not introduced by it).
- **Root cause.** `SyncController::resolvePullTenantScope()` honoured an `X-Store-Id` header value as long as that store belonged to the caller's *owner* (`Store::where('user_id', $ownerId)->exists()`) — any store under the same owner passed, not just the one store the staff account is actually assigned to via `users.store_id`. Push was already correctly narrowed to the caller's own `resolveOwnershipIdentity()` scope via `resolveAllowedOwnershipScope()`/`authorizeInsertTarget()`; pull had no equivalent narrowing.
- **Fix.** Replaced the owner-wide DB existence check with `in_array($requestedStoreId, $ownedStoreIds, true)` — `$ownedStoreIds` is already the caller's own correctly-narrowed scope from `resolveOwnershipIdentity()` (one store for staff, every owned store for an owner), the same list push already trusts, so both read paths now agree.
- **Regression coverage:** `tests/Feature/SyncPullStaffCrossStoreIsolationTest.php` (3 cases: a staff account is blocked from a sibling store via the header, still gets their own store's data with no header, and a multi-store owner can still switch between their own stores via the header) — confirmed RED before the fix by reverting it and re-running.

### A-130 — a force-password-reset wrote the plaintext temporary password into the audit log's `description`
- **Found:** 2026-10-01, while adding the super-admin profile-edit endpoint to the same service.
- **Root cause.** `AdminUserService::forcePasswordReset()`'s `ActivityLog::create()` call appended `"... Temporary password: {$tempPassword}"` to the logged description — readable by anyone with activity-log or database access, and live until the user changed it. The password was already delivered by email and in-app notification, so the log copy added no operational value.
- **Fix.** Dropped the password from the logged description, keeping only who was reset and by whom.
- **Regression coverage:** `tests/Feature/Admin/AdminForcePasswordResetAuditTest.php` — asserts the returned temp password never appears in the persisted `ActivityLog` description.

### A-131 — every admin `ActivityLog::create()` passed a `'status' => 'success'` key that no column or `$fillable` entry backed, so it was silently discarded
- **Found:** 2026-10-01, while asserting on a newly written audit row.
- **Root cause.** `activity_logs` has no `status` column and `ActivityLog::$fillable` has no `status` entry, yet ~20 call sites across `AdminUserService`, `AdminStoreService`, `AdminRoleService`, `AdminStoreDeletionService`, `AdminStoreController` and `UpdatesUserProfiles` passed one anyway. Eloquent silently drops non-fillable mass-assigned keys, so `$log->status` always read `null` — misleading convention, not a live bug, since nothing read the value back.
- **Fix.** Removed the dead `'status' => 'success'` key from every `ActivityLog::create()` call site (matching current behaviour, the smaller of the two fixes the finding offered). Two *different* `'status' => 'success'` keys on `PaymentTransaction::create()` calls in `AdminUserService`/`AdminStoreService` are a real, fillable column on that model and were correctly left alone.
- **Regression coverage:** existing admin test suite (846 → still 846 passing after the removal); no new test needed since the key was already provably inert — covered instead by the full suite staying green, including `AdminActivatePaidPlanTest`'s `PaymentTransaction` assertions, which caught an initial over-broad removal during this fix (see commit history) and confirmed the two payment-status keys needed to stay.

### A-133 — the admin panel had no way to confirm a platform user's current permission-override state before editing it
- **Found:** 2026-10-01 while building the per-admin permission-override UI (`UserProfileDialog`).
- **Root cause.** `AdminUserService::getGlobalUsers()` hand-built its response array and never included `effective_permissions`, even though `User::getEffectivePermissionsAttribute()` already computed it. `user-permission-overrides-form.tsx`'s 3-state control (Inherited/Granted/Revoked) could therefore only ever default every permission to "Inherited," regardless of whether the user actually held it.
- **First fix attempt (caught in code review before merge, corrected here).** Added `'effective_permissions' => $user->effective_permissions` to the mapped array in `getGlobalUsers()` — this reintroduced exactly the N+1 that commit `b7a39eea` had deliberately scoped this accessor away from (`hasPermission()` runs up to 3 queries per slug; with ~8 serialized slugs and 10 users per page, that's on the order of 100-150+ extra queries on every load of the Platform Users page). Caught by `/code-review` before this ever shipped.
- **Fix (shipped).** Reverted the list-embedded field. Added a dedicated single-user endpoint, `GET /admin/users/{id}/permissions` (`AdminUserController::effectivePermissions()` / `AdminUserService::getEffectivePermissions()`, `role:super_admin`), and a `useAdminUserEffectivePermissions()` hook that `UserPermissionOverridesForm` calls on demand only when the override form actually opens for one user — so "Granted" reflects the real effective permissions without re-running the accessor for every row of every page. An explicit revoke still can't be told apart from "role never granted it" (both read "Inherited") until a future endpoint exposes the raw `permission_user` override rows — unchanged from the original finding's scope.
- **Regression coverage:** `tests/Feature/Admin/AdminGlobalUsersEffectivePermissionsTest.php` (the new single-user endpoint reports a granted override; the list response no longer carries `effective_permissions` per row), `web/__tests__/user-permission-overrides-form.test.tsx` (defaults from the fetched endpoint, shows a loading state until it resolves).

### A-135 — `mails-tab.tsx`'s Send Email button and `feedback-tab.tsx` had no permission check of their own; their only protection was that `/admin/communications` was unreachable by anyone who shouldn't see it
- **Found:** 2026-10-01 while adding `permissions: ["send_notifications"]` to the `communications` sidebar item; the immediate reachability gap was closed in that same change (the parent `CommunicationsPage` wrapping both tabs' triggers/content in `checkIsSuperAdmin`), leaving the two tab components still trusting their caller to gate them.
- **Root cause.** Neither `MailsTab` nor `FeedbackTab` checked the viewer's role itself — a future refactor of `CommunicationsPage`, a deep link, or a default-tab change could re-expose either with no server-side consequence beyond a 403, and no client-side warning either.
- **Fix.** Both components now call `checkIsSuperAdmin(useAdminAuthStore().user?.role)` themselves and render `null` if false, independent of how their parent happens to render them.
- **Regression coverage:** `web/__tests__/admin-mails-feedback-tab-self-guard.test.tsx` (4 cases: each tab renders nothing for a non-`super_admin` and renders normally for a `super_admin`, mounted directly rather than through the parent page).

### A-137 — the Admin Permissions matrix saved a role-permission change on the checkbox click itself, with no confirm or save step
- **Found:** 2026-10-01 during the whole-branch final review of the platform admin delegation plan (review finding M2); confirmed live (toggling a cell wrote through to `permission_role` immediately, with no dialog and no undo).
- **Root cause.** `admin-permissions-card.tsx`'s matrix `Checkbox.onCheckedChange` called `togglePermission()`, which fired `useUpdateRolePermissionsMutation()` straight away — one mis-click instantly revoked a capability for every admin holding that role, platform-wide and live, with no recovery but noticing and re-ticking the box.
- **Fix.** Toggling a cell now only stages the change in local state (`pendingByRole`, keyed by role slug), shown as an amber dot on the role's column header and an amber banner naming every role with unsaved changes. Nothing is written until the operator clicks "Save changes" (which commits every staged role and reports any that failed, leaving those still pending) or "Discard" (clears all staged changes with no write at all). `saveAllPendingChanges()` tracks failures by role **slug**, not name — an initial version matched failures back to pending state by name and silently discarded (while reporting success for) a staged edit whose role was deleted by another admin/tab before Save ran, since a missing role was skipped without ever being recorded as a failure; caught in code review before merge.
- **Regression coverage:** `web/__tests__/admin-permissions-card.test.tsx` — rewrote the one test that asserted the old instant-write behavior, added cases for staging without writing, Save committing the full updated list, Discard reverting with no mutation call, and a role deleted mid-session keeping its edit staged with a visible failure toast instead of silently vanishing.

### A-138 — `updateUserPermissionOverrides()` looped over per-permission writes with no surrounding transaction, so a mid-loop failure left a half-applied override set
- **Found:** 2026-10-01 during the whole-branch final review of the platform admin delegation plan (review finding M5, second half).
- **Root cause.** `AdminRoleController::updateUserPermissionOverrides()`'s `foreach` called `AdminRoleService::setUserPermissionOverride()` once per submitted permission, each its own write and its own `ActivityLog` entry, with nothing wrapping the loop in `DB::transaction()`. A failure partway through (e.g. a permission slug deleted concurrently) left some overrides committed and audited while others were not.
- **Fix.** Added `AdminRoleService::setUserPermissionOverrides()` (plural), which wraps the per-permission loop in `DB::transaction()` and owns it entirely; the controller now calls this single method instead of looping itself, keeping it thin per `.agents/AGENTS.md` §4.
- **Regression coverage:** `AdminRoleControllerTest::test_a_mid_loop_failure_leaves_no_permission_override_applied` — deletes a permission mid-submission and asserts neither override was applied; confirmed RED before the fix by reverting it and re-running.

### A-139 — the Store Fleet table still rendered a Total Revenue column for a `platform_admin`/`agent`, filled with em-dashes
- **Found:** 2026-10-01 during the whole-branch final review of the platform admin delegation plan (review finding M8); seen live in the browser smoke test.
- **Root cause.** `store-table.tsx`'s `TOTAL REVENUE` `TableHead` and cell rendered unconditionally; `AdminStoreSummary.revenue` is optional and absent for any non-`super_admin` caller, so the cell fell back to an em-dash on every row — reading as "the platform has no revenue data" rather than "you are not shown this."
- **Fix.** The column (header and cell) is now wrapped in `checkIsSuperAdmin(user?.role)`, and the empty-state row's `colSpan` adjusts (6 vs 7) to match.
- **Regression coverage:** `web/__tests__/admin-store-table-revenue-gating.test.tsx` (hidden for a delegated admin, shown for a `super_admin`).

### A-142 — "Registered Devices" on the Store Details page was not a real per-device count
- **Found:** 2026-10-01 while investigating a user-reported "Last Active" timestamp bug.
- **Root cause.** `AdminStoreMetricsService::operationalMetrics()`'s `device_count` was `$store->device_id ? 1 : 0` — a single nullable column on `stores`, not a real devices table — so the Operational Metrics card's "Registered Devices" label could only ever read 0 or 1 regardless of how many physical POS terminals actually sync against that store.
- **Fix.** Relabeled the field "Device Registered" with a Yes/No value derived from the same underlying count, matching what the data actually measures instead of implying a count. A real per-device identity (a `store_devices` table keyed by device fingerprint) remains a genuine feature, not a fix, and was out of scope here.
- **Regression coverage:** none needed beyond the existing `device_count` backend assertions (unchanged) — this was a frontend label/value change with no new logic branch.

### A-141 — every custom platform role created via the delegation UI was locked out of the entire admin panel, both server- and client-side
- **Found:** 2026-10-01, by the user, immediately after the platform admin delegation plan's final-review fix wave was merged to `dev` — the wave's own C1 fix made custom-role *assignment* reachable but the resulting account could not actually sign in. The final review had surfaced this exact gap and parked it as a known limitation to defer; the user's pushback ("if newly created roles gets bounced, that means you aren't done with the implementations then") is the reason it was fixed immediately instead of staying deferred.
- **Root cause (two independent causes, both had to be fixed).**
  1. **Backend:** the entire `/admin/*` route group sits behind an outer `permission:manage_platform` gate (`routes/api.php`). `manage_platform` is seeded onto the 3 built-in roles but is not one of the 5 `User::DELEGATABLE_PERMISSIONS` slugs, and `AdminRoleService::createRole()` only ever synced client-chosen catalog slugs — so a role made through the "create custom role" UI could never hold it, and any user assigned to that role got 403'd on literally every admin request regardless of which of the 5 delegatable permissions they'd been given.
  2. **Frontend:** `web/lib/store/use-admin-auth-store.ts`'s `checkCanAccessAdmin()` hardcoded an allow-list of the 3 built-in role slugs (`super_admin`, `platform_admin`, `agent`), so a custom-role user was bounced at the admin login form and the layout guard before a single request ever reached the backend fix above.
- **Fix.**
  1. `AdminRoleService::createRole()` now always syncs `[...$permissionSlugs, 'manage_platform']`. `updateRolePermissions()`'s pre-existing "preserve non-catalog permission ids" logic (originally written to protect `platform_admin`/`agent`'s `create_accounts`/`grant_trials`) already keeps `manage_platform` from being stripped when a custom role's matrix is edited, and `listRoles()`'s catalog intersect already keeps it out of the matrix checkboxes — neither needed a change. Migration `2026_10_02_000004_backfill_manage_platform_onto_custom_roles` grants it to any custom role created before this fix shipped.
  2. `manage_platform` was added to `User::SERIALIZED_PERMISSIONS` (new `PLATFORM_ACCESS_PERMISSION` const) so it reaches the serialized `effective_permissions` the same way `grant_trials` already does.
  3. `checkCanAccessAdmin(user)` is now `checkHasPermission(user, "manage_platform")` — the literal frontend mirror of the backend route gate — instead of a role-slug allow-list, so a future custom role never needs a frontend change to be let into the dashboard shell.
  4. **Post-login landing page.** The login form and the login page's already-signed-in redirect both hardcoded a non-super-admin fallback of `/admin/referrals` — a page the referrals sidebar item itself still gates to the 3 built-in roles, so a custom-role user who got past the fix above landed on an unreachable-from-nav, empty page as their very first screen. Both now redirect to `visibleSidebarItems(user)[0]?.href`, i.e. whichever page that user's own sidebar actually shows, falling back to `/admin/referrals` only if that list is empty.
