- **Fix (visibility, not automation).** `PaymentController::recordUnhandledEvent()` is the shared `else` branch of both handlers: `Log::info` for ordinary noise (`transfer.success`, a plain failed charge, `subscription.*`) and, for anything reading as a refund/dispute/chargeback, `Log::warning` **plus** an `AdminAlertService` alert that states plainly that nothing was refunded or cancelled automatically and must be reconciled by hand. It still returns 200 — a 4xx/5xx only makes the provider redeliver forever. It runs before any transactional work in either handler, so the A-110 rule (never `AdminAlertService::send()` under a DB transaction or row lock) holds.
- **Matched on wording rather than an event-name list, deliberately, and flagged as such.** The needles are `refund`, `dispute`, `chargeback`, `charge_back`, `reversal`, `reversed`, tested against Paystack's `event` and against Flutterwave's `event` + `data.status` concatenated. Paystack's names are known (`refund.processed`, `refund.failed`, `charge.dispute.create|remind|resolve`); **Flutterwave's were not verifiable anywhere in this codebase** — its handler never read `event` at all before this, only `data.status` — so substring matching covers whichever spelling actually arrives instead of a guess that would silently miss. `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` records that, and says to write the real names down before narrowing the match.
- **Regression coverage:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/PaymentWebhookTest.php` — a PG-9 block covering six refund/dispute shapes across both providers (each alerts), a refund event never activating the subscription, an ordinary unhandled event logging at `info` without alerting, a plain failed Flutterwave charge not alerting, and a successful charge still activating and not being misrouted as unhandled.

### A-60 (P3) — a failed hard-delete during a held-sale recall left the sale recallable again while telling the cashier the recall failed
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `d1ec3682` (with `A-59`). From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `handleRecallTransaction()` committed `clearCart()`/`restoreCart(...)` to Zustand *before* `await remove("held_transactions", held.id)`, both inside one `try` whose `catch` reports "Failed to recall transaction". `remove()` fails for reasons unrelated to the recall — `assertWritable()` on a read-only (second) browser tab, `assertStoreOwnership()` on a device whose active store has moved — leaving the cashier with the recalled cart on screen, a failure toast, and the held row still listed as recallable a second time.
- **Fix.** The delete now runs after the parse/lookup work but **before** any cart mutation, so a failure leaves the pre-recall cart exactly as it was and the held sale genuinely still held. The parse-before-mutate ordering from the earlier malformed-`items_json` fix is unchanged; only the delete moved ahead of `clearCart()`.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/use-pos-held-transactions-reseller-recall.test.ts` — a `remove()` that throws leaves the original cart, its original price and `isResellerSale = false` intact while still showing the error toast. RED before the fix (the cart came back as the recalled sale).

### A-59 (P3) — recalling a held reseller sale dropped the markup and the commission, and blamed the loss on a price change
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `d1ec3682` (with `A-60`). From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** A reseller sale is three pieces of state: `isResellerSale`, `markupType`, and the per-line `unit_price` marked up above `original_unit_price`. `held_transactions` persisted none of the first two, and recall rebuilt every line from the current catalog, discarding the marked-up price `items_json` actually carried — while `clearCart()` had already reset the two flags and `restoreCart()` did not restore them. A ₦1,200 agreed price on a ₦1,000 shelf item came back at ₦1,000 with the toggle off, so checkout recorded `is_reseller_sale = 0` and zero commission. The only signal was the generic "prices have changed" toast, which pointed the cashier at a catalog change that had not happened.
- **Fix (full, not the fallback mitigation).** `held_transactions` gains `is_reseller_sale INTEGER DEFAULT 0` and `markup_type TEXT` in `client/lib/db/schema.ts` **and** in `SYNC_COLUMN_MIGRATIONS` (so existing databases get them on the next launch), with the server counterpart in `laravel-server/database/migrations/2026_09_30_000000_add_reseller_columns_to_held_transactions_table.php` plus `HeldTransaction`'s `$fillable`/`$casts` — per `.agents/AGENTS.md` §5's cross-repo rule. `handleHoldTransaction()` writes both; `restoreCart()` takes a fourth `restoredMarkup` argument and re-applies them through the **raw** store setters, never `setIsResellerSale()` (whose off-branch reverts every line to `original_unit_price` and would undo the very markup being restored). Recall keeps the held marked-up `unit_price`, clamped to the current catalog price, which remains `original_unit_price` (the markup floor).
- **The misleading toast is fixed at the root, not suppressed.** "Prices have changed" now compares the current catalog price against the held line's `original_unit_price` (falling back to `unit_price` for a pre-feature hold) instead of its possibly marked-up `unit_price`, so a restored markup is no longer reported as a catalog price change while a genuine change still is.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/use-pos-held-transactions-reseller-recall.test.ts` (hold persists both columns; recall restores flag, markup type and marked-up price; no price-change toast for a restored markup; a real catalog change on an ordinary hold still warns) and a `held_transactions` case in `client/__tests__/reseller-commission-schema.test.ts`. `npm run test:schema` re-run against a locally migrated MySQL: `held_transactions` reports no client column missing server-side.

### A-58 (P2) — `npm run test:schema` compared client `audit_logs` against a vestigial MySQL table and silently dropped any column after an inline `--` comment
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `d1cae072`. From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep.
- **Root cause, two independent defects in one script.** (1) `verify-schema-sync.ts` looked each client table name up directly in `information_schema`, but the sync engine writes client `audit_logs` to the server's **`activity_logs`** (`SyncController::getModelForTable()`); MySQL's own `audit_logs` table is vestigial and unused. So the script compared against the wrong table — three phantom warnings, and the one drift class that has already caused a production incident (`A-28`) could never reach its output. (2) `getSQLiteSchema()` split the raw column block on commas, so `type TEXT NOT NULL, -- 'earned', 'redeemed'` in `loyalty_transactions` produced two phantom columns and **lost `transaction_id`** entirely.
- **Fix.** An explicit `CLIENT_TO_SERVER_TABLE` map (`audit_logs` → `activity_logs`, a `Map` per `.agents/AGENTS.md` §8, mirroring `getModelForTable()`) resolves the server-side table for every comparison and every warning line, which now name both sides. The parser strips `--` to end-of-line from each line before splitting on commas. The pure parts (`parseSQLiteSchema`, `serverTableFor`) are exported and the script only self-executes when invoked directly, so they are unit-testable. The "add to CI" half remains deliberately out of scope per `A-25`'s ruling.
- **What the now-accurate check reports.** Re-run against a locally migrated MySQL: no client column is missing server-side in any synced table (the three `audit_logs` phantoms are gone — `activity_logs` has them all, and `loyalty_transactions.transaction_id` is present now that it is actually compared). Every remaining warning is a *server-only* column — `deleted_at` on the soft-deleting tables, plus `activity_logs`' Laravel-side `description`/`ip_address`/`properties`/`user_agent` — which is expected and harmless; pre-existing, unrelated to this change, and left as-is.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/verify-schema-sync-parsing.test.ts` — the alias map, a synthetic block whose column follows an inline comment, and the real `SCHEMA_SQL` keeping `loyalty_transactions.transaction_id`.

### A-57 (P2) — fulfilling an online order for a product with no unexpired active batch recorded revenue with zero stock-ledger trace and zero COGS
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `2726a733`. From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep. Not a regression of SF-P2-5, whose routing of this hook through `recordSaleItemStock()` still holds for every case where an active, unexpired batch exists.
- **Root cause.** `recordSaleItemStock()`'s two-step fallback (FEFO over positive-stock batches, then `getAnyActiveBatchForProduct()`) both exclude expired batches, and that function's `includeExpired` escape hatch was used by `submitStockAudit()` only. For a product whose only batches are expired — or which has none — both returned `[]`: no `sale_item_batches` row, no `stock_movements` row, `sale_items.stock_batch_id` null, `cost_price` 0. The POS can't reach it (the catalog query hides such products from `addToCart`), but online-order fulfilment had no equivalent guard, so gross profit was overstated by the whole line, the expired units stayed counted as on hand, and a later return *added* stock that was never deducted.
- **Fix, part 1 — every sale line leaves exactly one `stock_movements` row.** The fallback chain now escalates: FEFO → most-recently-touched active batch → the same lookup with `includeExpired: true` → `getOrCreateTargetBatchForProduct()` opening a `SALE-`prefixed batch valued at the line's cost price and scoped to the product's own store. The partial-shortfall fallback later in the same function escalates to `includeExpired` too, so a leftover quantity can't be left undeducted when only expired batches remain. The existing floor-at-0 and oversold-tagging behaviour is unchanged, so such a line is recorded as an oversell rather than silently.
- **Fix, part 2 — the fulfilment path asks first.** `findOnlineOrderStockGaps()` (`use-fulfill-online-order-mutation.ts`) returns every line whose sellable (active, unexpired, positive) stock doesn't cover the ordered quantity, and `components/pos/online-orders-modal.tsx` shows an `AlertDialog` naming each item with ordered-vs-sellable counts and requires "Fulfill anyway" before proceeding — no `window.confirm`, per `.agents/AGENTS.md` §9.
- **Deliberate behaviour change.** `record-sale-item-stock-depleted-batch.test.ts` previously *asserted* the no-batch case wrote a null `stock_batch_id` and no movement row; that expectation was the bug's contract and is now inverted (a batch is opened, one movement row is written). Called out here because the test rewrite, not just new coverage, is part of this fix.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/record-sale-item-stock-depleted-batch.test.ts` (no batch at all → batch opened, one `-1` movement, cost carried; expired-only → attributed to the expired batch, no new batch created) and `client/__tests__/use-fulfill-online-order-mutation.test.ts` (an expired-only product still writes a movement and deducts the batch; `findOnlineOrderStockGaps` reports zero-stock, partial-shortfall, and nothing when covered). Both new `recordSaleItemStock` cases verified RED first.
- **Not verified in a browser.** The modal change is unit-covered at the hook level only; exercising the dialog end to end needs a real pending online order from the server, which this local pass had no way to produce.

### A-56 (P2) — a mixed-payment sale accepted an over-allocated credit split and permanently overstated the customer's debt by the excess
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `e89e6893`. From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep. The change-due half of this family was closed earlier (credit excluded from `calculateMixedAmountPaid`/`calculateMixedChangeDue`); that fix still holds and the `outstanding_balance` half was never in its scope.
- **Root cause.** `validatePaymentReadiness()`'s mixed branch rejected only a *negative* split and *under*-coverage. Nothing capped an individual split, so cash ₦400 + credit ₦800 on a ₦1,000 total passed (splits sum to ₦1,200 ≥ ₦1,000) and `use-pos-payment.ts` added the ₦800 credit split **verbatim** to `customers.outstanding_balance`, while `applyCreditPaymentFIFO()` computes this sale's owed amount as `total_amount - amount_paid` = ₦600 and can never apply more than that against it. ₦200 of debt with no sale behind it, invisible in the store's own books.
- **Fix.** `calculateMaxAllowedCreditSplit()` / `calculateCreditSplitTotal()` / `isCreditSplitOverAllocated()` in `lib/utils/pos-calculations.ts` express the rule once (credit ≤ `total - sum(non-credit splits)`, with the module's existing `MONEY_EPSILON` tolerance, negative splits floored so they can't create headroom). `validatePaymentReadiness()` rejects the checkout with the remaining-unpaid amount in the message, and the `outstanding_balance` write in `use-pos-payment.ts` clamps to the same ceiling as a write-side guard in case any other caller reaches it.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/mixed-payment-credit-over-allocation.test.ts` (the three helpers, including the epsilon boundary and the over-tendered-cash case, plus `validatePaymentReadiness` rejecting the exact trace and accepting the correct split) and a case in `client/__tests__/pos-payment-stale-customer-balance.test.ts` asserting the over-allocated checkout writes no sale and leaves the balance at 0.

### A-55 (P2) — the outgoing user's permission group stayed in effect for the incoming user after a lock-screen account switch
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-client-2026-09-30` (worktree), commit `24aa6cf4`. From the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** AuthContext's loader effect reset `permissionGroup` to `null` only when `user` became null. The lock screen's "switch account" tile is a user-to-user transition — it calls `login()` without ever going through `logout()` — so `setUser(userProfile)` committed synchronously while `getUserPermissionGroup()` was an awaited sql.js read queued behind the connection-wide FIFO lock. `hasPermission(user, group, key)` took the two as independent arguments and never checked that the group belonged to the user, so for that window every gate was evaluated as `hasPermission(incomingCashier, outgoingAdminsGroup, …)`: `isAdmin`, `canManageStockBatch`, `canViewAllActivity` and every `useHasPermission()` call site read `true`, and a locally-gated write performed in that window succeeded (the local DB has no second permission check).
- **Fix — the group's identity is now explicit.** `getUserPermissionGroup()` returns `{ userId, id, permissions }`, and `hasPermission()`, `useHasPermission()`, `useOwnPermissionGroupId()` and `useOwnGrantScope()` all ignore a group whose `userId` is not the acting user's, falling through to `fallbackPermissions(role)` (fail-safe, role-tier only) instead of another user's grants. A group with no `userId` — the offline assistant's tool context builds one — is still honoured as-is, so nothing else changes. AuthContext additionally drops a group whose `userId` doesn't match on every `user?.id` change, which the report correctly called necessary but not sufficient on its own; the call-site check is the half that actually closes the window.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/has-permission-group-user-mismatch.test.ts` (a mismatched group grants nothing beyond the acting role's tier, an own group still applies, an untagged group still applies) plus a `userId` assertion in `client/__tests__/get-user-permission-group-malformed-row.test.ts`. The mismatch case was verified RED against the pre-fix module.
- Docs: `client/AGENTS.md`'s "Enforced permissions" section now states the group-identity contract and why the `userId` field must not be dropped.

### A-112 (P3, feature addition) — adjustment reasons for damage and loss fed no loss/shrinkage metric anywhere
- **Branch:** `fix/p3-loss-metric-2026-09-30` (worktree). Logged by the 2026-09-30 user-directed follow-up spot-check. Not a correctness bug — nothing computed a wrong number; nothing computed the number at all — so this entry records what was missing and what was added rather than a root cause.
- **What was missing.** `ADJUSTMENT_REASONS` (`client/components/stock-batch/adjustment-derivations.ts`) has let a cashier or manager tag a stock adjustment `Damage` or `Loss` since the Stock Adjustments feature shipped, and `submitStockAdjustment` persists that tag on every `stock_movements` row along with the `unit_cost` needed to value it. No consumer existed: no query in `finance.ts`/`reports.ts`, no dashboard figure, and no `laravel-server` admin metric read `stock_movements.reason` for a shrinkage figure. The categorisation was write-only — a pharmacy's expired/damaged/stolen stock was captured at the point of adjustment and then never reported as the cost centre it is.
- **What was added.** `getStockLossTotal({ from, to })` in `client/lib/db/queries/finance.ts` — `SUM(ABS(quantity) * IFNULL(unit_cost, 0))` over `movement_type = 'adjustment'` rows whose reason is one of the new `STOCK_LOSS_REASON_LABELS`, windowed on `COALESCE(movement_date, created_at)` and scoped by `getActiveStoreId()` like every sibling query in the file. Called from `getBIMetrics` in the same `Promise.all` as `getSmoothedExpensesTotal`, surfaced by `useBIData` as `stockLoss`, and rendered as a "Stock Loss (Damage/Write-offs)" line in the Financial Performance Statement card of Analytics → Profit & Loss (`client/components/analytics/profit-loss-tab.tsx`).
- **Matched on reason labels, not `value` keys.** `buildAdjustmentReason` persists the human label ("Damage"), optionally followed by `ADJUSTMENT_REASON_NOTE_SEPARATOR` and a free-text note, because `stock_movements` has no note column — so the query matches each label exactly *or* as that note's prefix. `STOCK_LOSS_REASON_LABELS` and the separator moved into `client/lib/constants/stock-adjustments.ts` so `lib/db/queries` need not import from `components/`, and `adjustment-derivations.test.ts` now pins the label list to every `direction: "decrease"` entry of `ADJUSTMENT_REASONS` so the two cannot drift.
- **Ruling — reported beside the P&L, not subtracted into Net Profit.** COGS derives from `sale_items.cost_price`, so written-off stock never entered it and the loss is genuinely unreported rather than double counted. Folding it into Net Profit would nonetheless silently redefine Net Profit, Net Margin and the Burn Distribution chart while disagreeing with every other surface reporting the same figures (the P&L CSV export, Daily Close, `fetchProfitLossReportData`). The line therefore carries its own caption saying it is not deducted; promoting it into the P&L proper is a product decision that must change all those surfaces at once. Rationale in `docs/STOCK_LOSS_METRIC.md`.
- **Deliberately not done:** no platform-wide admin equivalent (`AdminStoreMetricsService`, `AdminPlatformService`, `DashboardService` still compute no shrinkage figure) — out of scope for the minimum viable version, and noted as such in `docs/STOCK_LOSS_METRIC.md`.
- **Tests:** five new cases in `client/__tests__/finance-reports.test.ts` (damage and loss both counted and summed at unit cost; a reason carrying a free-text note still counted; `Receive items`/`Inventory count` not counted; out-of-window and soft-deleted movements excluded; 0 rather than null when nothing was written off) plus two drift guards in `client/__tests__/adjustment-derivations.test.ts`. All seven verified RED before the implementation.
- **Suite:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx vitest run` in `client/` — 347 files / 2266 tests passing (2259 baseline + 7 new), exit 0.

### A-76 (P2) — `CheckAccountStatus` inspected one arbitrary store, so suspending or archiving one store of a multi-store account enforced nothing
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep; the P2 that used to compound the already-fixed `A-74`/`A-75`.
- **Root cause.** For an owner the middleware ran `Store::withTrashed()->where('user_id', $user->id)->first()` — no `orderBy` — and checked only that row. Which store got enforced was whatever the storage engine returned first, so for a two-store owner an admin suspension bit only half the time, and archiving one store 403'd the owner out of the untouched sibling under a `STORE_ARCHIVED` message naming a store the admin never touched.
- **Fix.** The store is resolved **per request**, the same way `SyncController::resolvePushStoreId()` does it (`X-Store-Id` header or a `store_id` input when the caller's tenant owns it, else the caller's own `users.store_id`), and only that store's state blocks. Every lookup stays `withTrashed()` so an archived store can still answer "blocked". A request that names **no** store — an owner calling `/user`, `/dashboard/summary`, … — is blocked only when **every** owned store is suspended or archived; if anything is still in good standing the request proceeds and per-store scoping rejects the rest, so an account-wide 403 is never inferred from one store's state. A named store the caller does not own is ignored rather than honoured.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/MultiStoreAccountStatusTest.php` — 8 cases (suspended store named, sibling still usable, archived store named, archived sibling not locking the owner out, the all-suspended and all-archived unnamed cases, a foreign `store_id` ignored, staff of a suspended store still blocked). Three were RED before the fix. `ArchivedStoreAccessTest` and `StoreSuspensionEnforcementTest` were green throughout.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` gains a "`CheckAccountStatus` resolves the store per *request*" section, including the standing requirement that enforcement and push scoping keep using the same resolution rule.

### A-80 (P2) — coupon usage limits were check-then-act with no lock and only counted at activation, so a single-use coupon could be redeemed without limit
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `max_uses`/`max_uses_per_user` are counted from `coupon_usages`, and the only writer was `recordCouponUsage()` at **activation**. Validity was therefore evaluated against a count that only moved after the money was committed, with no lock across the check: twenty concurrent `POST /subscription/pay` calls on a `max_uses: 1` launch coupon all passed `isGloballyValid()` against zero usages and all got the discount, and a double-submitted 100%-off coupon took the `$finalAmount <= 0` self-activation branch twice, minting two overlapping active subscriptions for one coupon.
- **Fix — reservation under a lock, mirroring the `A-79` credit-reservation contract.** New `SubscriptionService::reserveCoupon()` opens a transaction, takes `lockForUpdate()` on the coupon row, re-validates inside it and writes the `CouponUsage` row before returning, so a second caller blocks and then sees the real count. `initiatePayment()` uses it for both branches and stamps `metadata.coupon_usage_id` on the `PaymentTransaction`. The reservation is then either **linked** — `attachCouponUsageToSubscription()`, at self-activation (now inside its own transaction with the `Subscription::create()`) and at `activateSubscriptionFromTransaction()` — or **released**: `releaseCouponUsage()` on an `initializeTransaction()` failure, and in `failTransaction()` stamped `coupon_usage_released` so the release is idempotent across whichever of the verify and webhook paths arrives first. Activation no longer creates a usage row for a transaction carrying a `coupon_usage_id`; the create branch survives only for transactions started before reservations existed.
- **Ruling — no `UNIQUE (coupon_id, user_id)` index.** The report offered one as extra enforcement for the common `max_uses_per_user: 1` case. `max_uses_per_user` is a real column and legitimately greater than 1, so the index would enforce a cap the product does not have. The lock is the enforcement point; `validateCoupon()` is now a read-only check and anything granting a discount must go through `reserveCoupon()`.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/CouponUsageReservationTest.php` — 8 cases (usage recorded at initiate with the id on the transaction, a second user getting no discount on a `max_uses: 1` coupon before either checkout completes, a 100%-off coupon self-activating exactly once, the usage row linked to the subscription it paid for, release on a provider failure, release on `failTransaction()`, activation not double-counting, and a legacy no-reservation transaction still recording at activation). Three were RED before the fix.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` gains a "Coupon usage is reserved under a lock" section next to the referral-credit one it mirrors.

### A-81 (P2) — every Paystack/Flutterwave HTTP call was made with no timeout, unlike every other outbound call in the app
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** Nine `Http::withToken(...)` calls across `PaymentService` and `PaystackSubaccountService` had no `->timeout()`, and Guzzle's default request timeout is 0 — wait forever. `POST /storefront/{slug}/checkout/initialize` is unauthenticated, so a slow or blackholed provider socket pinned a PHP-FPM worker for its whole lifetime; on the shared host's small pool a handful of hung checkouts takes the entire API down, sync included.
- **Fix.** Both services now funnel every call through one private client factory carrying `->timeout(10)->connectTimeout(5)`, so there is a single place to change and nothing can be added past it by accident.
- **"Unknown" is not "failed".** `verifyPaystack()`/`verifyFlutterwave()` catch `ConnectionException` and return `['success' => false, 'unknown' => true]`, logged. Consumers branch on that **before** their failure handling: `StorefrontController::checkout()` returns a **503** naming the reference, books no order and leaves the payment intent `pending`; `SubscriptionController::verifyPayment()` returns a 503 and — critically — does **not** call `failTransaction()`, which on a timeout would have declared a possibly-successful payment dead and released its reserved credits/coupon.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/PaymentProviderTimeoutTest.php` — a source-level assertion that every provider client in both files sets both timeouts (Guzzle's options are invisible on a faked request, so behaviour alone cannot pin this), plus the unknown-verification result, the storefront 503-and-no-order case and the subscription 503-without-marking-failed case.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`'s outbound-calls section now states both rules explicitly.

### A-82 (P2) — cancelling a paid online order transitioned and refunded outside any transaction or row lock, so concurrent cancels could double-refund
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `OnlineOrderController::markFulfilled()`'s pending-only guard was a check-then-write with no `lockForUpdate()` and no surrounding transaction, and the refund was a third-party call made after the save. Staff double-tapping "Cancel order" (or the POS retrying a request whose response was lost) meant both requests read `pending`, both wrote `cancelled` and both called `refundTransaction()` for the same reference — either a double payout from the platform's own Paystack balance, or, when Paystack rejected the second as exceeding the original amount, a *correct* refund falling through to `flagRefundRequired()` with `payment_status` still `'paid'`, telling the store to refund by hand money that was already back.
- **Fix.** The state read, the pending re-check and the transition are now in `commitTransition()`: one `DB::transaction()` holding `lockForUpdate()` on the order row. The loser of a race sees a non-pending status inside the lock and gets the existing 409, so only one caller ever reaches the provider. The refund happens after that commit — never with a lock held across a third-party call — and `payment_status` is written in its own short transaction on the outcome.
- **"Already refunded" is success.** `PaystackSubaccountService::refund()` maps a rejection whose message says the transaction is already refunded / fully reversed to `success: true, already_refunded: true`; the money is back, which is the outcome asked for. The duplicate store notification is suppressed for that case, while a genuine failure (unknown reference, provider error) still falls through to flag-and-notify.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/OnlineOrderCancelRefundLockTest.php` — 5 cases (second cancel never reaches the provider, already-refunded answer treated as success end-to-end, the service-level mapping, a genuine failure still flagged, and the cancellation committed even when the provider call throws), plus a new service case in `PaystackSubaccountServiceTest`. Two existing tests that used the message `'Already refunded'` as a stand-in for a generic failure were retargeted to `'Transaction not found'`, which is what they actually meant.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`'s storefront-refund section gains the lock-then-refund ordering and the already-refunded rule.

### A-83 (P2) — the dashboard summary's `staff` list included everyone the caller ever *referred*, leaking other store owners' emails
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `DashboardService::getSummary()` built the `staff` array as `User::whereIn('store_id', $storeIds)->orWhere('referred_by_id', $userId)`. `referred_by_id` is the customer **referral program** pointer, set at registration from a `ref` code — an unrelated store owner who signed up through the caller's link, not a staff member. `GET /dashboard/summary` therefore returned that owner's `email`, `role`, `store_id` and `last_login_at` to the caller, and the dashboard rendered them as one of the store's team.
- **Fix.** The `orWhere` clause is gone. Referral data has its own already-scoped endpoint (`GET /subscription/referral-stats`, name/store/status only, no email).
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/DashboardStaffScopingTest.php` — a referred owner absent from `staff` (RED before the fix) and genuine staff of the caller's store still present.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`'s owner-vs-staff section now names `referred_by_id` and `registered_by_id` as columns that must never widen a staff query.

### A-84 / A-85 (P2) — a tenant could lock itself out irreversibly: deleting its only store, or deactivating the owner's account
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep. Fixed together because they are one shape: a self-service endpoint reaching a state only a super_admin can undo.
- **Root cause (A-84).** `Store` soft-deletes, so `DELETE /stores/{id}` stamps `deleted_at` — the same state an admin archive produces — and `CheckAccountStatus` then 403s every request in the protected group. A single-store owner clicking "Remove store" deactivated all their staff, archived the store and from the next request on got "This store has been archived by an administrator", which was both untrue and unactionable; the only way back was `POST /admin/stores/{id}/restore` (super_admin). It also left `deleted_by_id`/`deletion_reason` null, so the row was inconsistent with an admin-archived one.
- **Root cause (A-85).** The owner's own row is deliberately visible and editable through the staff endpoints (the web staff table's "Main Account"), and neither `destroy()` nor `update()` excluded it from deactivation. `manage_staff` is not owner-exclusive, so any `admin`- or `manager`-role staff member could `DELETE /staff/{ownerId}` (or set `is_active: false` on it) and take the whole tenant offline: `CheckAccountStatus` 403s every call and `AuthenticatesSessions::login()` refuses the password login, with nobody left inside the tenant able to reverse it.
- **Fix.** `StoreController::destroy()` refuses with a **409** (`code: LAST_STORE`, pointing at support) when it is the caller's last remaining store, and when it does archive one it stamps `deleted_by_id`/`deletion_reason` the way `AdminStoreDeletionService::archiveStore()` does. `StaffController::destroy()` and any `is_active: false` in `update()` return **422** when the target is the tenant owner or the caller themselves; `AdminUserController::deactivateUser()` refuses self-deactivation for the same reason (a super_admin bypasses `CheckAccountStatus` but not the login check). `CheckAccountStatus`'s `STORE_ARCHIVED` 403 now distinguishes the two cases — `archived_by: 'owner' | 'administrator'` with matching `reason` text — so an owner who removed a branch themselves is not told an administrator did it.
- **Ruling — refuse rather than build a self-service restore.** The report offered either a 409 on the last store or routing the deletion through archive semantics *plus* an owner-facing restore endpoint. The 409 was taken: a new self-service un-archive path is a larger surface (it would have to re-activate the staff the deletion deactivated, and decide who may call it) than the failure mode justifies, and multi-store owners keep the ability to remove a branch, which is the real use case.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StoreSelfDeletionGuardTest.php` (6 cases: the 409, staff left active, the API still usable afterwards, deleting one of several stores still working and recording the actor, and the two `archived_by` branches) and `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StaffSelfLockoutGuardTest.php` (8 cases across both endpoints, both targets, the admin endpoint, and the two controls — deactivating an ordinary staff member and editing other fields on the owner's own row still work). All the guard cases were RED before the fix.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` gains a "No tenant-facing endpoint may create a lockout only a super_admin can undo" section.

### A-86 (P2) — every admin revenue figure counted soft-deleted sales, so the admin panel and the store's own dashboard disagreed
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `AdminStoreService::revenueSubquery()` and `AdminStoreMetricsService::salesQuery()` both read `sales` through `DB::table()` — necessary for their correlated legacy-`cashier_id` fallback — and so bypassed `Sale`'s `SoftDeletes` global scope that `DashboardService` (Eloquent) inherits for free. A store that voided 50 sales, or ran `POST /dashboard/reset`, saw ₦0 on its own dashboard while the admin fleet list and Store Details page still reported the full amount, with `average_order_value`, `active_days` and the 6-month trend all derived from the inflated numbers. Within one Store Details response `business_metrics.order_count` counted deleted rows while `operational_metrics.inventory.products` did not.
- **Fix.** `->whereNull('sales.deleted_at')` in both helpers, which are the single source for every consumer of those figures. The third revenue site, `AdminPlatformService::summary()`, uses Eloquent `Sale::sum()` and already agreed, so all three now match.
- **Decision recorded:** a voided sale is **not** revenue, on every surface. Written into `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` along with the rule that any new raw-builder query over a soft-deleting table must add the filter explicitly.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/Admin/AdminStoreSearchAndMetricsTest.php` gains a store-detail case (order count, revenue and AOV all excluding a voided sale) and a fleet-list case. Both RED before the fix.

### A-87 (P2) — `POST /subscription/verify-license` accepted any license key with no ownership check
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** The endpoint was authenticated but not authorised: `Subscription::where('license_key', $request->license_key)->first()` with no comparison against the caller. Since `GET /subscription/status` returns the key in plaintext to that store's own users, a free-tier user who obtained an `enterprise` key could present it with their own `machine_id`, have a `License` row created against the victim's subscription and be answered `{valid: true, plan: enterprise}` — an entitlement bypass plus a write into another tenant's `licenses` rows.
- **Fix.** The lookup is scoped to the caller's own subscription owner (`SubscriptionService::getSubscriptionOwner()`, so a staff member verifies their employer's key and nobody else's) and a key that exists but isn't theirs returns the same 404 as an unknown one, never confirming it as valid-but-not-yours. `last_check_in` also moved to **after** the `is_active` check, so a deactivated device no longer leaves a fresh check-in behind on its way to a 403.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/VerifyLicenseOwnershipTest.php` — 5 cases (stranger refused, no device registered for the stranger, the owner still able to verify, the owner's staff able to verify, and the deactivated-device check-in ordering). Three were RED before the fix.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` gains a section on the endpoint's scoping and on why the key's plaintext exposure makes "does this key exist" the wrong question.

### A-89 (P2) — staff created without an explicit PIN silently got the POS unlock PIN `1234`
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-a-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `pin` was `nullable` on `POST /staff` and the controller did `$pin = $request->pin ?: '1234'`. That PIN *is* the POS credential — `client/` verifies it entirely offline against the hash the sync pull ships down — so any account created without one was immediately usable at any till by anyone who guessed four obvious digits, with every audit log, `sales.cashier_id` and stock movement attributing the activity to that staff member. The same pattern had already been removed from the web `password` (`Hash::make('1234')`), and survived on the higher-value credential of the two.
- **Fix — `pin` is required, with no default.** Chosen over generating a random PIN and returning it once: no in-repo caller relied on the old default (`web/` has no staff-creation form any more — its dashboard is gone and only `webApiClient.createStaff()` remains, unused by any view — and `client/` creates staff locally and syncs them), so there was nothing to disrupt and no one-time secret to hand back in a response body. The OpenAPI contract was updated with it.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StaffPinDerivedPasswordTest.php` gains the 422-on-omitted-pin case (with nothing created) and a case pinning that an explicit PIN is the only PIN a created account gets. Its existing "no pin and no password" case now supplies a PIN, since a PIN-less account can no longer be created through the endpoint at all; the `/login`-with-`1234` case builds its row directly instead, keeping the `A-11` assertion intact.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`'s "Staff credentials" section now states the no-default rule for the PIN alongside the existing one for the password.

### A-110 (P3) — the first-sync super-admin alert ran a synchronous SMTP send inside the sync push's open transaction
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-b-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `SyncController::touchStoreLastSyncAt()` called `AdminAlertService::send()` (one `Mail::to(...)->send()` per configured admin email, no mail timeout) on a store's first-ever sync, and it was called *before* push()'s outer `DB::commit()`. Because this repo deliberately runs no queue worker, the send is synchronous, so a slow or unreachable SMTP server held the push transaction — and every `lockForUpdate()` row lock it had taken — open for the full mail timeout. The client's batch then timed out and retried against still-locked rows.
- **Ruling — move after the commit, do not introduce a queue.** `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`'s "no queue worker, always `->send()`" rule is load-bearing (a `->queue()` call fails silently here: nothing drains `jobs`, and the caller still reports success), so queueing the alert would have traded a lock-hold for a permanently undelivered alert. `touchStoreLastSyncAt()` now returns the first-synced `Store` (or `null`) and `push()` calls a new `sendFirstSyncAlert()` **after** `DB::commit()`. `last_sync_at` itself still moves inside the transaction, which is correct — only the mail moved.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncFirstSyncAlertTest.php` — listens for `MessageSending` and records `DB::transactionLevel()` at send time, asserting it equals the test's own baseline (RefreshDatabase holds one level of its own) rather than the push's nested level; plus a control asserting a second sync sends nothing. RED before the fix (level 2 vs baseline 1).
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`'s mail section gains the "never `->send()` inside an open transaction" corollary under the existing no-queue rule.

### A-109 (P3) — `BackupController::upload()` had no size cap, file-type restriction, per-tenant quota or retention
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-b-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** The endpoint validated only `required|file` and stored straight into `backups/{owner_id}/` on the local disk of a shared host. At the group's `throttle:60,1` an authenticated account could fill the disk with repeated max-size uploads, and nothing pruned old backups even in honest use.
- **Fix.** All four limits live in a new `config/backups.php` rather than inline in the controller: `max_upload_kilobytes` and `allowed_extensions` (`zip`, `json`, `sqlite`, `db`) become `max:`/`extensions:` rules; `tenant_quota_kilobytes` is enforced by a new `assertWithinQuota()` that sums the tenant's existing files and throws a `ValidationException` on the `backup` field (a 422, not a 500); `retention_days` drives a new `backups:prune` command (`App\Console\Commands\PruneBackups`, scheduled nightly at 03:00 in `routes/console.php`).
- **Ruling — the newest backup per tenant is never pruned.** Retention deletes only files past the window *after* the most recent one is set aside, so a store that has not backed up in a year still has a restore point. A pure age sweep would have deleted the last copy of the data the feature exists to protect.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/BackupControllerTest.php` gains five cases — oversized upload, unexpected extension, over-quota upload, a prune that removes expired files while keeping the newest, and a prune where every file is expired and the newest still survives. All five RED before the fix (the prune ones with `CommandNotFoundException`).
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` gains a "Manual backup uploads" section covering the four config keys and the keep-the-newest rule.

### A-108 (P3) — `GET /announcements` sat outside every named rate limiter and read its whole table unbounded
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-b-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** The route was declared outside the `throttle:public-read` group, and Laravel 11's `bootstrap/app.php` deliberately applies no `throttleApi()` floor, so nothing metered it. `BroadcastController::index()` then ran `Broadcast::active()->get()` with no bound.
- **Fix.** The route moved into the existing `throttle:public-read` group alongside `/system-configs/{key}` and `/downloads/manifest`, and the query is bounded by `BroadcastController::PUBLIC_FEED_LIMIT` (50, matching `.agents/AGENTS.md` §8's pagination cap).
- **Scope note.** The finding also names `POST /track/download` and `GET /health` as unmetered for the same reason. Those were left alone deliberately — they are a different change with a different blast radius, and `PG-8` already covers the webhook routes.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/PublicSurfaceHardeningTest.php` — the limiter assertion table gains `GET api/v1/announcements → throttle:public-read`, plus a new case seeding 60 active broadcasts and asserting the public feed returns at most 50. Both RED before the fix.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`'s "other unauthenticated surface" section now lists five routes instead of four, with `/announcements` and its bound.

### A-93 (P3) — `checkSlug()` ignored archived stores although `stores.store_slug` is DB-unique across them
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-b-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `Store::where('store_slug', $slug)` runs under SoftDeletes' global scope, so an archived store's slug read as available — but the uniqueness the check exists to predict is a database-level `UNIQUE` index that does not exclude soft-deleted rows. A store that claimed a freed-looking slug then failed every subsequent sync push of its own `stores` row on a duplicate-key error, permanently, while the UI showed the new slug as saved.
- **Fix.** `Store::withTrashed()->where('store_slug', $slug)`. Reclaiming an archived store's slug would need the unique index made soft-delete-aware first; doing that also requires adding a collision check to `AdminStoreDeletionService::restoreStore()` — see `A-41` above and the standing note in `docs/ADMIN_STORE_LIFECYCLE.md`.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/StoreCheckSlugTest.php` (new) — an archived store's slug reads unavailable, an active one still does, an unused one is available, and `ignore_id` still exempts the caller's own store. The first was RED before the fix.

### A-92 (P3) — the `limit` query param on three list endpoints was unvalidated and uncapped, and `?limit=-1` was a 500
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-b-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `StockMovementController::index()`/`adjustments()` and `PurchaseOrderController::index()` read `$request->get('limit', 50)` raw and passed it to `paginate()`. A negative value produced `LIMIT -1` → MySQL syntax error → uncaught `QueryException`; a huge one loaded the whole table with eager-loads into PHP memory.
- **Fix.** One shared trait, `App\Http\Controllers\Concerns\ValidatesPaginationLimit`, rather than three copies of a rule: `paginationLimit($request)` validates `sometimes|integer|min:1|max:50` and returns the value or the 50 default. All three methods call it.
- **Tests:** `StockMovementControllerTest` and `PurchaseOrderControllerTest` each gain a rejected-limit case (`-1`, `0`, `1000000`, `abc` → 422 with a `limit` error) and an accepted-limit case. RED before the fix.

### A-91 (P3) — the admin panel's "Recent Stores" sync status was always "Active" (Carbon 3's signed `diffInMinutes`)
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-b-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `AdminPlatformService` used `now()->diffInMinutes($store->last_sync_at)`. Carbon 3's `diffIn*()` are signed (`$other - $this`), so the result was always negative for a past sync and `< 60` was always true. The `'Away'`/`'Inactive'` branches were unreachable for any store that had ever synced — the same footgun `Store::boot()`'s slug cooldown was already fixed for.
- **Fix.** Flip the receiver: `$store->last_sync_at->diffInMinutes(now())`, matching `AdminStoreMetricsService::syncHealth()`. A grep for `diffIn*` across the package found no other wrong-direction call.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/Admin/AdminRecentStoresSyncStatusTest.php` (new) — four stores last synced 5 minutes, 5 hours and 30 days ago plus one that never synced, asserting `Active`/`Away`/`Inactive`/`Inactive`. RED before the fix at the `Away` case.

### A-90 (P3) — a store-less registration was auto-marked email-verified even with `require_email_verification` on
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-b-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `RegistersAccounts::register()` assigned `$requireVerification` **inside** `if ($request->filled('store_name'))` but read it unconditionally afterwards. On the store-less path PHP 8 raised "Undefined variable" (a 500 with `APP_DEBUG`-style error handling in tests, a null/falsy read otherwise), so the `else` branch stamped `email_verified_at = now()` and sent no verification mail — bypassing the only anti-abuse gate on self-serve signup by omitting one optional field.
- **Fix.** The computation is hoisted into a `requiresEmailVerification()` helper called above the branch, so both paths see the same value (and the truthiness test now also accepts `1`/`'1'`, not just `true`/`'true'`).
- **Also fixed in the same change.** `verifyEmail()` never compared `email_verification_tokens.created_at` against anything, despite its own error message claiming links can be "expired". A new `verificationTokenExpired()` enforces `EMAIL_VERIFICATION_TOKEN_TTL_HOURS` (168 = 7 days) and treats a missing `created_at` as expired. `resendVerification()` already refreshes `created_at`, so the resend flow needs no change.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/RegistrationEmailVerificationTest.php` (new) — 5 cases: store-less and store-creating registrations both left unverified with the config on, auto-verification still working with it off, an 8-day-old token rejected with a 400, and a 1-hour-old token still verifying. Three were RED before the fix.

### A-88 (P2) — "reset sales data" scoped by the owner's own `cashier_id`, so every staff-rung sale survived while the response said "cleared"
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-b-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep; the two branches the 2026-09-26 `P2-2` fix did not touch.
- **Root cause.** `DashboardService::resetData()`'s `sales` branch deleted `Sale::where('cashier_id', $ownerId)`. `sales` has no owner concept via `cashier_id` — it is scoped by `store_id` everywhere else in the app — so only the sales the owner personally rang up were deleted. Every staff cashier's sales survived, and the response still read `Sales records cleared.`. The `activity_logs` branch had the same shape (only the owner's own rows). The resulting state was worse than a no-op: products and inventory *do* scope by `user_id` correctly, so they went, leaving `sale_items` pointing at deleted products.
- **Fix.** Two new private helpers mirror the scoping the rest of the app already uses: `tenantStoreIds()` (every store the owner owns) and `tenantUserIds()` (the owner plus every staff member employed by those stores, the same set `ActivityLogController::index()` builds). Sales are deleted by `store_id IN (…)` **or** the legacy `store_id IS NULL AND cashier_id IN (owner + staff)` fallback — the exact shape `AdminStoreService::revenueSubquery()` and `AdminStoreMetricsService::salesQuery()` already model for rows synced before `store_id` existed. Activity logs are deleted by that same owner-plus-staff id set. The now-redundant `Schema::hasColumn('sales', 'cashier_id')` guard around the whole branch is gone (the column is not optional; its absence silently skipped the delete *and* the message).
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/DashboardResetScopingTest.php` gains three cases — a staff-rung sale and a legacy `store_id`-less staff sale both deleted alongside the owner's own, another tenant's sales left intact, and a staff member's activity log deleted by a `logs` reset. Two were RED before the fix.

### A-78 (P3) — the web dashboard's low-stock count included every zero-stock product with no reorder level set
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-laravel-b-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep; display-only.
- **Root cause.** Three copies of the same query in `DashboardService` (`getSummary`, `getStats`, `getWidgetSnapshot`) filtered on `total_stock <= reorder_level` with no `reorder_level > 0` condition. `products.reorder_level` defaults to 0, so every never-reordered product with zero stock satisfied `0 <= 0`. The client's equivalent (`getStockBatchStats()`) requires `reorder_level > 0`, so the server and the device answered the same question differently — a 2,000-product catalog reported ~1,700 low-stock alerts on the web dashboard, the Fleet Overview card and the Android home-screen widget at once.
- **Fix.** The three copies are replaced by one private `lowStockCount(string $storeId)` helper carrying `where('products.reorder_level', '>', 0)`, matching the client.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/DashboardLowStockCountTest.php` (new) — a catalog with one default-`reorder_level` product and one genuinely low product, asserting a count of 1 on all three surfaces. All three RED before the fix.
- **Known, unchanged:** `DashboardService.php` is 678 lines, well over the 350-line guideline. Extracting the shared helper was in scope; splitting the file was not.

### A-113 (P2) — `platform_admin`/`agent` had no way to see the stores they registered
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-web-2026-09-30` (worktree). Feature gap, not a pure bug: the server already permitted these roles to register stores while the nav gave them nowhere to see the result.
- **Fix.** New scoped list `GET /admin/stores/registered-by-me` (`AdminStoreController::storesRegisteredByMe` → `AdminStoreService::getStoresRegisteredBy()`), returning only stores whose owner carries the caller's id in `users.registered_by_id` — the column `registerStore()` already writes. Gated on `permission:create_accounts` (the same permission that lets them register a store), **not** `role:super_admin`, and registered above `/stores/{id}` so the wildcard doesn't swallow it. The payload is deliberately leaner than the fleet list's: no revenue figures, which these roles are not meant to see. Frontend: `useMyRegisteredStores()`, `app/admin/stores/mine/page.tsx` (search + pagination + empty state), `components/admin/stores/my-stores-table.tsx`, and a `my-stores` sidebar item with `roles: ["platform_admin", "agent"]`.
- **Deliberately skipped (still open as product work, not re-filed as a finding).** The grant-trial/activate-plan UI half. Worth recording why the scope is narrower than the finding assumed: `grant_trials` is granted to `platform_admin` but **not** `agent` (`RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` says so explicitly), so that surface belongs to `platform_admin` alone and its sidebar item would need `roles: ["platform_admin"]`.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/Admin/AdminRegisteredStoresScopeTest.php` (an agent sees only their own registrations; no revenue key in the payload; a store owner is 403'd; a super_admin sees their own; the full fleet list is still super_admin-only) and `web/__tests__/admin-my-stores.test.tsx`.

### A-99 (P2) — the last two native `confirm()` dialogs, including on a permanent coupon delete
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-web-2026-09-30` (worktree). Direct `.agents/AGENTS.md` §9 violation.
- **Fix.** The coupon delete now routes through `ConfirmDialog` (`couponPendingDelete` state, the dialog names the coupon code and says the delete is permanent, and the row trigger carries `aria-label="Delete coupon <CODE>"`). The impersonation environment-mismatch challenge became a state machine rather than a promise-resolving dialog: `useStoreImpersonation()` exposes `environmentChallenge`/`confirmEnvironmentChallenge`/`dismissEnvironmentChallenge`, and `app/admin/stores/page.tsx` renders a second `ConfirmDialog` for it. Chosen over the finding's suggested promise-resolving dialog because it needs no lifecycle bookkeeping and matches the archive/purge dialogs already on that page.
- **Why it mattered:** a native `confirm()` is suppressible — "prevent this page from creating additional dialogs" makes every later call return `false` (silent no-op) or, in some webviews, `true` (delete without asking).
- **Tests:** `web/__tests__/admin-marketing-confirmations.test.tsx` — spies on `window.confirm` and asserts it is never called, that the mutation only fires after the in-app confirm, and both branches of the impersonation challenge. No remaining `window.confirm` anywhere in `web/`'s UI code. `client/components/tauri/error-boundary.tsx:66` still has one, deliberately: it's the crash-screen "reset all local data" button, which must not pull the (possibly broken) DB module graph back in after a real crash, so it can't safely render a Radix dialog — a pre-existing, out-of-scope exception, not re-flagged here.

### A-97 / A-98 (P2) — clearing a numeric platform-config field committed `0`, and the Storefront Commission save swallowed its own errors
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-web-2026-09-30` (worktree).
- **Root cause (A-97).** Five money/entitlement-bearing fields did `onChange={(e) => Number(e.target.value)}` straight into form state. `Number("") === 0`, so selecting the box's contents and deleting them — the ordinary way to retype a number — committed `0` immediately: a zero-day free trial, a paid tier admitting no staff or no stores, a 0% referral reward, and a 0% platform cut on every storefront sale.
- **Fix.** The draft/commit pattern that already existed as `PriceInput` inside `plan-tier-card.tsx` was extracted into a shared `components/admin/numeric-config-input.tsx` (`NumericConfigInput`) and applied to all of them: plan prices, `limits.staff`, `limits.stores`, `trial_days`, the storefront commission and the referral reward. It holds the raw text as local draft state, commits only a value its `isAcceptable` predicate accepts, shows the rejection inline naming what stays saved, and discards the draft on blur.
- **Server-side floors, per the finding's recommendation.** `SystemConfigController::validatePlanPricing()` now also validates `value.trial_days` (`integer|min:1|max:365`) and `value.tiers.*.limits.staff`/`stores` (`integer|min:-1`), and a new `rejectZeroTierLimits()` rejects a literal `0` limit outright — `-1` means unlimited, `0` is only ever what a cleared field produced. The referral reward is client-side only for now (the server still accepts `0`, which is arguably a deliberate pause; noted rather than changed).
- **Fix (A-98).** The Storefront Commission card moved out of `subscription-config-tab.tsx` into `components/admin/views/storefront-commission-card.tsx` (which also brought that file back under the 350-line rule) and its save now has the same `try`/`catch` + `toast.error(error.message)` shape as its two sibling handlers, plus a `ConfirmDialog` naming the before/after rate — previously the `onClick` was a bare `async` function with no `catch`, so a 422 produced an unhandled rejection, no toast, and nothing to distinguish it from success.
- **Tests:** `web/__tests__/admin-numeric-config-fields.test.tsx` (clearing each of the five fields commits nothing and shows the inline rejection) and two new cases in `web/__tests__/subscription-config-tab-fee.test.tsx` (the confirm step, and the server's message surfacing on a rejected save); `laravel-server/tests/Feature/Admin/AdminMoneyValidationTest.php` gains three cases for the new floors.

### A-96 (P2) — the mobile admin nav rendered every sidebar item with no role filter
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-web-2026-09-30` (worktree).
- **Root cause.** `AdminSidebar` is `hidden lg:flex` and filtered by role; below 1024px the header's `Sheet` is the only navigation and it mapped the exported `sidebarItems` raw. An `agent` on a tablet saw the whole super_admin nav (every page reachable, every data call then 403ing), while "Register Store" — scoped to `platform_admin`/`agent` — was shown to super_admin.
- **Fix.** `sidebarItems` and the filter moved into `components/admin/sidebar-items.ts` as `sidebarItems` + `visibleSidebarItems(role)`, and both renderers now call it. The sheet trigger also gained an `aria-label` so the nav is addressable in tests and by screen readers.
- **Tests:** `web/__tests__/admin-nav-role-visibility.test.tsx` — the helper's own behaviour (including "unknown role sees nothing") plus a rendered assertion that the sheet and the sidebar agree for an agent.

### A-100 / A-107 (P3) — the platform summary outlived its session in `localStorage`, and a failed checkout shipped customer PII to telemetry
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-web-2026-09-30` (worktree).
- **Fix (A-107).** `sanitizePayload` (`web/lib/api/logger.ts`) now matches two ways: `SENSITIVE_KEY_FRAGMENTS` as substrings (`password`, `token`, `pin`, `credentials`, `customer_*`) and `SENSITIVE_KEY_NAMES` as whole keys (`email`, `phone`, `phone_number`, `address`, `delivery_address`, `full_name`), so a bare `email` is masked while `store_name`/`product_name` survive. Masking rather than dropping, deliberately: the response interceptor reports every failed request's body to `/logs/client-error` (unauthenticated on non-`/admin` paths) and the report still needs to be diagnosable. The storefront checkout body was the live case — `customer_name`/`customer_phone`/`customer_address`/`customer_email` reached the server-side error log, and `window.__DRX_API_LOGS__`, in the clear.
- **Fix (A-100).** `useAdminStore.getState().reset()` — the only thing that removes the persisted `admin-storage` platform summary (revenue, recent store names, owner emails) from `localStorage` — is now called from `initSession()`'s catch and from `base-client.ts`'s 401 refresh-failure branch, not just from the explicit Sign Out path. A lapsed session previously left the summary readable from devtools indefinitely on a shared machine.
- **Tests:** `web/__tests__/telemetry-and-session-hygiene.test.ts` — the checkout body's four PII fields and the bare `email`/`phone`/`address` keys masked (with the payload shape intact), and `initSession()`'s failure path wiping both the store state and the `admin-storage` key.

### A-101 (P3) — six broadcast and coupon mutations discarded the server's error message
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-web-2026-09-30` (worktree).
- **Fix.** All six sites (`broadcasts-tab.tsx`'s create/update/delete/toggle, `coupons-manager.tsx`'s toggle/delete) now use `toast.error(error instanceof Error ? error.message : "<fallback>")`, matching the `handleSave` pattern already in the same files. The message has already been replaced with the server's own (including a 422's first validation error) by the time it reaches these handlers, so an admin hitting a max-length or state validation error can now see which field or why, instead of retrying the identical payload against "Failed to create broadcast".
- **Tests:** `web/__tests__/admin-mutation-error-messages.test.tsx`.

### A-102 / A-103 (P3) — the metrics CSV skipped `escapeCsvCell`, and "Export CSV" silently covered only the current page
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-web-2026-09-30` (worktree).
- **Fix (A-102).** The Global Products metrics export was quoting cells by hand and never neutralising a leading `=`/`+`/`-`/`@`, so a sync-originated, store-controlled category name could inject a formula into an admin's spreadsheet. The row building moved into `lib/admin-metrics-export.ts` (`buildGlobalProductMetricsCsv`, unit-testable) and uses the existing `escapeCsvCell` from `lib/utils.ts` like the other two exporters.
- **Fix (A-103).** `downloadStoreFleetCsv` and the new `downloadUserDirectoryCsv` (extracted from `app/admin/users/page.tsx`) now return `false` for an empty page instead of returning silently, and both pages toast "Nothing to export yet" on that — matching the Products page's existing wording. Both buttons are relabelled `Export this page (N)` so the export can no longer look like it covered the whole fleet/directory. The deeper "iterate the paginated endpoint / add a server-side export" option is **not** done; the label now tells the truth about what is.
- **Tests:** `web/__tests__/admin-csv-exports.test.ts`.

### A-104 (P3) — `DOWNLOAD_URL`/`WEB_APP_URL` existed but three UI files hardcoded the same domains
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-web-2026-09-30` (worktree). `release-hooks.ts`'s fallback was already fixed as a side effect of A-94; what remained were the two Downloads pages' `defaultLinks` objects and `app/layout.tsx`'s metadata.
- **Fix.** Both Downloads pages now share `FALLBACK_RELEASE_LINKS` (exported from `lib/api/release-hooks.ts`, built from `DOWNLOAD_URL`) instead of their own literal copies, and `app/layout.tsx`'s OpenGraph/Twitter URLs are built from `WEB_APP_URL` like `app/sitemap.ts` already was — so `NEXT_PUBLIC_DOWNLOAD_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_WEB_APP_URL` are no longer no-ops on a staging or preview deploy.
- **Tests:** `web/__tests__/no-hardcoded-domains.test.ts` scans `app/`, `components/` and `hooks/` and fails on any new `https://(downloads.)?dumosrx.com` literal, so this cannot silently come back.

### A-105 / A-106 (P3) — the checkout button re-enabled mid-redirect, and the reprice dropped cart items without naming them
- **Branch:** `fix/p2p3-web-2026-09-30` (worktree).
- **Fix (A-105).** `window.location.href = ...` starts a navigation but doesn't stop JavaScript, so `finally { setLoading(false) }` re-enabled "Place Order" for the whole interval before the browser unloaded — a second tap on a slow connection minted a second, orphaned `StorefrontPaymentIntent`. `handleSubmit` now tracks a local `redirectStarted` flag that the `finally` honours, plus a `redirecting` state the button also disables on and labels ("Redirecting to Paystack...").
- **Fix (A-106) — the shallow half, deliberately.** `useCartRepricing` now splits its reprice result into `removed` (absent from the catalog response) and `repriced`, and names the specific items in each toast instead of the old blanket "Some prices or items in your cart changed". The **structural** half is not fixed and is recorded in `web/AGENTS.md`: `GET /storefront/{slug}` is still capped at `MAX_STOREFRONT_PRODUCTS = 300` while `checkout()`/`priceCart()` are uncapped, so a catalogue past 300 products can still drop a genuinely purchasable item — the customer now at least sees which one, rather than silently paying for a smaller order. The real fix is a cart-scoped pricing endpoint (`POST /storefront/{slug}/price-cart` taking item ids), which was out of scope for a P3 pass.
- **Tests:** `web/__tests__/checkout-redirect-and-reprice.test.tsx` — a second click after the redirect starts issues no second `initialize` call, and the reprice toast names the dropped item without naming the surviving one.

### A-54 (P1) — a deferred `stock_movements` quantity delta was lost forever if any later page of the same pull round failed
- **Branch:** `fix/p1-bug-sweep-2026-09-30` (worktree). Found by the 2026-09-30 `client/` sweep; not a regression of the 2026-09-22 fix (`16ef5bc9`), whose "delta commits atomically with the cursor stamp" guarantee still holds.
- **Root cause.** `pull.ts` held a deferred delta (a pulled movement whose `stock_batches` row hadn't arrived yet) in an in-memory `deferredMovementDeltas` array and applied it only after the whole page loop finished, while the movement row's own `INSERT` had already committed with its page's transaction. If any *later* page of the round threw — `apiClient.pullChanges()` on a network drop, the ordinary case on the low-end Android hardware this targets — the array died with the function, and the next pull took the `UPDATE` branch (the movement now exists locally) which applies no delta at all. Movements are an immutable log, so nothing ever re-offered that delta: `stock_batches.quantity` was permanently understated, which on the web/PWA build is the *entire* on-hand figure (pull deletes the server's own `quantity`). Second loss in the same block: a deferral whose batch never materialised (hard-deleted server-side, or outside the pull's tenant scope) was an `UPDATE … WHERE id = ?` matching zero rows, silently dropped, with the window stamped complete over it.
- **Fix.** Deferred deltas are now persisted, not parked in memory. New local-only table `_pending_stock_deltas` (`movement_id` primary key, `stock_batch_id`, `quantity`, `attempts`) is written **inside the same page transaction that inserts the movement row**, so the row and its pending delta commit or roll back together — the gap the bug lived in no longer exists. `client/lib/db/sync-engine/deferred-stock-deltas.ts` owns it: `applyDeferredStockDeltas()` drains the table at the start of every pull and again at the end of every round, applying each delta and deleting its row in one transaction so nothing can be applied twice, and `stock_movements`' two cursor stamps are still held back into that same final transaction.
- **Ruling — persisted table over the "self-healing UPDATE branch" alternative.** The `KNOWN_BUGS` entry floated a cheaper option (b): drop the `exists.length === 0` gate and, on the `UPDATE` path, apply the difference between the incoming `quantity` and the stored row's. That does not actually close this gap. The lost case is a movement whose row is already correct locally and whose *batch* never received the delta — a re-offer of the same immutable movement has `incoming === stored`, so the difference is `0` and nothing heals. Option (b) only repairs a movement whose own quantity changed server-side, which is not this bug. Hence (a).
- **`_deleted` semantics, explicitly.** A deferral whose movement has since been soft-deleted (or has gone) is discarded without applying, matching the insert path's own `!_deleted` gate — otherwise a round that deferred a delta and then pulled the movement's deletion would book stock for a deleted movement.
- **The silent-drop half is closed by never dropping.** A deferral whose batch still doesn't exist is kept and retried on every later pull, never deleted; after `REPORT_AFTER_ATTEMPTS` (10) rounds it is reported once through `logCrash` (emitted after the transaction commits, since `logCrash` writes to SQLite). Deleting it would have been the same silent loss with extra steps, and only a batch arriving can settle it.
- **Local-only bookkeeping, deliberately.** `_pending_stock_deltas` has no MySQL counterpart and is never pushed: nothing writes it through the `insert()`/`update()` helpers, so it never reaches `_sync_queue`, and it is absent from `STORE_SCOPED_TABLES` so `npm run test:schema` ignores it. It **is** added to `LOCAL_WIPE_TABLES`, so `resetDatabase()` can't leave a stale delta to be applied against a freshly re-pulled batch. Being in `SCHEMA_SQL`, it is created on existing databases by the normal every-launch schema pass; no migration needed.
- **Regression coverage:** `client/__tests__/pull-deferred-stock-delta-survives-failed-round.test.ts` — the exact trace (page 1 defers a delta, page 2 rejects with a network error, the next pull delivers the batch and the quantity ends up correct), plus same-round application with no double-apply on a later re-offer, a deferral whose batch arrives only in a much later round, and the soft-delete discard. All four verified RED against the pre-fix `pull.ts`.
- **Suite:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx vitest run` in `client/` — 347 files / 2259 tests passing, exit 0.

### A-79 — referral credits were never reserved, so a paid subscription could become permanently unactivatable
- **Branch:** `fix/p1-laravel-server-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `SubscriptionController::initiatePayment()` computed the credits a paid checkout would consume and stored them in `metadata.credits_applied`, but neither deducted nor reserved them. `activateSubscriptionFromTransaction()` deducted them later, inside the same `DB::transaction()` that performs the `status` → `'success'` transition, via `User::deductCredits()` — which **throws** on an insufficient balance. So if the balance had moved between initiation and activation (a second credit-funded checkout consuming it first), the throw rolled back the activation of an already-paid transaction and propagated out: `verifyPayment` 500s, the Paystack webhook then retries the identical failure forever, and the customer has paid for a subscription no code path can ever create.
- **Fix — reservation, the more robust of the two options the report offered.** Credits are deducted at `initiatePayment()` time for the paid path too, with `metadata.credits_reserved` stamped alongside `credits_applied`. `settleAppliedCredits()` (extracted from the activation transaction) therefore has nothing to do on the normal path. Every exit from a pending transaction releases the reservation: a new `failTransaction()` is now the single writer of `status = 'failed'` — shared by `verifyPayment()`'s verification-failure branch and `PaymentController::processSuccessfulPayment()`'s amount/currency-mismatch branch, replacing the two hand-rolled copies of the same lock/re-check block — and it marks failed and refunds under one row lock, stamping `credits_released` so the release is idempotent whichever of the two paths arrives first. `initiatePayment()`'s own catch block releases too, for a provider that fails at initialization.
- **Defence in depth for legacy rows.** A transaction created before reservation existed carries `credits_applied` with no `credits_reserved`. For those, `settleAppliedCredits()` clamps to `min($creditsApplied, $user->referral_credits)` and `Log::warning`s the shortfall instead of throwing — the report's "minimum acceptable" fix, kept as the fallback so no in-flight pending transaction can be stranded by this change itself.
- **Also fixed in the same change (the report's related note).** `User::addCredits()`/`deductCredits()` were an unlocked read-modify-write on `users.referral_credits`; both now wrap in a transaction and take a `lockForUpdate()` on the user row first (`lockCreditBalance()`, `withTrashed()` so SoftDeletes' global scope doesn't hide a deactivated user's row). Releases are recorded with type `'earned'`, not a new type — `referral_credit_transactions.type` is a MySQL `ENUM('earned', 'spent', 'admin_adjustment')` and a new value would need a migration.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SubscriptionCreditReservationTest.php` — 7 cases including the exact two-concurrent-checkouts race from the report (second checkout activating first, then the first; both succeed, credits deducted exactly once), the legacy-unreserved transaction activating against a balance that has since dropped, release-on-failed-verification, release idempotency, and the fully-credit-covered direct-activation path. Four were RED before the fix.
- Docs: the reservation contract, the "never let bookkeeping block an activation" rule and the single-writer-of-`failed` rule are written up in `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`.

### A-77 — `SyncController::push()`'s INSERT branch performed no ownership check, so a caller could plant rows in another tenant's child tables
- **Branch:** `fix/p1-laravel-server-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause, two gaps.** (1) `authorizeChangeTarget()` was called only on the UPDATE and DELETE paths. The INSERT branch had no ownership check at all, and `normalizePushPayload()`'s `$tablesWithStoreId` guard only covers the 15 tables that carry their own `store_id` — so the six child tables scoped through a parent FK (`stock_batches`→`product_id`, `sale_items`→`sale_id`, `sale_item_batches`→`sale_item_id`, `return_items`→`return_id`, `prescription_items`→`prescription_id`, `purchase_order_items`→`purchase_order_id`) were unchecked on INSERT entirely. A product id harvested off the unauthenticated `GET /storefront/{slug}` was enough to plant a `stock_batches` row plus a `stock_movements` delta in a stranger's tenant, and the victim's next pull — which scopes `stock_batches` by their own `product_id`s — handed their devices the phantom stock. (2) That same `$tablesWithStoreId` check was gated on `&& $currentStoreId`, so a caller for whom `resolvePushStoreId()` returns null (an account owning no store yet) had an explicit foreign `store_id` accepted verbatim.
- **Fix.** A new `authorizeInsertTarget()` resolves ownership from the *incoming payload* (there is no stored row yet) through the same `resolveChangeStoreId()` table map the UPDATE/DELETE path uses, so read scoping, UPDATE/DELETE scoping and INSERT scoping cannot drift. It rejects with the existing `'forbidden'` per-change failure shape when the parent resolves to a store outside `$allowedStoreIds`, and deliberately keeps the fail-open only for a parent that resolves to **no** store — a child pushed before its parent in the same batch, or a legacy row predating the `store_id` backfill, which `backfillStoreIdOnLegacyRows` is still live for. `stores`/`users` are exempt (a brand-new row of either is the caller's own setup row, gated by `validateSync()` and the plan's store limit). The `&& $currentStoreId` gate is gone: a missing `store_id` is still only backfilled when there is something to backfill from, but an explicit one is now always verified.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/TenantIsolationTest.php` gains a `stock_batches` INSERT under a foreign `product_id`, a `sale_items` INSERT under a foreign `sale_id`, the store-less caller sending an explicit foreign `store_id`, and a control case asserting a batch under the caller's **own** product still inserts. The three attack cases were RED before the fix; the control was green throughout.
- Docs: `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` now states that all three operations are authorized, that a new FK-scoped child table must be added to `resolveChangeStoreId()` in the same change, and why the fail-open must not be tidied away. `docs/KNOWN_BUGS.md` §8's "verified healthy" line was corrected — it had claimed foreign-`store_id` INSERTs were rejected, which was true only for the direct-store tables.

### A-75 — `stores.status`/`suspension_reason`/`is_demo` were writable through the generic sync push, so a store could un-suspend itself
- **Branch:** `fix/p1-laravel-server-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep.
- **Root cause.** `SyncController::STORE_SYNC_FORBIDDEN_FIELDS` stripped the `paystack_*` settlement columns and the sync-run bookkeeping, but not the account-state columns. All three are in `Store`'s `$fillable` and the push handler applies a `stores` payload with `forceFill()`, which bypasses `$fillable` anyway; `authorizeChangeTarget('stores', …)` only asks "does the caller own this row", which an owner does. A payload `{table_name: "stores", operation: "UPDATE", record_id: "<their store>", payload: {status: "Active", suspension_reason: null}}` therefore lifted an admin's suspension. Same shape moved `is_demo` in and out of the admin panel's demo filtering.
- **Fix.** `status`, `suspension_reason`, `is_demo`, `loyalty_defaults_seeded_at` and `permission_groups_seeded_at` added to `STORE_SYNC_FORBIDDEN_FIELDS`. Verified that nothing legitimately pushes them: the client mirrors these columns for pull only, and the one place it writes `status` locally (`client/lib/api/base-client.ts`, on an `ACCOUNT_SUSPENDED` 403) uses a raw `execute()`, which does not enqueue into `_sync_queue`.
- **Tests:** `laravel-server/tests/Feature/SyncStoresPaystackFieldGuardTest.php` gains three cases — a push that tries to lift its own suspension, one that flips `is_demo`, one that nulls the seeding watermarks. All three RED before the fix.

### A-74 — suspending a store wrote `'Suspended'` while every storefront guard compared `'suspended'`, so a suspended store kept selling
- **Branch:** `fix/p1-laravel-server-2026-09-30` (worktree). From the 2026-09-30 `laravel-server/` sweep; the pass's most severe finding.
