### Whole-client UX + performance pass (U1–U14, P1–P10)
- **Branch:** `feature/whole-app-ux-perf-fixes` (commits `45c0bef9`…, POS / sync indicator / cycle count / catalog / staff / reports / responsive-branch groups).
- Scope was a fresh-context UX + performance audit of `client/` outside procurement (a concurrent branch owned that area). Every finding is either fixed with a test, fixed with a ruling recorded below, or deferred below.

**Design decisions worth knowing before touching these areas again:**
- **A failed read is never an empty list.** `usePOSData`, `useCustomerData` and `product-database` all expose their query's `isError` rather than coercing with `|| []`, and the POS grid, product catalog and customer directory each render a distinct, retryable "couldn't load … on this device" state. When adding a new list surface, thread `isError` through the same way — `|| []` alone silently turns a dead local database into "no results, try a different search term".
- **`useSortableData` reads `accessors` through a ref**, deliberately not through its memo's dependency array, because every call site builds that object inline. The trade-off: changing an accessor alone does not re-sort. Accessors must stay pure projections of a row.
- **An in-progress cycle count is persisted** (`lib/hooks/use-stock-audit-draft.ts`, zustand `persist`, same pattern as the POS cart) and is cleared at exactly the points that clear the POS cart: submit, logout, a different user signing in, and a store switch. Backing out of an edited count confirms first and *keeps* the draft; it is not discarded.
- **The stock-quantity quick-edit in the catalog commits only on an explicit confirm** (`commitOnBlur={false}`), unlike its sibling cells, because its save posts a real inventory adjustment and an immutable `stock_movements` row. Any future cell with consequences beyond itself should opt out of blur-commit too.
- **`sync()`'s "Sync already in progress" is a no-op, not an error** — it is exported as `SYNC_IN_PROGRESS_ERROR` so callers can't drift from the literal. The sync indicator's pending state is driven by the queue count alone; the 30-minute staleness check only escalates the badge's colour.
- **Responsive list branches are conditionally rendered, not CSS-hidden**, wherever one branch is virtualized and the other isn't (POS-adjacent lists, stock movements, customer directory/activity, expenses, activity log, staff activity, suppliers). `hooks/use-media-query.ts` starts at `false` by design (static export), so the mobile branch renders for one frame on desktop — acceptable here, but don't build anything with side effects on that first frame.
- **Only one `SyncIndicator` is mounted at a time.** The mobile header row is gated on a media query rather than `sm:hidden` because each indicator installs its own auto-sync daemon, and two racing daemons made a successful sync report the loser's refusal as a red "Sync Error".

**Deferred, with reasoning:**
- **Virtualizing the *mobile* branches** of the five lists converted in P2. Now that only one branch renders, mobile no longer pays for a hidden desktop render, and each of those card lists needs its own scroll-container/measurement work. Worth doing per-screen, not as one sweep.
- **`React.memo` on `POSCart` is currently inert.** It is applied, and the cart mutators from `usePOSCart` are now stable (`useCallback`), but five handlers it receives (`onCheckout`, `onHoldSale`, `onRequestClearCart`, `onOpenHeldSales`, `onEditPrescription`) are still per-render closures built in `pos-system.tsx`, and `onCheckout` in particular wraps `useFeatureGate`'s `withRestriction`, which is itself recreated every render. Making the memo actually bail out means stabilising `withRestriction` first. The concrete win that *was* taken here is `handleAddToCart` (`useCallback`, with `searchTerm` read through a ref): it is `POSProductCard`'s only per-card prop, so the existing `memo` on hundreds of product tiles now holds across keystrokes.
- **The remaining ~24 `useQuery` call sites with no `isError` branch.** The three highest-traffic list surfaces were done properly; a shallow pass over the rest would add noise without changing what staff actually hit.

## 2026-09-26

### fix: a batch of many sync conflicts crashed the page ("Maximum update depth exceeded")
- **Commit:** `e5be3b1c`
- Direct follow-on to the mass-requeue-on-boot fix below: draining that backlog meant a single push batch could report up to `SYNC_BATCH_SIZE` (50) version conflicts at once. The conflict-reporting loop called `toast.warning()` once per conflicted record with no upper bound — Sonner's `Toaster` does a `flushSync`-driven state update per `toast()` call, and ~25-30 of those fired synchronously in one tick trips React's own "Maximum update depth exceeded" guard, crashing the whole page (reported live via the crash logger, `pushChanges` at the bottom of the stack).
- Past a small threshold (5), the loop now collapses into a single summary toast ("N changes could not be saved...") instead of one per record; below the threshold, unchanged.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/push-limits-conflict-toasts.test.ts` (a batch of 30 conflicts reproduces 30 synchronous `toast.warning()` calls against the pre-fix code; asserts far fewer after). Full suite (904 tests / 169 files) and `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### fix: markSynced() never flagged the source row synced, mass-requeuing a device's entire history every boot
- **Commit:** `b2cd256c`
- Reported as a recurring "A change to Activity Log could not be saved because the record changed since this edit" toast, plus the sync-status indicator showing up to ~15,000 unsynced changes.
- Root cause: `insert()`/`update()` (`client/lib/db/base-helpers.ts`) set `_synced = 0` at write time, but `markSynced()` only ever `DELETE`d the processed `_sync_queue` row — nothing ever flipped the source row's `_synced` back to 1 (`stores`, via a separate pull-path update, was the sole exception). Every already-synced row across the other ~24 tables therefore looked permanently "unsynced with no queue entry" — exactly what `requeueOrphanedRows()` (`client/lib/db/reconcile-identity.ts`, run every app boot from `DatabaseProvider.tsx`) treats as needing a fresh queue entry, mass-requeuing a device's entire synced history on every launch.
- `audit_logs` specifically has no `_version` field in its queued payload (`logAction()` never sets one), so a resubmitted already-accepted INSERT falls to the server's legacy `stale_timestamp` conflict fallback and gets rejected every time — hence the toast loop landing there rather than some other table.
- Almost certainly a long-standing bug, not something the two DB-lock fixes above introduced — it was most likely masked until those fixes made boot/sync reliable enough to run this path to completion instead of crashing first on "Statement closed".
- `markSynced()` now also takes each successfully-pushed change's `{table_name, record_id}` and sets `_synced = 1` on it. `audit_logs` version conflicts are now silenced the same way `feedback`'s already are (both are push-only telemetry the user never edits — a resubmit "conflict" is sync plumbing, not a real edit collision worth a toast).
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/push-marks-source-row-synced.test.ts` (asserts `_synced = 1` after a successful push, and that `requeueOrphanedRows()` no longer re-adds the row on a simulated next boot) and `client/__tests__/push-silences-audit-log-conflicts.test.ts` (reproduces the exact toast text against the pre-fix code, then asserts it's gone). Full suite (903 tests / 168 files) and `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Not addressed:** the one-time backlog of already-accumulated `_synced = 0` rows on devices that hit this bug will still get requeued and pushed once more after this fix ships (self-healing from then on, since `_synced` is now correctly maintained) — no migration was written to pre-clear that backlog, so a device with a large existing backlog will see one more (now-silent, for audit_logs) sync burst before it stabilizes.

### fix: writer-promotion db-swap race, and N one-at-a-time saves in the boot-time orphan-row requeue
- **Commit:** `3b6edc15`
- Follow-up to the connection-lock fix below: after that fix shipped, the same "bad parameter or other API misuse" error still surfaced from `requeueOrphanedRows()` (via `DatabaseProvider`'s boot-time call), and the login PIN page started feeling noticeably slower on first load.
- **Root cause 1 (the error):** `rehydrateFromIndexedDb()` — run when a read-only tab is promoted to writer — closed the old `db` handle and swapped in a freshly-read one with zero coordination with the new connection-wide lock. A promotion landing during boot could still invalidate an in-flight `query()`/`execute()`, exactly the race the lock was meant to close everywhere else. It now reserves `reserveDbSlot()` before swapping.
- **Root cause 2 (the hang):** `requeueOrphanedRows()` issued one `execute()` — one lock-acquire-plus-full-`saveDatabase()`-export round trip — per orphaned row, run unconditionally on every app boot. Once every DB operation shares one FIFO lock, that meant the login screen's own user-lookup query had to wait behind however many of those individual round trips were still in flight. Now batches a table's requeue inserts into a single `transaction()` (skipped when already nested inside a caller's own transaction, e.g. `remapForeignKey()`, since nesting `transaction()` deadlocks) — one lock/export cycle per table instead of one per row.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/reconcile-identity.test.ts` (new test asserting one `idb-keyval.set()` call for three orphaned rows in one table, not three). Full suite (900 tests / 166 files) and `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Not covered by a new automated test:** the writer-promotion race itself (`rehydrateFromIndexedDb()` vs. an in-flight query) — reproducing a real multi-tab Web Lock promotion mid-boot in the test environment wasn't attempted; the fix reuses the already-tested `reserveDbSlot()` lock, but this specific interleaving is unverified beyond that.

### fix: query()/execute()/transaction() now serialize onto one connection-wide lock ("Statement closed" / "bad parameter or other API misuse")
- **Commit:** `7f6663db`
- sql.js has a single shared connection with no reader isolation. `query()`'s row-fetch loop yields a tick every 200 rows so a large result set doesn't block paint; a write landing in that window (a `transaction()`'s `BEGIN`, or a bare `execute()`) could invalidate the in-flight prepared statement, throwing "Statement closed" or sql.js's own SQLITE_MISUSE string. A retry-on-error mitigation already existed (`MAX_MISUSE_RETRIES = 2`) but could exhaust under real collision load.
- Reported as two symptoms that turned out to be the same race: the sync engine crash-logging `area: "sync-run"` with an `audit_logs`/`feedback` queue breakdown, and — separately — a fast login sometimes throwing "bad parameter or other API misuse", traced to `DatabaseProvider`'s unawaited boot-time jobs (`requeueOrphanedRows()`'s per-table scan in particular) racing `login()`'s own fire-and-forget `logAction()`/`migrateLegacyPinToHash()` writes.
- `query()` (when called outside an open `transaction()`) now reserves the same connection-wide `reserveDbSlot()` lock `transaction()` already used, for its whole duration including every yield — not just retrying after the fact. `execute()` reserves it too when called outside a transaction. A call already inside an open `transaction()` skips reserving its own slot (the enclosing transaction holds it; reserving again would deadlock). The prior retry logic is kept as defense-in-depth.
- Regression coverage: `client/__tests__/query-torn-read-retry.test.ts` (updated — a write attempted mid-read now queues behind it instead of tearing it, `prepares === 1`) and the existing `client/__tests__/query-retries-on-statement-closed.test.ts`. Full suite (899 tests / 166 files) and `tsc --noEmit` both clean.
- See `docs/DATABASE_CONCURRENCY.md` §2.3/§2.4 for the updated mechanism writeup.

### Storefront pass (`docs/STOREFRONT_REVIEW.md`) — 15 of its 16 findings closed

All of `SF-P0-1`, `SF-P1-1`, `SF-P1-3`, `SF-P2-1`…`SF-P2-6` and `SF-P3-1`…`SF-P3-6`. **`SF-P1-2` (wiring the built-and-tested Paystack flow into the storefront UI) was deliberately left open** — enabling real online payment collection is a business decision, not a routine bug fix, and was explicitly deferred to its own round. Nothing below makes Paystack reachable from a client; `checkout-form.tsx` still offers only `in_store`/`transfer`. **It was closed the same day**, once the subaccount payment design that deferral was waiting on shipped — see the entry immediately below.

#### A-201. `laravel-server/` — an audit row from an admin soft-deleted mid-session is re-attributed to the store owner
- **Found:** 2026-10-09, reviewing the on-till inspection repair-audit attribution fix.
- **Location:** `app/Http/Controllers/Api/App/SyncController.php`, the `audit_logs` branch (`!User::where('id', $payload['user_id'])->exists()`).
- **What's wrong:** `User` uses `SoftDeletes`, so the default query scope excludes trashed rows and `exists()` returns false for a soft-deleted user. The backfill then replaces the client's `user_id` with the sync token's owner — typically the store owner. For an on-till inspection repair (`ADMIN_TILL_FOLD_STOCK_QUANTITIES`, `ADMIN_TILL_OVERRIDE_CLOCK_LOCKOUT`) that means the server's record names the owner as having run it, which is exactly the misattribution the `actorId` fix closes for the ordinary case.
- **Why the window is narrow:** `AdminTillSessionService::verify()` resolves the admin through the same soft-delete-scoped query, so a trashed admin cannot start a session at all. The only path is an admin soft-deleted *during* a live session who then runs a repair.
- **Consequence:** one misattributed audit row in a rare window. No access is granted or lost — `is_active` and the role check still gate entry, and `server-time` still authorises the override against a live session row.
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-09. `User::withTrashed()->where('id', ...)->exists()` in that branch. The FK accepts a trashed id (`foreignUuid('user_id')->constrained()->onDelete('cascade')` — the `users` row still exists), so keeping the client's id is both safe and more truthful than substituting whoever happened to push. Behaviour changes only for a soft-deleted actor; a hard-deleted or unknown id still falls back, which is what protects the FK. Pinned by `tests/Feature/SyncEndpointTest.php::test_a_pushed_audit_row_keeps_a_soft_deleted_actor_rather_than_reattributing_it`, mutation-checked.

#### A-200. `web/` — the admin settings Account Manager tab 404s on a direct visit or reload
- **Found:** 2026-10-09, adding the Till Access tab beside it.
- **Location:** `web/app/admin/settings/[[...tab]]/page.tsx` (`generateStaticParams`), `web/app/admin/settings/[[...tab]]/settings-client.tsx` (the `account-manager` `TabsTrigger`).
- **What's wrong:** `next.config` uses `output: "export"`, so every optional-catch-all route segment must be listed in `generateStaticParams` to exist as a static page. `account-manager` is rendered as a tab trigger and has its own `TabsContent`, but was never added to `generateStaticParams`. Clicking the tab works (client-side routing), so it looks fine — but the URL it produces has no exported page, so a reload, a bookmark, or a link shared to a colleague 404s.
- **Consequence:** one admin settings tab is unreachable by URL. Low severity, invisible in dev (`next dev` serves it fine) and only reproducible against an exported build — which is why it survived.
- **Fix:** added `{ tab: ["account-manager"] }` alongside the new `{ tab: ["till-codes"] }` in the same change. Kept as a record because the class of bug recurs: **any new tab under an `output: "export"` catch-all needs its `generateStaticParams` entry, and nothing in CI checks it.** A test asserting every `TabsTrigger` value has a matching entry would close it permanently.
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-09, in the same change that added the Till Access tab beside it.

#### A-198. `client/` — the diagnostics settings tab is absent from `ALL_SETTINGS_TABS` and ungated by role
- **Found:** 2026-10-09, fourth review pass.
- **Location:** `client/lib/constants/settings-tabs.ts`, `client/lib/hooks/use-settings.ts`, `client/components/settings/settings-client.tsx`.
- **What's wrong:** two separate gaps. `diagnostics` is not in `ALL_SETTINGS_TABS`, so `use-settings.ts`' URL resolution early-returns for it and a client-side navigation to `/settings/diagnostics` renders the Appearance panel instead; it works today only because `activeTab` is seeded from the URL on mount. And `canAccessSettingsTab("diagnostics", …)` returns **true for every role**, because the tab is in neither `SETTINGS_TAB_PERMISSIONS` nor `ADMIN_ONLY_SETTINGS_TABS` — the `isImpersonatedSession()` render check in `settings-client.tsx` is the only thing holding it closed.
- **Consequence:** none today, since nothing links to the tab. But adding a trigger without also adding the gate would expose the console to every role, and adding it to `ALL_SETTINGS_TABS` first would do so immediately. Both must land together.
- **Fix:** add `diagnostics` to `ALL_SETTINGS_TABS` and to the admin-only list (or give it its own permission key) in the same change that adds any trigger for it. Deliberately left out for now rather than half-done.
- **Fixed:** 2026-10-09. `diagnostics` added to `ALL_SETTINGS_TABS` so it resolves from a URL at all, and `canAccessSettingsTab()` now takes an `isInspecting` flag and returns it **for that tab alone**. The first attempt at this fix ORed `isInspecting` into the whole function, which would also have unlocked `staff`, `roles`, `data` and `danger-zone` — whose restore and factory-reset paths run through `core.ts` raw, outside the read-only write guard, handing an inspection session the one button that destroys the evidence it came to collect. `__tests__/settings-diagnostics-tab-gate.test.ts` asserts every other admin-only tab stays closed. Extracting `hooks/use-settings-tab-resolution.ts` was required in the same pass because `use-settings.ts` was one line from the 350-line limit.

#### fix: storefront online payment is wired up end-to-end via per-store Paystack subaccounts (SF-P1-2)
- **What it was.** `checkout-form.tsx` offered only `in_store`/`transfer`; the entire two-step Paystack payment-intent flow (`initializeCheckout()`, `StorefrontPaymentIntent`, its dedicated limiter, 14 of the 29 storefront tests) was built and tested server-side but unreachable from any client, deliberately deferred earlier the same day because wiring it up as-is would have meant every customer payment landing in DumosRx's own Paystack account with no ledger, no payout mechanism and no way to get it to the store owner — a custodial-money problem, not a UI gap. Its two named prerequisites were a real refund path (`SF-P2-4`'s remaining half) and an answer to "whose money is it, and how does DumosRx get paid?"
- **The money question was answered with direct-to-store Paystack subaccounts**, not platform-collect-then-payout: each store gets its own Paystack subaccount (created programmatically from the owner's bank details — the owner never touches Paystack directly, no "plug and play" burden), and a checkout charge is split automatically at the point of payment, so the store's share settles straight to their own bank account and DumosRx never holds it. Full design and rationale, including the two rejected alternatives and why Cameroon is explicitly out of scope (Paystack doesn't reach it — that's a Flutterwave/separate-project question): `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-storefront-paystack-subaccounts-design.md`.
- **What shipped, in build order** (see `git log` for the full 13-commit sequence): new `stores` columns (`paystack_subaccount_code`, `paystack_subaccount_country`, `paystack_bank_code`, `paystack_account_number_last4`, `paystack_fee_dirty_at`) on both sides per the usual synced-column convention; `PaystackSubaccountService` (`listBanks`/`resolveAccount`/`createSubaccount`/`updateSubaccountFee`/`refundTransaction`, `Http::fake()`-tested against success and Paystack-error responses); `PaymentService` gained an optional subaccount/currency parameter on `initializeTransaction()` and a provider-agnostic `refundTransaction()` delegate; store-owner endpoints (`GET /store/payment-banks`, `POST /store/payment-account/resolve`, `POST /store/payment-account`) that re-resolve the account server-side even after the client already called resolve, are idempotent (409 once connected), and persist only the masked last 4 digits; a superadmin-editable `storefront_platform_fee_percentage` `SystemConfig` value that dirties every connected subaccount on change; a new `SyncSubaccountFeeRates` scheduled command (same cadence as `RebuildStorefrontIfDirty`) that propagates a changed rate to every dirty subaccount, clearing the flag on success and alerting via `AdminAlertService` after repeated failures; `StorefrontController` gating the Paystack checkout option on a connected subaccount and pricing in the store's own currency; a real `PaymentService::refundTransaction()` call replacing `OnlineOrderController::flagRefundRequired()`'s log-and-notify stub (falls back to the old log-and-notify behaviour only if the provider call itself fails); a new "Online Payments" settings panel in `client/` for the owner to connect a subaccount; a third `paystack` radio in `web/`'s `checkout-form.tsx` that calls `/checkout/initialize` and redirects to Paystack; automatic order confirmation on return (`?reference=`/`?trxref=` read via `sessionStorage`, not a URL round-trip through the server); and a superadmin settings card for the platform commission in `web/app/admin`.
- **`percentage_charge` is the platform's cut, not the store's** — a real, easy-to-get-backwards fact about Paystack's subaccount API worth stating plainly, since `createSubaccount()`/`updateSubaccountFee()` pass it straight through.
- **Refund cost, accepted as a v1 tradeoff, not fixed:** Paystack draws a refund on an already-settled split transaction from DumosRx's own balance, not clawed back from the store. Confirmed accepted with the user; no Transfer/Transfer-Recipient claw-back was built. Revisit only if refund volume makes it worth automating.
- **`web/` gained its own vitest test infrastructure** as part of this work (mirroring `client/`'s), since it previously had no test runner at all — new tests for the checkout radio, the return-handling flow, and the admin fee card all run under it.
- **Verified:** `laravel-server` — 435 tests passing (up from 399 before this plan). `client` — 897 tests / 166 files passing (up from 894), clean `npx tsc --noEmit`. `web` — 5 tests / 3 files passing (new; `web/` had none before), clean `npx tsc --noEmit`. **Not verified:** a real sandbox Paystack checkout click-through against a running local stack (`php artisan serve` + `npm run dev` in both `client/` and `web/`, a live Paystack test-mode account, a real browser session) — the repo's local `.env` only has placeholder Paystack keys (`PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY=sk_test_xxxx`), so this remains an explicit, named gap rather than something claimed as done. Whoever picks this up next should run that click-through — connect a subaccount with one of Paystack's documented test bank numbers, place a real sandbox charge, confirm settlement — before treating the flow as production-verified end to end.

#### fix: final whole-branch review of the Paystack subaccount work — currency, settlement-column sync hole, and four silent gaps
- **Every non-NGN store's checkout was charged and then rejected.** `initializeCheckout()` charged in `stores.currency` but stamped the payment intent with, and verified against, the global `config('payment.currency')` — so a Ghana/Kenya/SA store's customer paid successfully and then got a 422 with no order created and no refund path (the refund hangs off order cancellation, and there was no order). All three places now come from one `storeCurrency()` helper, and verify compares against the intent's own stamped currency, the same per-record shape `PaymentController`'s subscription webhook already used. New `StorefrontPaystackLifecycleTest` fakes Paystack at the HTTP layer (rather than mocking `PaymentService`, as every per-task test did — which is how this shipped unnoticed) and walks a KES store through connect → initialize → confirm → cancel → refund; it fails against the pre-fix code.
- **Any staff device could redirect a store's settlement.** `SyncController::push()` applies a `stores` payload with `forceFill()`, which bypasses `$fillable`, and the ownership check admits staff as well as the owner — so a pushed `paystack_subaccount_code` would silently repoint every future storefront sale, bypassing the onboarding endpoint's resolution, its 409 idempotency guard and its last-4-only rule. All five `paystack_*` columns are now stripped in `normalizePushPayload()` (`STORE_SYNC_FORBIDDEN_FIELDS`, mirroring the existing `users` strip). Coverage: `SyncStoresPaystackFieldGuardTest`.
- **"We can't verify this country" and "this account number is wrong" were the same branch**, so a typo'd Nigerian account could be waved through the escape hatch meant for Rwanda/Kenya. `PaystackSubaccountService` now declares `RESOLVE_COUNTRIES` (Nigeria, Ghana — what Paystack's `/bank/resolve` actually covers) and exposes it; the resolve endpoint reports `verifiable`, the create endpoint refuses `confirmed_unverifiable` for a verifiable country, and the settings panel only offers the override when the server says the country can't be verified at all.
- **A paid order that became unfulfillable kept the money.** Nothing is reserved at initialize time, so stock selling out (or a product being deactivated) during the customer's detour to Paystack's hosted page failed the confirm call with a bare stock error on an already-charged customer. `checkout()` now verifies and refunds that payment, marks the intent `refunded`, and returns a 422 saying so. An unpaid/failed reference is never refunded and stays retryable.
- **Two more silent gaps closed:** a customer returning from Paystack with no `sessionStorage` entry (new tab, new session, cleared storage) got an ordinary empty-cart page with no hint they had just paid — now a dedicated panel quoting the reference; and a store that already had a subaccount was still shown the empty onboarding form, with the bank code and masked last 4 it syncs down for display shown nowhere — now a read-only "Connected" state.
- **A refunded order no longer reads `paid`.** `online_orders.payment_status` gained a `'refunded'` enum value (migration `2026_09_26_000003`; the table is API-only, so there is no client-schema half) and `refundOrFlag()` sets it on a successful refund — a failed refund deliberately stays `paid`, because the money is still owed. Anything summing paid online orders as revenue must treat `'refunded'` as not-revenue.
- **Also in this pass:** the three payment-account endpoints moved out of `Api/Web/StoreController` (394 lines, over the 350-line guideline) into `Api/Web/StorePaymentAccountController`, and the plan's over-length inline comment blocks were trimmed to the 2-line convention with their rationale moved into the relevant `AGENTS.md`.
- **Verified:** `laravel-server` 447 tests passing (up from 435), `client` 899 / 166 files + clean `npx tsc --noEmit`, `web` 6 / 4 files + clean `npx tsc --noEmit`. The manual sandbox Paystack click-through named in the entry above is **still** not done and is still the open verification gap.

#### fix: a transient `/storefront-slugs` failure during any `web/` deploy deleted every live storefront from production (SF-P0-1, SF-P1-1)
- `getStorefrontSlugs()` swallowed every failure (non-200, non-JSON, network throw) and returned the single `demo` placeholder, so `npm run build` **exited 0** having emitted one page and `FTP-Deploy-Action`'s sync — which deletes server files absent locally — removed every real `/store/<slug>/` directory. Nothing re-triggers a rebuild, so the paid `store_url` feature stayed dark indefinitely behind a green checkmark. `getStorefrontData()` had the same shape one scope down: any non-OK response became `notFound()`, publishing a rendered 404 page at that store's path while the build still succeeded.
- `getStorefrontSlugs()` now **throws** on a fetch/parse failure, with `STOREFRONT_ALLOW_EMPTY=1` as the explicit escape hatch for offline/sandbox builds (the only legitimate consumer of the `demo` fallback). A genuinely empty list — 200 with `{"slugs":[]}`, i.e. no store has an online store yet — is still a legitimate answer and still falls back to `demo`: a demo-only sync has nothing real to delete in that state.
- The per-store fetch moved to a new `web/lib/api/storefront-data.ts` shared by both `[store_slug]` routes, which now distinguishes the two cases: **404/403** (store gone, suspended, plan lapsed) returns `null` → `notFound()` as before; **5xx, network failure or non-JSON** throws `StorefrontFetchError`, failing the build for that page so the last good deploy stays live.
- Belt to those braces: `web/scripts/verify-storefront-output.mjs` (`npm run verify:storefront-output`) re-asks the API for the slug list and asserts every slug actually landed in `web/out/store/<slug>/index.html`, wired into `deploy-web.yml` as a step **before** the FTP sync so a lost storefront aborts the deploy instead of propagating. It honours the same `STOREFRONT_ALLOW_EMPTY=1` skip.
- Verified against a local stub API (the `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` trick documented in `web/AGENTS.md`): a healthy build emits both storefronts with correct metadata and exits 0; a 500 on `/storefront-slugs` fails the build with the new message; a 500 on one store's `/storefront/<slug>` fails the build (exit 1) naming that store; `STOREFRONT_ALLOW_EMPTY=1` with a dead API still builds `demo` only; and the guard script passes, then fails with a non-zero exit when a storefront directory is removed by hand. **Note for future debugging:** Next's persistent `.next/cache` fetch cache can mask a newly-failing storefront fetch across successive local builds — `rm -rf .next` before trying to reproduce one. CI checks out fresh, so this doesn't affect the real pipeline.

#### fix: no rate limit on the public storefront reads or on order placement (SF-P1-3)
- Only `POST /storefront/{slug}/checkout/initialize` was throttled. `GET /storefront-slugs`, `GET /storefront/{slug}` and the unauthenticated `POST /storefront/{slug}/checkout` had **no limiter at all** — Laravel 11 removed `throttle:api` from the default `api` group and `bootstrap/app.php` never calls `throttleApi()`. An `in_store` order needs only a name, a phone string and a product id, and each accepted order fans out a `Notification` row per store user.
- Added `storefront-order` (5/min/IP, tighter than the initialize step) around the checkout route and `storefront-read` (120/min/IP) around both GETs, in `AppServiceProvider` beside the existing named limiters. `throttleApi()` was deliberately **not** called: a platform-wide floor would have changed the behaviour of every other route in the app as a side effect of a storefront fix.
- `storefront-read` is deliberately generous because the static-export build pulls the slug list plus every storefront from one runner IP in a single pass (~2 requests per store, since `generateMetadata` and the page share a fetch). At 120/min that leaves headroom for roughly 50 storefronts before a deploy would start 429ing — which, after the fix above, now fails the build loudly rather than silently deleting anything, but is still a ceiling to raise (or replace with a build token) before the platform gets there. Recorded in `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`.
- New `tests/Feature/StorefrontThrottleTest.php`, which deliberately does **not** `withoutMiddleware(ThrottleRequests::class)` — that blanket disable in `StorefrontControllerTest` (needed there, and left alone) is exactly why this gap was invisible to the suite. It asserts order placement really does 429, and that all four public storefront routes carry their named limiter.

#### fix: storefront products and stock were scoped by owner, not by store (SF-P2-1)
- `Product::where('user_id', $store->user_id)` in both `show()` and `priceCart()`, and a `StockBatch` sum with no store scoping at all, meant a multi-store owner's every storefront listed all their stores' products and summed stock across them — one store's inventory published, and sellable, on another store's page.
- Both queries now go through one private `storeProducts(Store)` helper scoping by `store_id` with the legacy-NULL tolerance (`store_id = ? OR store_id IS NULL`) for rows predating the `2026_08_14_164310_add_store_id_to_domain_tables` backfill; the batch sum is scoped the same way. Drop the NULL branch once a backfill is confirmed.
- Three new tests: the same-owner-two-stores case the existing `test_storefront_only_shows_its_own_stores_products` is *named* for but never covered (it builds two different owners, i.e. cross-tenant isolation, which always held); checkout rejecting a sibling store's product id; and availability not counting a sibling store's batches.

#### fix: the rebuild trigger missed almost everything a customer actually sees (SF-P2-2)
- `storefront_dirty_at` was stamped only on `wasChanged(['online_store_enabled', 'store_slug'])`. A price change, a new/deleted product, a `show_online` or `is_active` toggle, the store's own name/logo/phone/email/address, and admin suspension all left the published static page stale until something unrelated triggered a rebuild.
- `Store::boot()`'s `saved()` hook now fires on a `STOREFRONT_PUBLISHED_FIELDS` list (the columns the public page actually renders) and on a `status` → `suspended` transition. New `Product::booted()` hooks mirror it for the other half of what a customer sees — `created` (when `show_online`), `updated` (on `name`/`selling_price`/`show_online`/`is_active`), `deleted` (when `show_online`) — using the same raw `DB::table()->update()` convention, not another `->save()`, so model events don't re-fire. Narrowed to stores with `online_store_enabled`, so a bulk product sync writes nothing for the accounts that publish no storefront.
- The stamp is always refreshed rather than only set when currently null. That matters: the confirmation callback below clears flags stamped at or before the moment the rebuild was dispatched, so a change landing mid-build has to move the timestamp past that cutoff or it would be cleared without ever shipping. Covered by a test that does exactly that.
- Deliberately **not** done: the suspension/plan-lapse half of the review's recommendation (a client-side liveness check on the published page) and the nightly unconditional rebuild. Suspension now dirties the storefront, so the page does get rebuilt — but until that rebuild lands, a suspended store's published page is still browsable with only checkout failing. Still open, unchanged, in `docs/STOREFRONT_REVIEW.md`.

#### fix: `storefront_dirty_at` was cleared when GitHub *accepted* the dispatch, so a failed build lost the pending rebuild forever (SF-P2-3)
- `repository_dispatch` returns 204 as soon as GitHub queues the event — it says nothing about whether the workflow ran, the build compiled, or the FTP sync finished. The flags were cleared on that 204, so a store could enable its online store, have the build fail, and never be retried: a URL the owner was told is live, 404ing, with nothing left in the database to say a rebuild was owed.
- Implemented the review's **option (a)**, not the watchdog fallback: `deploy-web.yml` calls back `POST /internal/storefront/rebuild-complete` (new `Api/Internal/StorefrontRebuildController`) after a successful sync, authenticated by a shared-secret `X-Storefront-Rebuild-Token` header — the inbound counterpart to `config/dumos.php`'s existing outbound GitHub token, and deliberately a dedicated endpoint with an explicit header check rather than anything ambient, per `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`'s standing rule about cookie-derived auth. The callback clears `storefront_dirty_at` only for stores dirtied at or before the dispatch, so mid-build changes survive. No schema change was needed: the dispatch timestamp lives in `SystemConfig` under `storefront_rebuild_requested_at`, avoiding a new synced column on a table the client mirrors.
- `RebuildStorefrontIfDirty` no longer clears anything on dispatch when confirmation is configured; it records the request and skips re-dispatching while one is outstanding, re-dispatching only after `STOREFRONT_REBUILD_TIMEOUT_MINUTES` (default 45) in case the workflow died. **When `STOREFRONT_REBUILD_TOKEN` is unset it keeps the old clear-on-dispatch behaviour**, so the two sides can be configured in either order without a window where every scheduled run fires another full rebuild. The workflow's callback step is `continue-on-error` — a failed callback costs a duplicate rebuild, never a lost one.
- **Needs an ops action to actually engage:** set `STOREFRONT_REBUILD_TOKEN` in the production `.env` *and* as a `STOREFRONT_REBUILD_TOKEN` repository Actions secret. Until then the old behaviour stands (documented in `.env.example` and `laravel-server/AGENTS.md`).
- New `tests/Feature/StorefrontRebuildPipelineTest.php` (15 tests) covers the whole loop with `Http::fake()`: which changes dirty a storefront and which don't, flags surviving dispatch, no double dispatch while unconfirmed, re-dispatch after the timeout, the unconfigured fallback, the callback clearing pre-dispatch flags while leaving mid-build ones, and 401/503 on a bad/missing/unconfigured token.

#### fix: no stock reservation between order placement and fulfilment — concurrent oversell (SF-P2-4)
- `priceCart()` checked raw `sum(stock_batches.quantity)` and deducted nothing (stock leaves the ledger when POS staff fulfil), so two customers ordering the last unit in the same second both got a 201.
- Implemented the review's "cheap version": availability is now stock **minus everything already committed to `pending` online orders for that store**, and `checkout()` re-checks it inside the order-creating transaction after taking a `lockForUpdate()` row lock on the store. The lock is what makes the check hold — under REPEATABLE READ two concurrent transactions would otherwise each read the other's pending order as not yet existing. Per-store storefront order volume makes a per-store lock free. A loser gets the same 422 the pre-check returns, via a dedicated `StorefrontStockUnavailableException` carrying the already-built response. Not a reservation system: a `pending` order holds its quantity until it is fulfilled or cancelled, which is the intended semantics, and a fulfilled order stops holding (its stock has actually left) — both covered by tests.
- **The refund half is flagged, not implemented.** `PaymentService` exposes `initializeTransaction`/`verifyTransaction` only — no refund call — and fabricating one against an unverified provider API would be worse than none. So cancelling an order whose `payment_status` is already `paid` now logs a `Cancelled online order requires a refund` warning with the order id, reference and amount, and raises a "Refund required" notification for every user of the store, instead of silently leaving a paid order cancelled with no compensating action. **A real provider refund is still an open follow-up and must land before `SF-P1-2` makes Paystack reachable.**

#### fix: fulfilling an online order was a non-atomic two-phase write, and `markFulfilled` had no state guard (SF-P2-5, SF-P3-6)
- The client marked the order fulfilled on the server **first**, then wrote the local `sales` row and per-item stock deductions. Any failure in step 2 — a SQLite write error, the writer-tab lock, the app being killed mid-loop, one item of several throwing — left the order server-side `fulfilled` (and therefore hidden from the POS's `pending`-only actionable list) with no sale recorded and stock never deducted: revenue missing from every report, inventory permanently overstated, silently and unrecoverably through the UI.
- Inverted per the review's preferred option: `useFulfillOnlineOrderMutation` now writes the local sale **and** every item's stock deduction inside one `transaction()`, then calls the server. A server failure surfaces as a retryable error with the local books already correct and already queued in `_sync_queue`; a local failure rolls back completely and the order stays actionable.
- That retry is only safe because `markFulfilled` now rejects a transition out of anything other than `pending` with a **409**, so it can't re-fulfil an already-fulfilled order or fulfil a cancelled one.
- `markFulfilled` also no longer promotes `payment_status` to `paid` just because an order was fulfilled — defensible as "fulfilling implies money changed hands", but recorded as a payment fact with no evidence, including for a `transfer` order whose transfer was never confirmed. It now requires an explicit `payment_confirmed: true`; the POS client sends it, because "Fulfill & Deduct Stock" is pressed at the counter as the goods are handed over and the money is taken.
- Two new client tests (real in-memory sql.js, only the network mocked): a rejected server call still leaves exactly one sale and the stock deducted; a failure on the second of two items leaves **no** sale row and stock untouched. Four new server tests: the 409 guard, an unconfirmed `transfer` staying `pending`, `payment_confirmed` promoting to `paid`, and the refund flag firing only for an already-paid cancellation.
- **Vetting this fix caught a second bug the inversion itself introduced**: writing the local sale first closes the original hole, but creates a new one on the opposite side — if the local write succeeds and only the *server* call fails, the order stays `pending` server-side (so the modal still offers "Fulfill" on it), and a second click had nothing to recognize its own prior local write, so it recorded a second `sales` row and deducted stock a second time. Fixed by deriving `transaction_number` deterministically from `order.id` (`ONL-<order.id>`, no schema change) and checking for it before writing the local leg — a retry becomes a no-op on the local side and just re-attempts the server call. New test: retrying after a server failure records exactly one sale and one stock deduction, not two.

#### fix: an owner-fulfilled online order wrote `sales.store_id = NULL`, hiding its revenue from every store-scoped report (SF-P2-6)
- `online-orders-modal.tsx` passed `storeId: user?.store_id`. A store **owner**'s own `users.store_id` is deliberately always NULL (they "have" a store via `stores.user_id`), and `insert()`'s auto-scope guard was a strict `data.store_id === undefined` — so an explicit `null` skipped it and the row was written NULL-scoped. Every store-scoped read in the client is `... ${storeId ? " AND store_id = ?" : ""}`, and `getActiveStoreId()` *is* set for an owner, so a fulfilled online order's money was simply absent from daily close, revenue, COGS, BI and dashboard figures — in the owner-operated single-store case, which is the default deployment.
- Both halves fixed, as the review recommended: the modal passes `user?.store_id ?? storeProfile?.id`, and `insert()`'s guard is now `data.store_id == null`, which closes the whole class rather than this one caller. An explicitly chosen *other* store still wins, as before.
- Two new tests in `multi-store-scoping.test.ts`: an explicit `null` is auto-scoped to the active store; an explicit other store id is still honoured.

#### fix: storefront hygiene — inert `revalidate`, unpaginated order list, unbounded product grid, unvalidated slug (SF-P3-1, SF-P3-2, SF-P3-3, SF-P3-4)
- **SF-P3-1:** `fetch(..., { next: { revalidate: 60 } })` with a `// Cache for 60 seconds` comment, on a page that is a static export — nothing revalidated, and the comment read as "at most 60 seconds stale" when the truth is "frozen until the next full rebuild, potentially weeks". Both deleted; the architectural reality is documented in `web/AGENTS.md`'s storefront section instead.
- **SF-P3-2:** `OnlineOrderController::index()` returned every order the store had ever received, with `items.product` eager-loaded, and the POS modal rendered all of them — a direct violation of `.agents/AGENTS.md` §8. Now bounded to 50 newest-first with a `has_more` flag and an optional `?status=` filter (the actionable set is `pending`). The response still has the same `orders` key, so no client change was needed. Deeper history needs a real paginated view, which is a follow-up.
- **SF-P3-3 (half):** `show()` now caps at `MAX_STOREFRONT_PRODUCTS = 300`, ordered by name — well above any real online selection, so nothing existing is truncated, but an unauthenticated GET can no longer be asked to serialise an unbounded result set. The base64-logo weight and the missing product images are **not** addressed: both need a storage decision first (see the review's feature #6), and true pagination with a client-side UI is feature #4. Still open.
- **SF-P3-4:** `store_slug` format was enforced only client-side; the real write arrives through the sync engine's `stores` push, where `SyncPayloadMapper` has no rule for it and `Store::boot()`'s `saving()` hook only enforced the 6-month cooldown — so any string fitting the column became a static-export output path. That hook now slugifies (`Str::slug`, capped at 100 chars) next to the cooldown check, reverting to the previous slug if nothing usable remains, so it applies to every write path. A value that slugifies to its own previous value is treated as no change and doesn't burn the cooldown.

#### accepted: `GET /storefront-slugs` enumerates every customer running an online store (SF-P3-5)
- Resolved as the review itself framed it — "confirmed, likely acceptable… a product call, not a defect" — rather than fixed. Requiring a build token would mean the deploy workflow must send it, and a missing/rotated secret would then fail every `web/` deploy: a worse failure mode than the disclosure, introduced into the exact pipeline `SF-P0-1` was about. Individual storefronts are public by design; the index is the incremental disclosure and exists only because the static build needs it. Revisit alongside any future decision to authenticate the build pipeline generally.

#### feat: per-store SEO metadata, plus a generated sitemap and robots.txt (storefront review, feature #12)
- Neither `[store_slug]` route had `generateMetadata`, so every storefront's `<title>` was "DumosRx - NextGen Retail & Store OS" and its OpenGraph description, image and `siteName` were DumosRx's own marketing copy — pasting a store's link into WhatsApp previewed **DumosRx**, not the store.
- Both routes now have `generateMetadata`, reusing the same `getStorefrontData()` fetch (Next's per-render fetch memoization means no extra request). `web/lib/storefront-metadata.ts` builds the store page's title/description/canonical/OpenGraph/Twitter from the store's own name and location, using `title.absolute` to bypass the root layout's `"%s | DumosRx"` template; the checkout page gets `Checkout — <store>` and `noindex`. A store the API reports as unavailable gets a `noindex` "Store unavailable".
- Added `app/sitemap.ts` (marketing pages plus every live storefront, via the same slug list) and `app/robots.ts` (disallowing `/admin/` and `/dashboard/`). Both emit as real `out/sitemap.xml` / `out/robots.txt` files under `output: "export"` with `trailingSlash: true` — verified in a real build, since that combination is the part most likely to misbehave. URLs come from `lib/constants.ts`'s `WEB_APP_URL`, not a hardcoded domain. JSON-LD was not added (out of scope for this pass).

#### feat: `show_online` in the CSV/XLSX importer and exporter, plus a bulk show/hide action (storefront review, feature #7)
- `products.show_online` defaults off, the only toggle was a per-product switch in the add/edit dialog, and `product-import-export.ts` didn't handle the column at all — so a store that imported 800 products and enabled its online store got an **empty storefront** and 800 dialogs to open. This silently defeated the paid feature at the exact moment a new customer first tried it.
- Importer: new `show_online` field with header aliases for the spellings owners actually use ("Show in Online Store", "Show Online", "Sell Online", "Online Store", "Online", "Visible Online"), and a `parseBooleanValue()` accepting `Yes/No`, `true/false`, `1/0`, `on/off`, `online/offline`, `show/hidden`. An unrecognised or blank value returns `undefined` and the product keeps whatever it has — an ambiguous cell must never silently publish a product to a public page. A new product with no column stays unpublished, matching the schema default.
- Exporter: `showOnline` added to `ExportableProduct` and `EXPORT_COLUMNS` as `"Yes"`/`"No"`, the same spelling the importer reads back, so export → edit in Excel → re-import is itself the bulk workflow. The column picker dialog is driven by `EXPORT_COLUMNS` and needed no change.
- Bulk action: a new "Online" dropdown (Show online / Hide online) in the catalog toolbar beside Import/Export, following that toolbar's existing convention of acting on the currently-filtered set — "what's on screen" — and falling back to the whole store when no filter is active. Confirmed through an `AlertDialog` (never `window.confirm`, per `.agents/AGENTS.md` §9), hidden entirely unless the store has `online_store_enabled`. `setProductsShowOnline()` skips rows already in the target state so it doesn't queue a no-op sync push per product, and runs the changes in one `transaction()`. No row-checkbox selection was introduced: the products table has no such pattern, and the filtered-set convention already existed.
- `product-import-export.ts` was over the 350-line limit (`.agents/AGENTS.md` §4) before this change and these additions made it worse, so its CSV/XLSX plumbing moved to `lib/utils/spreadsheet-io.ts` (267 lines) and is re-exported from `product-import-export.ts` (271 lines) — every existing import keeps working unchanged.
- 26 new tests in `__tests__/product-import-show-online.test.ts`: header detection, the boolean parser's accepted and rejected spellings, row mapping, importer create/update/leave-alone behaviour, and `setProductsShowOnline`'s filtered/whole-store/store-scoping/no-op-skipping/sync-queueing behaviour.

#### Verification for the whole storefront pass
- `laravel-server`: **399 tests / 1162 assertions passing** (35 new; 364 before). `client`: **894 tests / 165 files passing** (30 new; 864 before), clean `npx tsc --noEmit`, no new lint errors (the one remaining warning in `product-import-export.ts` was pre-existing and is now resolved by the split above). `web`: clean `npx tsc --noEmit`, clean `eslint`, and five real static-export builds against a local stub API covering the healthy path and each new failure mode. No build artifacts left behind; `git status` clean of them in all three packages.

### fix: two mail paths depended on a queue worker the codebase documents as unconfirmed in production (P1-1)
- `AdminAlertService::send()` and `Api/Admin/MailController::send()` were the only two `Mail::to(...)->queue(...)` call sites in the whole backend, against mailables that `implement ShouldQueue` — while `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` states plainly that no queue worker has been confirmed to drain the `jobs` table on the shared host. One of the two is the sync engine's own escalation path (a one-time superadmin report after `SYNC_FAILURE_REPORT_THRESHOLD` consecutive failures), so the mechanism meant to surface *other* silent failures was itself silently failing, with no error anywhere and no return value checked. The other is the admin "broadcast email to all/specific users" feature, which returned `200 {"success": true, "message": "Emails have been queued for sending."}` on every call regardless.
- Both switched to `->send()` (synchronous), matching every other mail call site in the codebase (`RegistersAccounts`, `RecoversPasswords`, `StoreSummaryController`, `SendEndOfDaySummaries`, `SendFunnelReminders`, `AdminUserService`). Neither is a hot request path: one is a background sync-failure check, the other an admin action that already chunks 100 users at a time. `MailController`'s response message and its OpenAPI `description`/200-response summary were corrected from "Queued (not sent synchronously)"/"Emails have been queued for sending" to accurate wording.
- The mailables' `implements ShouldQueue` was deliberately left in place — harmless with `->send()`, and useful if a real worker is ever stood up. `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` now states "always `->send()`, never `->queue()`" as its own rule, and notes explicitly that the `ShouldQueue` interface's presence is not a signal that queueing is safe here.

### fix: "reset store data" scoped its deletes by the caller's own id, silently no-opping for non-owner admin staff (P2-2)
- `DashboardService::resetData()` set `$userId = $user->id` and scoped every delete (`Sale`, `ActivityLog`, `Customer`, `StockBatch`, `Product`, `Store`) by it. Tenant-owned rows live under the **store owner's** `user_id`, and `DashboardController::resetData()`'s gate admits any caller whose `role` is `admin`, `store_owner` or `super_admin` — `admin` being a real, assignable, non-owner staff role. For such a caller every delete matched zero rows while the endpoint still returned `{"status":"success","message":"All data cleared."}`: a false confirmation on a destructive, password-confirmed, explicitly "irreversible" action, which is worse than an ordinary silent no-op because the caller has no reason to re-check.
- Fixed with a private `DashboardService::tenantOwnerId($user)` mirroring `App\Http\Controllers\Concerns\ScopesToTenant::tenantOwnerId()` (the trait itself takes a `Request`, so a service class can't use it directly). `resetData()` resolves through it; the three read-only sibling methods in the same file still resolve by `$user->id` and were deliberately left alone in this pass, now documented in `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` as the same latent shape.
- New `tests/Feature/DashboardResetScopingTest.php`: a staff `admin`-role caller's reset actually deletes the owner's products and customers, an owner's own reset still works, and neither touches another tenant's rows. Verified the two staff-caller tests fail against the pre-fix code before confirming they pass against the fix. Full Laravel suite green (364 passed, up from 359 — includes the sync double-scaling regression test added below).

### fix: purchase-order receiving was all-or-nothing, permanently forfeiting a short delivery's balance (P2-3, P3-4)
- The receive form accepted any quantity per line (floored at 0, no upper bound) and `receivePurchaseOrder()` then unconditionally flipped the *entire* PO to `received`; its own guard (`status === "received"`) blocked it from ever running again. There was no partial status anywhere in the schema and no "receive remainder" or re-open action in the codebase, so a short delivery — routine in real procurement — was recorded as fully received the moment any quantity was submitted, and the undelivered balance had no path back into the system.
- **Schema, both sides in the same change** (per the recurring "synced column added only on one side" gotcha): `purchase_order_items.quantity_received` added to `client/lib/db/schema.ts` and to `SYNC_COLUMN_MIGRATIONS` in `client/lib/db/schema-migrations.ts`, with the matching Laravel migration `2026_09_26_000001_add_quantity_received_to_purchase_order_items.php` following the existing idempotent `Schema::hasColumn(...)` guard pattern.
- **Sync mapping**: the client tracks `quantity_received` in bulk units (same unit as `bulk_quantity`), the server in base units, because `SyncController::normalizePushPayload()` now scales it by `units_per_bulk` exactly as it already did for `quantity_ordered` — preserving the "received <= ordered" invariant server-side. That branch only fires when `bulk_quantity` and `units_per_bulk` are both present, and a sync-queue `UPDATE` payload carries changed fields only, so `receivePurchaseOrder()` deliberately re-sends both unchanged alongside `quantity_received`. **Vetting this fix caught a real double-scaling bug**: an earlier version of this change also added a `quantity_received *= units_per_bulk` branch to `Services/Web/SyncPayloadMapper::map()`, which runs *before* `normalizePushPayload()` on the same payload — so a value already scaled once by the mapper got scaled a second time by `normalizePushPayload()` (units_per_bulk applied squared, e.g. 6 bulk units at units_per_bulk=5 landing as 150 instead of 30). Removed the mapper's branch entirely, since scaling belongs in the one place that also unsets `bulk_quantity`/`units_per_bulk` from the payload. Added `test_push_sync_scales_purchase_order_item_quantity_received_by_units_per_bulk_exactly_once` to `SyncEndpointTest.php`, verified to fail against the double-scaling code and pass against the fix. `purchase_orders.status` is already `VARCHAR` (since `2026_07_22_221505_alter_purchase_orders_status_enum.php`), so the new status value needed no server-side enum change — the MySQL-`ENUM`-for-a-client-controlled-string footgun documented in `laravel-server/AGENTS.md` was already closed here.
- **Business logic**: `receivePurchaseOrder()` clamps each line to its outstanding balance (ordered minus already-received), skips zero lines, accumulates `quantity_received`, and only sets `received` when every line is complete — otherwise `partially_received`, which stays receivable. An omitted line defaults to its outstanding balance rather than the full ordered quantity. A submit with every line at 0 against a PO with nothing received is a deliberate no-op rather than a bogus partial. `createAndReceivePurchaseOrder()` (Immediate Purchase) writes `quantity_received = bulk_quantity` up front, so those rows are complete by construction.
- **UI**: new `outstandingBulkQuantity()`/`clampReceivedQuantity()` helpers in `po-line-item-math.ts` (single source of truth, alongside the existing cost-math helpers); both receiving surfaces — `ReceiveLedgerTable` (tablet+) and `ReceiveItemCard` (phones) — prefill and clamp to the outstanding balance and show what's already been received, closing P3-4's unbounded input. New amber "Partially Received" badge (`purchase-order-table.tsx`), a "Partial" filter chip (`purchase-order-status-filter.tsx`), a half-state on the details panel's Received timeline step with a "Receive Balance" action and per-line received counts (`purchase-order-details.tsx`), the dashboard procurement dialog's status style + an underscore fix so it renders "Partially received" not "Partially_received" (the same raw-underscore display bug class as the `transfer_out` fix on 2026-09-25), the `missing-expiry` filter widened to include partially-received orders, and a distinct success toast for a partial receipt.
- "Edit Order" stays gated to `pending`/`sent` deliberately: `updatePurchaseOrder()` soft-deletes and re-inserts every line, which would discard `quantity_received`. Documented in `client/AGENTS.md` alongside the rest of the above.
- **Three product/UX questions deliberately left for the owner's judgement**, none of which block the data-integrity fix above: (1) **over-delivery** — a vendor shipping *more* than ordered is currently clamped down to the outstanding balance and the excess is silently dropped, rather than being accepted with a variance flag; (2) **no way to close out an abandoned balance** — a PO whose remainder will never arrive stays `partially_received` forever, with no "cancel remainder"/"close short" action (the status enum has no `cancelled` value in the client either); (3) **correcting a mistyped received quantity** still isn't possible — the input is now clamped so it can't exceed what was ordered, but an *under*-count that was already confirmed can't be revised down, only topped up. P3-4's suggested "confirm when the entered quantity differs from ordered" dialog was also not added — the panel now shows ordered/already-received/outstanding inline and prefills the outstanding balance, which was judged the better affordance than an extra modal on what is a legitimate, routine action.
- Six new regression tests in `__tests__/procurement.test.ts` (against a real in-memory sql.js database, matching that file's existing approach): short receipt leaves `partially_received` with only the delivered quantity in stock and no `received_at`; a follow-up receipt closes the balance and only then flips to `received`; an over-large submitted quantity is capped instead of overbooking stock; an omitted line defaults to outstanding; an already-received PO is a no-op; an all-zero submit leaves the order untouched. Full client suite green (864 passed), clean `tsc --noEmit`, no new lint errors.

### fix: `sw.js`'s catch-all cache branch could cache an HTML response under a JS/WASM asset's key (P3-3)
- Unlike the `mode: "navigate"` path (which got an explicit `isHtmlResponse()` guard on 2026-09-23 after a real shipped bug where an RSC text payload was served as a page), the stale-while-revalidate branch for every other same-origin GET — JS/CSS chunks, the sql.js WASM binary, manifest, icons, Next's `.txt` RSC payloads — wrote to cache on a bare `response.ok`. A captive portal or misconfigured proxy answering a chunk/WASM request with its own login page (still `200 OK`) would poison that entry under the real asset's cache key and keep being served from cache even once back online, until eviction or a `CACHE_VERSION` bump.
- Extended the same guard to that branch's cache-write as an inverse check: `expectsNonHtml` tests the request pathname's extension (`js|mjs|css|wasm|json|txt|webmanifest|` image/font types) and HTML is refused only for those requests, so a legitimately-HTML response on some other same-origin GET still caches as before. `CACHE_VERSION` bumped to `v4`, per that file's own documented rule for a caching-*strategy* change — which also makes `activate()`'s prune drop any already-poisoned entry on upgrade.
- `sw.js` still has no test coverage of any kind (unchanged, and now the stated open item in `client/AGENTS.md` in place of this finding); verified with `node --check` and the full client suite.

### fix: `getSuppliers()`'s debt/order-stats aggregation had no explicit store scoping (P3-6)
- The `total_debt` LEFT JOIN and the `po_stats` subquery in `client/lib/db/procurement.ts` joined `purchase_orders` to `suppliers` purely on `supplier_id`, unlike every other query in that file, which scopes explicitly. Harmless today only because supplier/PO ids are globally-unique per-store UUIDs — an implicit, unenforced invariant that a future cross-store supplier-sharing feature (a pattern this app already supports for other entities) would silently break, producing a supplier card whose debt and order stats came from another store's orders.
- Added the same `store_id` predicate the rest of the file uses to both, defensively. A no-op today by construction, which the existing `getSuppliers` tests confirm (unchanged results).

## 2026-09-25

### fix: product catalog's batch panel labeled every batch "Sell first (FEFO)"
- `product-batch-history.tsx` hardcoded the "Sell first (FEFO)" suffix on every batch card, regardless of expiry — not just unhelpful, actively misleading (naming several batches "first" at once).
- Now computes which batch the real FEFO sale-deduction path (`SaleController::store`, `inventory.ts`'s `recordSaleItemStock`) would actually dispense from first: active, in-stock, not-yet-expired batches, earliest expiry first with a null expiry sorted last (unknown expiry is never "soonest"), tied by received-first. Only that one batch gets the label.
- New tests in `product-batch-history-fefo-label.test.tsx`: earliest-expiry batch wins over a later one; a null-expiry batch is never picked over a dated one; an inactive/depleted/already-expired batch is skipped even if its raw expiry date is earliest.

### fix: three user-reported display bugs (session name, movement type, duplicate receipt date)
- **Sessions & Devices showed "auth_token" as a device name:** `RegistersAccounts.php`'s registration flow hardcoded `$user->createToken('auth_token')`, unlike `login()`'s `createToken($request->device_name)`. Now accepts an optional `device_name` (falling back to `'Client App'`, matching what the client already sends on login) — new tests in `PinHashingTest.php`. Also added a client-side fallback in `sessions-list.tsx`'s `parseDeviceName()` so an already-existing session with that literal name self-heals immediately without needing the token recreated.
- **"Stock transfer_out" shown raw** in the dashboard's Recent Activity feed and the movement detail modal: `use-dashboard-overview.ts` interpolated `activity.movement_type` directly into the feed message, and `stock-movement-details-dialog.tsx` rendered `movement.movement_type` directly (CSS `capitalize` alone doesn't fix an underscored value - it only uppercases the first letter, giving "Transfer_out"). Both now go through `stock-movement-utils.tsx`'s existing `getTypeLabel()`, which already had the underscore-to-space mapping and was already used elsewhere in the app.
- **Receipt printed the date twice:** `receipt-view.tsx` showed `Date: <full datetime>` near the top and a second, redundant `Date: <date only>` right above "Total paid" near the bottom, adding a line with no new information. Removed the second one.
- Full test suites green: 352 Laravel (up from 350), 829 client (unchanged).

### fix: service worker registered (and 404'd) on every `npm run dev` session
- `pwa-registrar.tsx` called `navigator.serviceWorker.register("/sw.js")` unconditionally, including in local dev - `sw.js`'s `install()` then fetches `precache-manifest.json`, a build artifact only `npm run build`'s postbuild script ever writes. Every dev session therefore hit a guaranteed 404, logged both a caught error and (`install()` deliberately re-throws so a broken precache never activates) an uncaught promise rejection, on every load.
- Fixed by skipping registration entirely when `isDevelopment` (`lib/constants.ts`). A service worker already registered in a browser from before this fix persists independently of the page's own JS and needs a manual one-time unregister (DevTools -> Application -> Service Workers, or clear site data) to fully quiet down.

### fix: memoize the POS product grid's derived data and card component (Performance & Scalability finding)
- `pos-product-list.tsx` rebuilt `cartQuantityMap`, three `Set`s, and the grouped/sorted product arrays as new references on every render, and `POSProductCard` wasn't memoized — every visible card re-rendered on any cart mutation regardless of catalog size (effectively O(catalog size) work per tap).
- Wrapped the grouping/sorting work (keyed on `filteredProducts`/`suggestions`/`recentlySoldIds`/`commonlySoldIds`) and `cartQuantityMap` (keyed on `cart`) in `useMemo`, and wrapped `POSProductCard` in `React.memo`. Deliberately did NOT touch `use-pos-cart.ts`'s `addToCart` to make it referentially stable too (which would be needed for `React.memo` to skip re-renders on the exact cart-tap case this finding calls out) — its `updateQuantity` path re-derives the authoritative stock figure from live `products` state rather than trusting the tapped card's own (possibly stale, e.g. after a multi-device stock sync) prop, and collapsing that into a pure `useCallback([])` would have meant trusting the stale value instead. The `useMemo` fix still closes the actual O(catalog) recomputation cost described in the finding; `React.memo` remains a no-op until/unless `addToCart`'s stability is addressed as its own scoped change.
- New test: `__tests__/pos-product-list-memoization.test.tsx` (grouping/cart-badge correctness survives rerenders with different props; not a render-count test, which would be brittle).

### fix: `POST /app/sales` now accepts discount/tax fields (L1)
- Flagged as latent/unreferenced — no client code calls this REST endpoint — but the gap (no way to specify a discount or tax at all) was real, so closed it now rather than leave it for whoever wires the endpoint up later without noticing.
- `SaleController::store` now accepts optional `discount_type` (`fixed`/`percentage`), `discount_amount`, and `tax_amount`, mirroring the shape the client's own offline POS checkout (`use-pos-payment.ts`) already writes into the local `sales` schema. A percentage discount over 100 is rejected (422); a flat discount larger than the subtotal is clamped to the subtotal rather than going negative.
- New tests in `SaleControllerTest`: fixed discount + tax, percentage discount, over-100 percentage rejection, flat-discount-exceeds-subtotal clamping. Full Laravel suite green (350 passed, up from 346).

### fix: Laravel 11→12 and `client/` Next.js 15→16 upgrades (M1, M2)
- Both were flagged as needing their own multi-day/high-risk project. In practice, everything else in the stack (PHP 8.2, Node 20, React 19, Tailwind 4, Sentry) had already moved ahead of the two laggards, so both upgrades resolved cleanly with no dependency conflicts.
- **Laravel:** `composer require laravel/framework:^12.0` resolved to `v12.69.2` with one new package (`symfony/polyfill-php84`) and zero conflicts — `sanctum`, `l5-swagger`, `sentry-laravel` already tolerated 12. `composer audit` now reports zero advisories (was 3). Full suite green (346 passed) with no code changes needed — the app was already on the minimal `bootstrap/app.php` skeleton.
- **Next.js:** bumped `client/` to `next@16.3.6` (matching `web/`, already on 16). Two real breaking changes, both fixed:
  - `next lint` was removed in Next 16. Replaced the `lint` script with plain `eslint`, and rewrote `eslint.config.mjs` to use `eslint-config-next`'s direct flat-config exports (`eslint-config-next/core-web-vitals` + `/typescript`) instead of the `FlatCompat` bridge that v15's legacy-shaped exports needed — matching the pattern `web/eslint.config.mjs` already used. This also turned on `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`'s new v6 "React Compiler" rules (`purity`, `refs`, `static-components`, `immutability`, `preserve-manual-memoization`, `set-state-in-effect`) as hard errors; downgraded all six to `warn` (this codebase predates React Compiler assumptions), matching `web/`'s existing precedent for `set-state-in-effect`. Also disabled `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` for `e2e/**` — its new name-based heuristic misreads Playwright's `({page}, use) => ...` fixture parameter as React's `use()` hook.
  - Turbopack is now the default builder for `next build`, not just `next dev`, and doesn't run the `webpack()` config function at all — so `next.config.mjs`'s existing `fs`/`path`/`crypto` fallback (there so sql.js's UMD bundle, which `require()`s them behind an always-false-in-browser `isNode` check, doesn't fail bundling) stopped applying, breaking the build (`Module not found: Can't resolve 'fs'`). Added the Turbopack equivalent (`turbopack.resolveAlias`, new `lib/polyfills/browser-empty.js` stub) — scoped to the `browser` condition specifically, since a first unscoped attempt also broke `@sentry/nextjs`'s legitimate server-side use of `path.dirname`.
  - Also removed `next.config.mjs`'s now-unrecognized top-level `eslint` key (silently ignored since `next build` no longer runs its own lint step).
- Verified beyond `tsc`/vitest (826 passed, unchanged): a real `npm run build` static export (47 routes) served locally and opened in an actual browser — confirmed `sql.js`/WASM initializes and runs a live query (`window.diagnoseLegacySchema()`) with zero console errors, not just that the build completes.

### fix: address gaps an ultrareview of the single-writer-tab/sync branch caught
- Cloud ultrareview of the `dev` branch (single-writer-tab work) found six real issues, all fixed:
- **Read-only tab's auto-sync threw instead of failing cleanly:** `sync-engine/index.ts`'s `sync()` called `push()`/`pullChanges()` unconditionally, which throw mid-sync via `core.ts`'s `assertWritable()` as soon as a read-only tab has anything to push/pull, surfacing as a misleading "Sync failed. Check your connection." Gated `sync()` on `isWriterTab()` (mirroring the impersonation check already there), and `sync-indicator.tsx`'s manual button + auto-sync daemon now skip entirely while `isReadOnlyTab` is true instead of flashing an error state every interval.
- **Broken-formula import produced literal "[object Object]":** `product-import-export.ts`'s `cellPlainValue()` had no branch for ExcelJS's error-cell shape (`{error: '#REF!'}`, or nested in a formula's `result`), so it fell through to `String(value)`. Added an `error` check and made the `result` branch recurse through `cellPlainValue()` so a nested error hits it too.
- **Release workflow's root-file deploy could regress the auto-updater feed:** splitting the release workflow's concurrency into a per-tag `deploy-ftp` group and a separate `deploy-ftp-root` group dropped the old FIFO guarantee — a slower-building older tag could reach the shared root-files queue after a faster newer tag and overwrite `updater.json`/`index.php` with the older version. Added a version-compare guard step in `deploy-ftp-root` that fetches the currently-live `updater.json` and skips the deploy outright if the live version is already >= this tag's, so ordering no longer matters.
- **Legacy-row store_id claim never reached the server for deletes:** `SyncController::push()`'s `DELETE` branch never read `$payload`, so `base-helpers.ts`'s `softDelete()` claim (added in the prior day's fix, for the `UPDATE` path) still never landed server-side for a delete. Now applies the payload's `store_id` to the target model (when the column is empty) before authorization and deletion *(superseded 2026-10-23 by A-298: applying it before authorization let the claim authorize itself; it is now applied after, and only when it names one of the caller's stores)*, saving first if it was actually claimed.
- **Promotion-failure left the read-only banner permanently stuck:** `DatabaseProvider.tsx`'s `handleUseThisWindow()` leaves `handoffState` at `"requesting"` pending an async promotion; if that promotion then failed (rehydrate-from-IndexedDB error), nothing ever reset it, since only the success path (`onWriterTabChange(isWriter=true)`) did. `onPromotionFailed`'s handler now also resets `handoffState` to `"offer-force"` so the banner offers a usable next action.
- **Stolen writer lock never notified the old holder:** `tab-lock.ts`'s `stealWriterLock()` steals the Web Lock directly via `{steal: true}`, which the Web Locks spec never signals to the previous holder — a merely-slow (not dead) old writer tab kept believing it was still the writer and kept saving over IndexedDB. Added a `steal-notice` broadcast over the existing handoff `BroadcastChannel`, sent right after a successful steal; every tab (including one busy mid-write) flips itself to read-only as soon as it processes the message.
- Full client vitest suite green (826 passed, up from 816 — 10 pre-existing `sync-indicator` tests needed a `@/lib/db/DatabaseProvider` mock added after `sync-indicator.tsx` started reading `isReadOnlyTab` from it), clean `tsc --noEmit`, full Laravel suite green (346 passed, unchanged).

### fix: address gaps an independent Opus review caught in this same date's H1/C1/architecture-test fixes
- **Commits:** `73e63856` (client), `8a9a5837` (laravel-server)
- A dispatched Opus review of the xlsx→exceljs migration (H1), the single-writer-tab fix (C1), and the `ScopesToTenant` architecture test found three real gaps, all fixed and covered by a regression test that reproduces each before the fix and passes after:
- **H1 remainder — silent row truncation on any blank-row sheet:** `product-import-export.ts`'s `worksheetToSpreadsheet()` used `worksheet.actualRowCount` (the count of *non-empty* rows) as the loop's upper bound instead of `worksheet.rowCount` (the real last row number). Any blank row partway through a sheet — routine in real QuickBooks/Moniebook exports as a section separator — made the loop stop short and silently drop every row after it. Live import data loss; fixed to use `rowCount` (the existing blank-row skip already handles empties correctly).
- **C1 remainder, gap 1 — promotion on a failed rehydrate:** `tab-lock.ts`'s promoted-tab path called `setWriterTab(true)` unconditionally after `onPromoted()`, even if rehydrating from IndexedDB had failed — reopening the exact C1 data-loss hazard on the rehydrate-failure path (a tab writing over the real snapshot with a stale copy it never actually caught up to). `onPromoted()` now returns whether rehydration actually succeeded; a `false`/rejected result keeps the tab read-only and releases the lock rather than promoting on stale data, firing a new `onPromotionFailed()` listener so the UI can tell the user to reload.
- **C1 remainder, gap 2 — migration writes could land before the writer election:** `core.ts` ran schema migrations (including `clearLegacyTransactionsOnce`, a live one-time destructive cleanup for any device that hasn't crossed its flag yet) *before* electing this tab's writer/read-only role, so a tab about to lose the election could still persist that cleanup to the shared IndexedDB snapshot first. Fixed by electing the writer before running migrations, and gating the migration's persistence callback on `isWriterTab()` — a read-only tab still migrates its own in-memory copy (so its own reads see the current schema) but never writes that back to shared storage.
- **Architecture-test remainder — two controller directories invisible to the guard:** `TenantScopingArchitectureTest`'s glob covered `Api/*`, `Api/App/*`, `Api/Web/*` but missed `Api/Admin/*` (9 controllers) and `Api/Public/*` (1), both of which also reference tenant-owned models. Neither hid a real bug (all three now-flagged files have legitimate reasons — `StorefrontController` is public/unauthenticated with no caller identity to scope by; `AdminStoreController`'s `ActivityLog::create()` logs the super_admin's own action, not a tenant query; `AdminPlatformController`'s cross-tenant listing is deliberately platform-wide and super_admin-gated) but they were invisible rather than explicitly allow-listed, defeating the test's own premise. Added both directories to the glob and allow-listed the three files with verified justifications; also documented the scan's known structural blind spots (route-model binding, relationship traversal, service-mediated queries).
- New tests: an xlsx blank-row-truncation regression, an `onPromoted`-failure regression, a migration-persistence-gating regression, and confirmed (by temporarily removing `StorefrontController`'s allow-list entry) that the expanded glob genuinely works. Full client suite green (818 passed, up from 815); full Laravel suite green (346 passed, unchanged).

### fix: add ScopesToTenant architecture test; fixes the gap it immediately caught
- **Commit:** `e7e7c019`
- The Recommended Engineering Improvements section called for a backstop against this review's recurring failure shape — a tenant-scoping fix applied to one controller but not mirrored onto a structurally identical sibling.
